## v1.13.44 (local, not tagged or pushed), 2026-11-28 (Inch Pincher parts: methanol, Porsche transaxle, weight to 1200 lb; neutral drag start; outlier input check)

* **Why.** Wim (t49u): add the parts the Inch Pincher is missing as sourced parts through the one generic model, stage the drag start in neutral, and give the >10 % outliers a last check of their inputs and generic physical causes. 87c14e3 (v1.13.43) is still unpushed; this is a second local commit on top.
* **Generic fuel path (`js/parts.js`).** A fuel part may carry `tm.fuel` = { lhv, afr_st, afr, h_fg, M, x_ev, egt }. `Parts.fuelOf()` turns it into (a) heat per kg of trapped air, LHV/AFR_st × the fuel-air-cycle curve at the running mixture, relative to the fitted petrol; (b) trapped-air density from the extra charge cooling and the air displaced by the fuel vapour, against petrol's own (x_ev 0.3, M 100, ESTIMATES); (c) exhaust mass 1 + 1/AFR and the equivalence ratio for the exhaust model. The sound overrides scale the kernel's `q_comb` by the charge energy and its EGT law (`t_prim0`, `t_prima`) by `egt`. Without `tm.fuel` (every petrol) nothing changes. Octane stays the existing soft knock rule.
* **New parts.** `fuel.methanol` (LHV 19.9 MJ/kg, AFR_st 6.45, h_fg 1.17 MJ/kg, M 32, RON 108.7 sourced; running AFR 4.8, evaporated share 0.12 from methanol's vapour pressure and EGT 0.945 ESTIMATES; net ≈ +3.5 % heat per kg of air × ≈ +6 % air = +9.7 %); `fuel.avgas` (racing / aviation petrol, octane only); `gear.p741` Porsche 356B/C 741 transaxle 3.091 / 1.765 / 1.130 / 0.815, 4.428 (sourced); `clutch.hauserman` (documented; 270 N·m ESTIMATE); `rod.empibox` (documented; same rod as the 311 in the model, purpose part); `cam.erson210` (Sig Erson VW-210 card: 322°, 0.311 in at the cam, LSA 110; IVC 74° ESTIMATE); `heads.vwdp_ip` (ported dual-port, 1.5 / 1.35 in valves as the documented 1966 engine); new weight slots `wwheel` (BRM wheels −8.2 kg, sourced 9 vs 13.5 lb) and `wtank` (drag fuel cell −32.5 kg ESTIMATE). `cyl.88hc` is now labelled Forgedtrue (sourced).
* **Kit `kit.ip1967`.** The documented 1967 H/Gas combination: Forgedtrue 12:1, EMPI boxed rods, Sig Erson cam on stock rockers, ported dual-port heads, 48 IDA, Vertex, EMPI header, racing petrol (a gas class: methanol belonged to the blown Shorrock engine), Porsche transaxle 4.428, Hauserman clutch, and the 1967 lightening plus BRM wheels, fuel cell, rear seat, spare and bumpers: −189.2 kg, 541 kg = 1192 lb without the driver.
* **Neutral drag start.** Manual cars now stage in N (ready = engine running). The revs are built in N (held at the launch rpm when one is set), shift up (key →, the ▲ button on iPhone / Tesla touch) into 1st at the green: the clutch stays down through the gear change and is dumped as the gear goes in. The drag card ("Stage in") and key N switch back to the 1st-gear start. Physics: `inp.launch.n`; without it nothing runs, so `test/drag_test.js` (its own harness) is bit-identical.
* **Outlier check.** See `proposals/SR26_v11344_report.md`.
* **Stock: no deltas.** Drag 23.219 s, `?course=mesa` 2:51.93 (61.75 / 61.26 / 28.24 / 20.67), 111/111 audio hashes, worklet md5 3573eb1e93f9e67a1616da2296b37d72. drag, road, physics, stage, traction, arcade, rivals_map, wheel_angle, aids and collide test outputs identical to v1.13.43 (collide differs only in its ms-per-frame timing line).
* **Gates.** Parts gate PASS; kit effect check PASS (29); effect check (556 parts): PASS 443, NOEFF_PASS 69, VEHICLE_OK 37, NO_HOST 22, with the same 7 known front-tyre / vw041 sound-path items as v1.13.43; none of the 547 existing parts changed in PS, torque, CR, ET or verdict; headless picker PASS; ES2017 syntax OK; old models 24/24 bit-identical; neutral-start browser smoke test (keyboard, iPhone touch, Tesla-size touch) PASS with no console errors.
* **Results.** Inch Pincher kit 144.8 PS (claim 165 hp = 167 PS, −13 %), 541 kg + 75 kg driver; best scripted run 13.92 s @ 97.9 mph at a 5500 rpm launch vs the documented 12.92 @ 102 (staging in N gives the same ET; it costs only the 0.14 s gear change in the reaction). Methanol (not documented for this engine): 158.2 PS, 13.54 @ 101.2. Power rms over the A/B references 13.8 % → 14.6 % because two reference inputs were corrected (CB 2110 / CB 1835 sheets say 044 CNC); the model itself is unchanged for every existing part.

## v1.13.43 (local, not tagged or pushed), 2026-11-28 (SR-26 power-model refit through parts only; CR from parts; weight parts; race clutch; launch rpm)

* **Why.** Wim (t46u–t48u): build all six SR-26 fixes, with one engine power model that changes only through the selected parts and their physical parameters (no per-kit or per-car code, no player fudge settings); CR follows from the parts; weight reduction as individual parts; re-check every kit and part.
* **Rule kept.** There is still one `torqueAt` model. Every change below is either a generic model term that reads a part's physical parameter, or part data. No kit, car or id is named in `js/parts.js`.
* **Fix 1, anchoring cap.** `anchAt()` (new): above the last stock table point, where the stock model has breathed out (torque ≤ 1 N·m) or the build/stock ratio would pass the 5× clamp, the build carries on from the anchored value along its own model, instead of being cut by the clamp. Elsewhere it uses the same table × ratio as before. Only `kit.t4` moves through this (rev-out above 5400 rpm).
* **Fix 2, intake tuning from the runner length.** The intake ram term now uses the physical runner length of each carb or manifold (same law for every carb). Weber IDF on Type 1 manifolds: 0.259 m = manifold 100 mm (ESTIMATE) + IDF body 102.7 mm (Pierce Manifolds 18990.030) + 56 mm air horn (Weber Performance AU). Other down-draft / IDA-pattern / throttle-body individual runners: 0.25 m (ESTIMATE). It was 0.12 m (manifold only). The IDF 44 default venturi is now 36 mm (Weber factory calibration; CB's 1915 sheet used 32 mm). A separate per-runner tuning gain was tried and fitted to zero, so it is not in the model.
* **Fix 3, CR from parts.** The clearance volume now includes the cylinder base shims (bore area × thickness, `tm.shim`) and the piston dome (`tm.dome`). New parts: `spacer.s010/s020/s030/s040/s060` (.010–.060 in shims, CB shim kit sizes) and `cyl.88hc` (88 mm high-dome forged pistons). The dome is DERIVED from the documented 12:1 of the Inch Pincher with VW dual-port heads on 88 × 78 mm (17.8 cc), flagged 'd'. The Inch Pincher kit now uses them: CR 8.78 → 12.0. Stock CR = 6.6000 and all factory CRs are unchanged. The CB reference builds use shims to reach their sheet CRs (2110 .040 in → 8.88, 2276 .060 → 8.96, 2332 .060 → 9.15).
* **Fix 4, valve-flow ceiling.** VE × (Φ/Φ₀)^0.30, with Φ = c_d(lift) × (inlet valve / bore)² against the stock engine's Φ₀. The same term applies to every build: bigger valves and more lift raise it, and a bigger bore on the same valves lowers it. The exponent 0.30 was fitted on the period and independent dyno set (George Brown's independent 2110 included, not only CB's sheets). The stock car has Φ = Φ₀ exactly, so vef = 1.
* **Fix 5, Judson blower.** The R&T / flat4ever data (9.7 mkg @ 2200, 48 PS @ 4000, ~5 psi at peak revs) is now matched through the blower's physical parameters: displacement 0.6 → 0.8 L/rev and leakage 250 → 1000 rpm, both fitted ('d'; they were estimates). Result: 42.8 → 45.8 PS (−5 % vs 48), 6.0 psi at 4000.
* **Fix 6, parts.**
  * 14 weight-reduction slots, each with a factory part and a reduction part with a kerb-mass delta (kg), summed in `Parts.compose` and added to the vehicle mass in `Parts.vehicle` (one generic path, no body preset). The parts: rear seat out −12, light buckets −16, glass hood −6, glass lid −2.5, glass fenders −10, running boards off −8, glass doors −18, Lexan side/rear −7 or Plexiglas all round −12, stripped interior −18, bumpers off −8, small battery −10, aluminium floor −12, straight axle −26, spare and tools out −18 kg. Sourced weights: forum part weights (lid 4, bumper 4, fenders 5 / 3, beam 61, pan 88 kg) and the EMPI steel hood (21 lb). The rest are estimates (prov 'e'). Only mass is modelled, not CG or stiffness.
  * Kit `kit.ip1967` sets the documented 1967 combination: −110.5 kg, 805 → 695 kg with driver. The quoted 1200 lb car would be lighter still; that gap is not filled by invented parts.
  * `clutch.kennedy3`: Kennedy Stage 3, 2600 lb, up to 260 lb-ft = 353 N·m (sourced).
  * **Launch rpm** (drag strip, manual cars, key L or the result card): while staged, the clutch is down and the revs are held at the chosen rpm; flooring the gas after the green dumps the clutch (~0.1 s). This is driver technique, not an engine change: the default is off, so the stock run is unchanged.
* **Stock: no deltas.** Drag 23.219 s, `?course=mesa` 2:51.93 (61.75 / 61.26 / 28.24 / 20.67), 111/111 audio hashes, worklet md5 3573eb1e93f9e67a1616da2296b37d72. drag, road, physics, stage, traction, arcade, rivals_map, wheel_angle, aids and collide test outputs are identical to v1.13.42 (collide differs only in its ms-per-frame timing).
* **Gates.** Parts gate PASS; kit effect check PASS (29); effect check (547 parts): PASS 439, NOEFF_PASS 68, VEHICLE_OK 33, with the same 7 known front-tyre / vw041 sound-path items as v1.13.42; headless picker PASS; ES2017 syntax OK; old models 24/24 bit-identical.
* **Correlation and kit tables.** See `proposals/SR26_v11343_report.md`. Power rms over the grade A/B references: 16.7 % → 13.8 %. The remaining gaps are listed there honestly: modern CB / independent big-bore builds are still −8 … −24 %, Denzel / Denzel S / Okrasa TS are over by +12 / +21 / +17 %, the Shorrock claims (C) are missed by −25 … −36 %, and the Inch Pincher is 146.8 PS vs 165 claimed (−12 %).

## v1.13.42 (local, not tagged or pushed), 2026-11-28 (no rev limiter: revs are bounded by the torque curve, not by a cut)

* **Why.** Wim (t42u): "Fix the rpm limit. There should be none." The v1.13.41 kits showed it: a 4600 rpm ignition cut that no part raised capped the CB Builder's Choice 1180 at 81.7 PS (claimed 105 hp) and the Inch Pincher 1967 at 93.3 PS (claimed 165 hp), and the picker's dyno line stopped at the same rpm.
* **Where rpm was capped (all of it found, generic, no per-car code).**
  1. `js/physics.js` ignition cut with 150 rpm hysteresis at `limiterRpm` (the "limiter"): **removed**. The HUD tag "REV LIMIT" and the `limiterCut` state went with it.
  2. `js/physics.js` downshift refusal ("would over-rev", above limiter + 250): **removed**. Only the numeric guard below can refuse a downshift.
  3. `js/physics.js` rev-match blip target capped at limiter - 100: **now the gear's real matched speed** (same numeric guard).
  4. `js/physics.js` torque tables ended at their last point and held that torque flat forever (hidden behind the cut). Past the last point the torque now **runs off linearly to zero**, along the table's own last slope but never more gently than "zero within a quarter of the last table rpm" (ESTIMATE; the shipped tables already end in the game's over-rev run-off, this continues it). Below the last point the arithmetic is unchanged (bit-identical). ICE only; the EV motor table is not touched.
  5. `js/parts.js` `vehicle()`: a non-stock build's torque table stopped at limiter + 400. It now carries on along the **build's own parts model** (intake, exhaust, friction: `torqueAt`) from the last anchored point, to where the model torque reaches zero. `RPM_MODEL_MAX` = 16000 rpm only guards the table length. A build whose engine model equals the stock one (tyres, brakes, no-effect parts) keeps the shipped table and the same run-off as the stock car, so a no-effect part still changes nothing (the per-part effect check gives the same verdict for every part as on v1.13.41: PASS 433, NOEFF_PASS 68, and the same seven sound-only misses that v1.13.41 already had).
  6. `js/parts.js` `summary()` (the picker's dyno line and the headline PS / torque): skipped every point above the limiter (`if (n > lim) continue`). It now covers the whole curve.
  7. `js/main.js` / `js/engine-specs.js` / picker: the "limiter" wording is now "red line". `limiterRpm` stays as data with a new meaning: the **rated / red-line rpm** (tach red mark, reference for the AUTO-shift on the road course, `road.js` plan top speed). It is no longer a cut. A build whose power peaks above its part-data red line gets 1.10 x the peak-power rpm (rounded to 100) as red line (the stock Beetle is red-lined 3800 for a 3400 rpm peak), also in the tach marks and as `car.redline` for the sound model so the kernel's rpm / redline scaling stays where it was designed. Builds that are not raised are untouched.
* **Numeric guard (not a limiter).** `RPM_GUARD` = 30000 rpm in `physics.js`: engine speed above it, or NaN, is a runaway of the integrator and is clamped. No engine gets near it: the torque has run off to zero long before and closed-throttle friction brakes the engine above that.
* **Not changed, on purpose.** `js/road.js` `vTop()` still plans the AI / checkpoint speed profile with the car's rated rpm in top gear: it is a plan estimate for the kinematic AI cars and the time allowances, not a cap on any car's physics (changing it would move the Mesa Run par times). The tach needle stops at the end of its scale (a gauge stop; the number and the HUD bar keep going). The sound worklet (`engine-worklet.js`, md5 3573eb1e93f9e67a1616da2296b37d72, untouched) does not clamp rpm anywhere that matters: its own `rev_limiter` flag is false for every car; the only clamp is the VarioCam phase map (P997), which uses min(rpm, 1.05 x redline) for the cam phase only. Rendered at 1.0 / 1.3 / 1.6 / 2.0 x redline for every car key: no NaN; louder at over-rev as a real engine is (Beetle raw peak 5.5 → 14.3 at 1.6 x).
* **No valve-float model.** The engine bay has none and none was invented: a mild engine now winds to where its parts model stops making torque (about 6200 rpm for a 1600 dual-port in neutral), where a real one would float its valves earlier. A valve-float / over-rev wear model is a possible follow-up (queue SR-17 wear and failure).
* **What did not move (checked).** Stock 30 PS drag 23.219 s / 54.755 mph / 3.121 s 60-ft; `?course=mesa` Beetle lap 2:51.93 (sectors 61.75 / 61.26 / 28.24 / 20.67); all drag_test figures for every car; stage_test, traction_test, arcade_test, rivals_map_test, wheel_angle_test output; 111 / 111 audio hashes; `vehicles.js` and every sound data table byte-identical (baseline data / vehicles diff 0); worklet md5. Stock drivers (shift 150 rpm under the red line) never reach the old cut, so removing it does not touch them.
* **What did move (honest list).** (a) GT350 and BRM top speed in the test (`physics_test`): GT350 202.8 → 226.1 km/h, BRM 307.8 → 338.0 km/h, because their top gear used to sit on the cut and now sits where drag and torque run-off balance (GT350 7266 rpm, BRM 11773 rpm against red lines 6500 / 11000). In the 4-gear top-speed run: GT350 201.4 → 226.1, BRM 351.5 → 377.8 km/h. (b) BRM wheelspin test without aids: squeal 3.4 → 3.1 s (the cut used to interrupt it), 0-100 km/h with TC unchanged 6.05 s. (c) GT350 stage-banner run 182.8 → 182.7 s. P997, Cadillac, Model X and the Beetle: unchanged in all of these.
* **Kits, old v1.13.41 → new v1.13.42** (dyno = the picker line; ET = the scripted drag driver of `test/drag_test.js`: 0.45 s reaction, shift 150 rpm under the red line, stock tyres; peak rpm in that run; free-rev = full throttle in neutral for 15 s, i.e. where the model torque reaches zero; red line = rated rpm). Where the PS and the peak did not change but the ET did, the driver now shifts later because the red line was raised for that build.

A sweep over the shift rpm (`v11342-notes/shift_sweep.cjs`) puts the best shift at 1.15 - 1.20 x the peak-power rpm (stock 3900, CB 1180 7200, Inch Pincher 6550) and the harness shift is within 0.1 s of it (stock 23.22 vs 23.12 best; CB 17.31 vs 17.21; Inch Pincher 17.16 vs 17.10). Over-revving by 30 % costs time again (CB 17.38, Inch Pincher 18.37), so a sensible shift emerges from the physics.

## v1.13.41 (local, not tagged or pushed), 2026-11-28 (Beetle kits: EMPI, Shorrock, Judson, Okrasa TSV, Scat, CB and Inch Pincher packages)

* **Why.** Queue job SR-24 (Wim: "a few more kit options"). The picker had eleven presets (Okrasa TS / TSV, Speedwell, Rajay, Schwitzer, Garrett, CB radiator, Volkswagen Sport merged, 1600 dual-port, Type 4, Rajay + carb kit). Eighteen new complete kits were added, each assembled only from parts already in the catalogue, so the sound, dyno and drag time follow through the existing generic forwards (`Parts.vehicle`, `Parts.soundOverrides`). No new parts, no per-car code.

* **Kits** (every kit is hard-rule legal when applied to the stock build; soft period / knock / cooling warnings still show). Dyno figures are the model's, not claims; the maker's claim is shown for comparison only.

  | `kit.shorrock_1600s` | EMPI Shorrock PSKV-600 | 1585 | 55.9 PS @ 4400 | 85 hp (220 bhp on methanol, Inch Pincher) | 19.04 / 68.0 / 2.67 | 18.82 / 68.2 |
  | `kit.okrasa_tsv1600` | Okrasa TSV 1600 H (Oettinger) | 1585 | 59.1 PS @ 3900 | ~58 PS @ 4200 | 18.55 / 70.2 / 2.65 | 18.31 / 70.5 |
  | `kit.okrasa_tsv1700` | Okrasa TSV 1700 H (Oettinger) | 1699 | 62.0 PS @ 3700 | ~70 PS @ 4200 | 18.38 / 71.9 / 2.74 | 17.91 / 72.2 |
  | `kit.scat2110` | Scat-style 2110 stroker | 2110 | 59.4 PS @ 3000 | none | 20.58 / 58.2 / 2.76 | 19.91 / 58.7 |
  | `kit.cb_bolt78` | CB bolt-in 78 mm stroker | 1791 | 55.3 PS @ 3400 | none | 18.93 / 68.9 / 2.68 | 18.63 / 69.1 |
  | `kit.cb1180` | CB Builder’s Choice 1180 (1776 cc) | 1775 | 81.7 PS @ 4600 | 105 hp (dual 40 IDF, 1.5 in merged exhaust) | 19.57 / 68.0 / 3.17 | 19.30 / 68.6 |
  | `kit.ip1967` | Inch Pincher drag engine 1967 | 1898 | 93.3 PS @ 4600 | 165 hp (1966, Okrasa heads) | 19.52 / 69.4 / 3.51 | 19.27 / 70.0 |

  Stock 30 PS Beetle for comparison: 23.2190 s / 54.75 mph / 3.121 60-ft on stock tyres; 23.14 s with the M&H 8.5/26.0-15 slick (`tyre.mh26x85`, v1.13.40). Same scripted driver as `test/drag_test.js` (0.45 s reaction, throttle ramp 4 / s, feather on wheelspin, shift 150 rpm under the limiter), stall-free automatic clutch from idle.

* **Sources and flags.** Each new kit carries `src` (sources), `prov` (`s` sourced, `ss` sourced-secondary, `d` derived, `e` ESTIMATE per field), `claim` (what the maker advertised, not a target), optional `year` (the package's own date when its parts are later equivalents) and, where a period price is known, `period`. The kit picker now shows the description, the claim, the sources and the estimates of the chosen kit, and keeps it selected until a slot is changed. Main sources: EMPI catalogue 98-1947 / 1948 / 1949 / 1953-B displacement table; TheSamba (John Moxon, 2009) and oldspeed.net (EMPI Shorrock packages and prices); Seume p.16, 22, 29, 97; the flat4ever Okrasa / Judson / Denzel table (Judson 48 PS); the Oettinger delivery programme as listed on felix03.jimdofree.com (TSV 1600 H 1585 cc / TSV 1700 H 1695 cc, original VW dual-port heads from 1966); cbperformance.com item 1180 (contents list, 105 hp rating, 8:1, mid-grade fuel; its Eagle Racing cam 2241 is `cam.cb2241`); aircooledvintageworks.com 2110 kit; EMPI history / Drag Strip 1966 / oldeskool.tripod.com (Inch Pincher).
  Estimates and deviations: the EMPI PSKV-601 / PSKV-600 contents are not published (derived as the 1500 engine, and 85.5 mm barrels for the 1600); TSV 1600 H / 1700 H use `carb.p11` for the Oettinger Solex 40 PDSIT and the 356 74 mm crank for the Oettinger special crank; the 1967 Inch Pincher uses the Engle W130 for the Sig Erson cam, 311 rods for the EMPI boxed rods and runs on petrol (no methanol); the Porsche transaxle of the real car is not modelled.

* **Not added (cannot be sourced or built).** Bugpack street kit (no fixed, published package found); a Gene Berg kit (only separate cranks); Okrasa TS 1200 H / TSV 1300 H on the 34 / 40 PS engines (the catalogue's Okrasa heads and TSV crank rule allow only the 36 hp case; the Oettinger list names the 34 / 40 PS variants, so the rule could be widened as a separate change); the 1966 Okrasa-head Inch Pincher 1900 (Okrasa heads are 36 hp-case only and 88 mm barrels need a 40 hp-family case, so the 1967 dual-port-head engine is built instead); the 1965 Denzel-head Inch Pincher (Denzel heads and the Solex 40P11 manifold are incompatible in the catalogue).

* **Fixed.** `kit.rajay_carb` (Rajay turbo plus the PICT-3 carb and advance kit) was blocked on the stock build because the PICT-3 needs the dual-port manifold; it now also sets the 1600 dual-port engine and 12 V.

* **Reading the ETs.** The scripted driver launches from idle with a stall-free clutch and the engine model has a 4600 rpm limiter that no part raises, so the strongest engines (CB 1180, Inch Pincher 1967, Scat 2110) are held back by low-end torque, wheelspin on stock tyres and the limiter (the model peaks at 81.7 / 93.3 PS inside the limiter against the 105 / 165 hp claimed; claims are gross, with parts not in the game). Their ETs are therefore not a measure of the engine at high rpm.

* **Tests.** New gate: every kit names real parts in the right slots and is legal on the stock build (29 kits). New `checks/v11341_kit_effect_check.mjs`: every kit changes the dyno (PS or torque or cc), the sound override keys and a rendered idle and wide-open sample versus stock, with no NaN.

* **Unchanged.** Every sound (111 audio hashes, worklet md5 `3573eb1e93f9e67a1616da2296b37d72`), the stock drag strip and the old flat Mesa Road lap (`?course=mesa`, Beetle 2:51.93). Generic default-off keys (`year`, `src`, `prov`, `claim`, `period` on a kit), no per-car code, ES2017 in shipped JS.

## v1.13.40 (local, not tagged or pushed), 2026-11-28 (Beetle tyres: period street, radial and drag options)

* **Why.** Queue job SR-14: the catalogue only had `tyre.stock` (5.60-15) and `tyre.radial` (Michelin X 165 R 15). Add period-correct tyres, including drag tyres, that change the drag launch, ET, top speed (diameter versus gearing, and the speedo reading) and road-course grip, all through generic keys. Front and rear tyres may differ. Live main was v1.13.39 (`3423fbc`).

* **Catalogue.** The factory 5.60-15 keeps the shipped physics (bit-identical stock car). The description now notes period OE brands (Continental, Firestone, Dunlop) without adding no-op brand clones. New or kept tyres, with sourced sizes and estimates flagged in `prov`:

  | Part | Size / construction | OD (in) | Radius (m) | Width | Mass (kg) | muPeak / muSlide / crr | Road | Notes / sources |
  |---|---|---:|---:|---|---:|---|---|---|
  | `tyre.stock` | 5.60-15 bias (factory) | 25.79 | 0.307 (unchanged) | 5.94 in | 7.71 (17 lb) | 0.78 / 0.62 / 0.016 (unchanged) | legal | Coker / Longstone Firestone 5.60-15 |
  | `tyre.60015` | 6.00-15 bias | 26.00 | 0.3095 | ~6.3 in (e) | 8.62 (e, ~19 lb) | 0.80 / 0.63 / 0.016 (e) | legal | Coker / BFGoodrich 6.00-15 OD |
  | `tyre.radial` | Michelin X 165 R 15 | 25.55 (d) | 0.304 (kept) | ~6.5 in (e) | 8.20 (e) | 0.85 / 0.70 / 0.012 (e, kept) | legal | Michelin X from 1949 |
  | `tyre.x155` | Michelin X 155 R 15 | 24.68 | 0.2938 | 153 mm | 8.00 | 0.85 / 0.70 / 0.012 (e) | legal | closer radial equivalent of 5.60-15; OD from CA67 155HR15 |
  | `tyre.cint165` | Pirelli Cinturato CA67 165 HR 15 | 25.55 | 0.3041 | 171 mm | 8.50 | 0.86 / 0.71 / 0.012 (e) | legal | cinturato.net / Longstone |
  | `tyre.cint155` | Pirelli Cinturato CA67 155 HR 15 | 24.68 | 0.2938 | 153 mm | 8.00 | 0.86 / 0.71 / 0.012 (e) | legal | same sources; not a documented Inch Pincher front |
  | `tyre.185ss` | 185 HR 15 radial (EMPI / Sprint Star era) | 25.98 | 0.3093 | 185 mm | 9.80 (e) | 0.88 / 0.73 / 0.013 (e) | legal | CA67 185HR15 660 mm; wide-tyre warning |
  | `tyre.castler` | Castler recap slick (early drag) | 26.00 (e) | 0.3095 (e) | 7 in (e) | 8.00 (e) | 1.00 / 0.88 / 0.018 (e) | strip | EMPI history ch. 3 (brand/use only) |
  | `tyre.mh26x6` | M&H Racemaster 26x6-15 slick | 26.00 | 0.3095 | 6 in | 6.80 (e) | 1.12 / 0.95 / 0.015 (e) | strip | ShopTalk / Glenn Ring VW size; Inch Pincher "M&H rear slicks" (EMPI ch. 4) has no size |
  | `tyre.mh26x85` | M&H 8.5/26.0-15 slick (MHR062) | 26.00 | 0.3095 | 10.5 in sect., 8.3 in tread | 8.16 (18 lb) | 1.15 / 0.98 / 0.014 (e) | strip | M&H / Interco tables; wrinkle-wall, not DOT |
  | `tyre.mhcheat` | M&H 8.5/26.0-15 cheater (MHD-01) | 26.00 | 0.3095 | 8.5 in tread | 7.26 (16 lb) | 1.05 / 0.88 / 0.015 (e) | DOT-strip | M&H tables; shallow tread |
  | `tyre.gy268` | Goodyear 26.0x8.0-15 Blue Streak / Eagle slick | 26.45 | 0.3149 | 8 in | 8.20 (e) | 1.13 / 0.96 / 0.014 (e) | strip | period catalog / current equivalent ~26.4-26.5 in |
  | `tyre.mt245` | Mickey Thompson 24.5/8-15 slick | 24.50 | 0.2916 | 8 in | 7.26 (e) | 1.14 / 0.97 / 0.014 (e) | strip | TheSamba VW discussions |

  Rolling radius convention (unchanged for stock): `r = 0.307 * OD_in / 25.79`. A burnout / warm-up is not modelled; slick and cheater grip is the hot figure, with a soft warning. Front slot (hidden, property of Tyres): `tyreF.same` (default, no keys), `tyreF.stock`, `tyreF.x155`, `tyreF.cint155`, `tyreF.radial`, `tyreF.185ss`, `tyreF.mhfront` (M&H 4.5/26-15 front runner MSS-018: OD 25.9 in, 4.5 in tread, 6.5 in section, 13 lb, M&H tables). A claim that the Inch Pincher ran Pirelli fronts was not found in the EMPI history; the CA67 155 is offered as a period Pirelli front, not a documented Inch Pincher fitment.

* **Generic model.** New optional keys `muPeakFront`, `muSlideFront`, `crrFront`, `tyreRadiusFront`, `speedoRadius`. When unset, the existing single `muPeak` / `crr` / `tyreRadius` path is unchanged (stock and other cars bit-identical). Staggered road-course lateral grip uses the harmonic mean of front and rear `muPeak`. Rolling resistance is load-split only when `crrFront` is set. Tyre mass (`attrs.tyreMass`) adjusts `massKg` and axle inertias in `Parts.vehicle()`; tyres never overwrite `massKg` as a whole. The period speedo stays calibrated to the stock rolling radius (`speedoRadius`); indicated speed is shown only on the analogue dash (the HUD, drag trap and brake cues stay true speed). Taller tyres make the speedo under-read; shorter tyres make it over-read.

* **ETs** (same scripted drag driver as `test/drag_test.js`). Stock 30 PS Beetle, tyres all-round: stock tyres 23.2190 s / 54.755 mph / 3.121 60-ft (bit-identical); Michelin X 165 23.0323 / 55.491 / 3.109; M&H 26x6-15 slick 23.1203 / 55.034 / 3.113; M&H 8.5/26.0-15 slick 23.1402 / 55.084 / 3.122; Mickey Thompson 24.5/8-15 23.0723 / 55.330 / 3.095. The 30 PS car does not spin on stock tyres, so a slick is a small crr / diameter / mass change, not a traction win. 1600 DP longblock (legal companions): stock tyres 19.2966 s / 67.108 mph / 2.702 60-ft, 1.776 s of wheelspin; M&H 26x6-15 19.0611 / 67.395 / 2.578, no spin; M&H 8.5/26.0-15 19.0899 / 67.379 / 2.585; cheater 19.0808 / 67.335 / 2.581; Mickey Thompson 24.5/8-15 19.0363 / 67.738 / 2.567 (lowest trap-limited top speed, 140.2 vs 147.6 km/h); Goodyear 26.0x8.0-15 19.0952 / 67.320 / 2.589 (tallest, 151.4 km/h). Staggered 1600 DP, M&H 8.5 rear + M&H front runner: 19.0413 s.

* **Unchanged.** Every sound (111 audio hashes, worklet md5 `3573eb1e93f9e67a1616da2296b37d72`), the stock-tyre drag strip, the old flat Mesa Road lap (`?course=mesa`, Beetle 2:51.93). No per-car code. Old saves without `tyresF` load (`Parts.full()` fills `tyreF.same`). Not pushed.

* **Follow-ups (analysis only).** A Porsche 356 gearbox with a 4.59 final is not a standard 356 ratio (7:31 = 4.428, 6:31 = 5.167, 7:34 = 4.857). 4.57 is a Type 2 32:7. The Inch Pincher used a Porsche transaxle by 1967 (EMPI history). Adding a gearbox part on the existing slot (`vehicle.gears`, `finalDrive`) is a moderate follow-up, plus fitment. A chopped light Inch Pincher body: Inch Pincher Too (1971) used a chopped 1959 sunroof shell; the original also had fibreglass, plexi and weight removal. The `body` slot exists but is `menu: false` with only `body.stock`. A mass/Cd-only lightened body is easy; a visible chop is a larger art task.

* **Tests.** Parts gate PASS (all new tyres compose). Stock drag bit-identical to v1.13.39. Old Mesa lap 2:51.93, output identical to v1.13.39. engine-worklet.js byte-identical. Headless tyres ET / save-load / speedo script in `/tmp/v11340_tests/tyre_et.json`.

## v1.13.39 (local, not tagged or pushed), 2026-11-28 (Beetle parts: remaining Seume interchange-manual catalogue)

* **Why.** Wim's SR-13: compare the catalogue with the Bill Fisher / Seume-era VW speed books and add the parts that are still missing. The book already used in this repo (v1.13.31) is Keith Seume, *Aircooled VW Engine Interchange Manual* (Motorbooks, 1996, ISBN 0-7603-0314-2) — not John Seume. Bill Fisher's *How to Hot Rod Volkswagen Engines* (HP Books, 1970) is a different, earlier book and is not the OCR source here. Live main was v1.13.38 (`7a51130`).

* **Added (sourced, estimates marked).** Named book parts that were still missing after the v1.13.31 import, in existing slots where they have a modellable effect: Engle VZ-14 / 15 / 25 / 30 / 35 and hydraulic 1466 / 1467 / 1468, Scat C65 / C75 / C95, GBE GB302, SPG 82 mm roller crank (Seume p.22 warns against it), GBE / Bugpack / Pauter / CB / Super HO / Auto-Craft 910 / Scat Split-Port heads that print valve sizes, dual Solex 40P11, Weber 40 DCN, Type 3 Solex on a Type 1, Baby Dell 34 FRD, Autocavan 32 IMPE, Berg 42 DCNF and 58 mm IDA-pattern, JayCee Terminator 62 mm, Bosch 019 and 050, Vertex / Scintilla and Mallory magnetos, GBE Stinger S4 GB629B. Chamber volumes that the book does not print follow the v1.13.31 54 / 56 / 58 / 62 cc estimates. Venturis that are not printed are marked `e`. The Vertex / Mallory 38° total is a period Type 1 drag setting (Hot Rod Dec 1972, mid of 37–39°) marked as an estimate — Seume p.124 lists the magnetos but not a curve. The model's MBT cap stays 32°.

* **No performance effect.** Parts whose purpose is strength, oil control, spark energy, cooling headroom or dress-up, and heads / carbs whose valve size or venturi is not in the book, get a generic `purpose` key and the picker line “No performance effect — purpose: …”, as in v1.13.33. New slots (all with a no-keys OEM placeholder so stock data stays bit-identical): valve material, cam drive / timing gears, piston rings, valve covers, ignition coil / box, windage tray, oil-filler breather. Named aftermarket cases, Glyco bearings, lightened and wedgemated flywheels, Serck / Scat / EMPI coolers, GBE / Pauter / CB pulleys, some street mufflers and Street Tubes, 311-length aftermarket rods, roller lifters and JayCee pushrods are in existing slots the same way. Listed in `tools/parts/NO_EFFECT_PARTS.md` (v1.13.39 section).

* **Unchanged.** Every sound (111 audio hashes bit-identical), the drag strip, the old flat Mesa Road lap (`?course=mesa`, Beetle 2:51.93). No per-car code. Not pushed.

* **Same-day review (Wim).** Re-read the p.69–71 cam chart from a clean transcription (TheSamba Engine Blueprint Specifications worksheet) and filled remaining figures from manufacturer and period sources (Gene Berg, Limebug Scat cards, Porsche 912 Solex 40 P II-4 workshop manual, Weber jetting tables, sparkingplugs.com / type2.com distributor curves, Hot Rod Dec 1972 / Feb 1971). Added the left-out chart grinds (Auto-Craft, Bugpack, CB Performance, Crower, remaining Engle, FAT, remaining GBE, Norris, Pauter including VN roller cams, Scat C85 / C89, Web-Cam, Weber Cam), Bugpack 86 / 90 mm and SPG 78 mm cranks, Chevy 327 / 400 / Capri rods, and the Scat/Mallory adjustable distributor. Corrected Scat C20–C95: the book’s first duration column is advertised, not duration at 0.050. GBE GB302 LSA 108° is now sourced from the p.69 chart. GBE GB850 / 840 / 852 / 855 use factory 35.5 × 32 mm valves (sourced-secondary) and now have a performance model. Valve material is a property of the heads (Manley / Del West kept as the source of that property); piston rings are a property of the cylinders / pistons; flywheel hardware is a property of Clutch / flywheel (it was listed twice). Valve covers stay as a slot, flagged no performance effect; ignition coil / box is unchanged. The hidden `valves` / `rings` / `flyhw` keys remain so old saves still load. Remaining estimates (IVC, chamber cc, a few venturis and ignition statics, unread pulley / muffler diameters) are still flagged; see `/workspace/streetrod-parts/seume/MISSING_BY_PAGE.md`. After Wim asked for confirmation that a with-effect part moves both models, `soundOverrides` now forwards `tm.rod_l` as the sound kernel’s `car.rod` (generic; the worklet already has Q_ROD). Stock audio hashes, the worklet file, the drag strip and the old Mesa lap are unchanged. The sound kernel still has no intake-valve diameter, so a head that only changes `tm.iv_d` (the 1600 041, same 32 mm exhaust as the 040) moves the dyno but not the rendered sample; that is a frozen-worklet limit, not a parts-layer miss.

## v1.13.38 (local, not tagged or pushed), 2026-11-28 (Stage map, coloured Beetle rivals, live rear-view, higher interior HUD)

* **Why.** Wim drove v1.13.37 and asked for a map so he can see where he is and where the opponents are, and for the opponents to be VW bugs in another colour.
* **Map (js/main.js, js/road.js `mapProj`).** The top-right tile now draws the whole Mesa Run outline (a dark under-stroke so it reads on the desert), you as a cream chevron that follows your heading, each rival as a dot in its car colour, checkpoints as gold ticks, the start in green and the finish in white. A compact position list sits under the tile (hidden on the small portrait tile; shown when the map is enlarged). The tile is larger than in v1.13.37 (about 11 % of the scene width, 84-154 px; 78 px in portrait) but still a corner chip — it does not cover the road. Phone portrait now shows it too, in the top-right beside the dash (it was hidden there). Tap the map, or press M, to enlarge it (tap again to shrink).
* **Rivals as Beetles (generic course key `rivals`, no per-car code).** Mesa Run sets `rivals: { body: 'beetle', colours: [...] }`. js/road-traffic.js reads that key; a course without it still spawns the old coupes. The three pace slots stay 0.90 / 0.95 / 0.99 of the player car's point-mass profile. Colours are taken from the course palette in order, skipping any colour within 72 RGB of the player's `paint` (Beetle Agave, Model X matt black; cars without a paint field keep Red / Yellow / Blue). A Type 1 sprite (bubble roof, oval rear window, round lamps, running boards) is drawn in js/road3d.js `carSprite('beetle', ...)`. Body size is the sourced 1956 Type 1: 4.07 x 1.54 x 1.50 m, 805 kg (the game Beetle's 730 kg ready + 75 kg driver). Civilian traffic is unchanged.
* **Live rear-view (js/road3d.js look-back, js/visuals-painted.js).** On a stage course the interior rear-view glass is filled with a live render of the road behind the car (rivals and traffic, camera-relative fronts so a following car shows its nose, flipped left-to-right). The same fill is used for the BRM’s round stalk glasses. The chase view gets a small generic mirror strip at the top of the frame. Cars without a `mirror` / `stalks` rect keep their existing painting; off the stage the glass still shows the old desert crop. No per-car code beyond the rects already in the art.
* **Interior HUD.** The cabin framing is panned up so a visor / sky band sits at the top of the canvas. The race HUD (and the brake banner under it) moves to that band, clear of the dashboard and the road. The small map drops just under the mirror so it does not cover the glass, but only when the top-right is free (deck below the scene, or portrait). On a short landscape with GAS / AUTO on the canvas the tile stays in the top-right sky, or moves left of the gear chip, and never covers a control.
* **Interior rear-view on a short landscape.** If the cabin glass is cropped off the top (iPhone landscape, behind the top bar), the same generic mirror strip used in the chase view is drawn in the visible safe area so the road behind is still on screen.
* **Tests.** New test/rivals_map_test.js (course key, beetle bodies, 0.90/0.95/0.99 pace, paint-avoidance, traffic unchanged, map projection). aids / arcade / traction / collide / physics pass. Drag strip and the old lap (2:51.93) are bit-identical to v1.13.37 (md5 match of road_test and drag_test output). Audio 111/111 identical to v1.13.37 (streetrod-parts/checks/v11338_audio.json). Parts gate PASS. Syntax ES2017 OK (34 scripts + worklet). engine-worklet.js byte-identical to v1.13.37. gearbox_test still fails as before (Beetle vs v1.10.4). Headless picker 704 OK (streetrod-parts/checks/check_v11338_local.json). Menu fit 6/6 (v11338_local_fit.txt; iphone_portrait_safari timed out in the first batch and passed on retry, v11338b_fit.txt).

## v1.13.37 (local, not tagged or pushed), 2026-11-28 (Stage course: traction control, ABS, brake cue, compact HUD)

* **Why.** Wim drove v1.13.36 and wrote: add ABS and traction control (the BRM is undrivable; on the drag strip you can modulate power, on a road course it is about cornering and braking), add a small banner showing when to brake, and the window top left blocks the view. Numbers: lotus-notes/launch_v11337.txt, stage_runs_v11337.txt, traction_after_v11337.txt.
* **Root cause found on the way: the v1.13.36 aids never reached the engine model.** js/road.js returned abs / tc in the course input, but js/main.js built the engine input from grip / resist / grade only. So BRM, GT350 and Caddy ran with no traction aid and no ABS at all. main.js now passes every key of the course input (`Object.assign({ throttle, brake }, rIn)`). test/aids_test.js guards it.
* **Traction control (physics.js; generic key `inp.tcl`, default off; ICE and EV).**
  * Feed-forward torque cap: the driven tyres' peak friction x axle load (with the load transfer of the acceleration and the share of grip the bend already uses) plus what spins up the wheels' own inertia, through the gearing and driveline loss, x `tcl` (0.92 = ESTIMATE, a margin below peak). The throttle is trimmed with the exact inverse of the engine torque curve (`Vehicle.throttleFor`), no on / off cutting.
  * It also caps what the clutch or torque converter can pass (a 400 Nm clutch otherwise keeps the wheels spinning with a limited engine), and cuts towards sliding friction if the tyre has already gone into spin (spin-exit raised to 11.5 % slip under TC). The bend-stability trim (`inp.tc`, js/road.js) is now smooth: first-order, 0.1 s, down to 15 %, instead of a quick on / off.
  * Without `tcl` nothing changes: the drag strip, cruising and the old lap are bit-identical (drag_test and road_test output identical to v1.13.36).
* **ABS (physics.js; `inp.abs`, now actually delivered).** Per-axle brake force cap at 0.97 of the tyre's peak friction x its load (a hold just under lock-up, so it stays at peak slip instead of releasing). 120 -> 0 km/h: Beetle 5.2 -> 4.3 s, Caddy 5.1 -> 3.6 s, GT350 4.3 -> 3.5 s, BRM 3.8 -> 3.3 s (locked wheels before).
* **Indicator lights and toggle.** Two small lamps (TC, ABS) lit while the aid works, in the top-left line. Key T or a tap on the lamps switches both aids off or on (default ON on the road course; the choice is kept). `Sim.aids = false` removes the keys from the course input.
* **Brake cue (js/road.js `brakeCue`, `cueLevel`; generic).** For each point ahead: the speed the bend allows (grip x curvature x speed factor, x 0.93 = ESTIMATE, at the lane offset) and the distance the car needs to slow to it at 85 % (ESTIMATE) of what its brakes and tyres give now (brake fade included). Slack = distance - needed distance. Amber when the slack is under 2.8 s of driving, red under 0.45 s (latched until you are within 5 % of the target). A small banner in the top centre with the distance; it never covers the road. Following only the banner (GAS held, BRAKE while red) every car finishes the stage inside 0.3 over the limit (Caddy 0.6: its drums fade; known gap).
* **HUD.** The box top left (time, map, best time, next-bend text) is replaced by one slim translucent line: time left, speed, gear, position, checkpoint, TC / ABS lamps. The map is a small tile top right (hidden in phone portrait), the sound debug line is hidden on the course, and everything scales with the viewport (iPhone landscape and portrait, Tesla 1920 x 1200, desktop).
* **Before / after (digital GAS / BRAKE 0 or 1 with the touch-button ramps, key steering, auto-shift; whole stage; BRM, v1.13.36 as live -> v1.13.37).**
  * Standing start, full throttle for 14 s: BRM 0-100 km/h 7.78 -> 6.05 s (spinning 13.4 s -> 0, slip up to 10.8 -> 0); GT350 7.93 -> 7.08 s; Caddy 10.92 -> 10.38 s. 911, Beetle, Model X are not traction limited there and are unchanged.
  * Stage, braking to a plan: BRM 2:44.47 -> 2:45.01, wheelspin 77.5 -> 0 s, slip over 15 % 62.5 -> 0 s, locked 13.2 -> 0 s. GT350 3:00.30 -> 2:59.01 (spin 28.9 -> 0, lock 9.9 -> 0). Caddy 3:26.42 -> 3:23.95 (spin 25.9 -> 0, lock 15.2 -> 0). 911 2:48.16 -> 2:48.71, Model X 2:43.97 -> 2:44.95, Beetle 4:25.39 -> 4:24.69. No car leaves the road more than before; no run with the aids has any wheelspin or lock-up.
  * Stage with GAS held down all the time (brakes only when the plan says): BRM 2:41.78 -> 2:42.66, spin 100.7 -> 0 s, lock 31.6 -> 0 s.
* **Tests.** New test/aids_test.js (40 checks: engine gets the keys; TC with no squeal and smooth throttle on BRM / GT350 / Caddy; unchanged cars; ABS vs locked wheels on six cars; brake cue distances; the banner follower finishes on five cars). Arcade, traction (the old table values within 0.1 m), collide, road (old lap 2:51.93, md5 identical to v1.13.36), drag (md5 identical), physics passing; gearbox_test still fails as before v1.13.37 (Beetle vs v1.10.4). Audio 111/111 identical (streetrod-parts/checks/v11337_audio.json). Parts gate PASS. Headless picker 704 OK (check_v11337_local.json). Menu fit 6/6 (v11337_fit.txt; iphone_portrait_safari timed out in the first batch and passed on retry, v11337c_fit.txt). Syntax ES2017 OK. engine-worklet.js byte-identical to v1.13.36.

## v1.13.36 (local, not tagged or pushed), 2026-11-27 (Stage course: drive controls and traction)

* **Why.** Wim drove v1.13.35 and found the road course "not really a pleasure" for two reasons. The drive buttons felt off, and there was no traction when accelerating out of a corner or braking into one. This pass is meant to make it fun. Full audit: lotus-notes/CONTROLS.md. Numbers: lotus-notes/TRACTION.md.
* **Traction: root causes (js/road.js stepArcade, arcade course only).**
  * The share of tyre grip that the road model hands to the engine model (inp.grip = 1 - (ay / ayCap)^2, floor 0.05) fell to almost nothing at the cornering limit. The rear axle's normal load was scaled with it, so in a bend any real throttle lit the tyres up and any firm brake locked them.
    * Out of the R30 hairpin on full throttle, the tyres squealed for 2.6 s (911), 5.4 s (GT350) and 5.7 s (BRM).
    * Full braking into it locked the wheels for 1.0-1.5 s (Beetle, GT350, BRM) and cut the deceleration (Beetle 0.64 g, GT350 0.77 g).
  * The friction circle took the whole lateral budget for driving or braking (circ floor 0.15, so 39 % of the grip was left). The scrub resistance fed back into it, so heavy throttle or brakes in a bend swung the outward push up to 2.5x.
  * The cars without ABS (Beetle, GT350, BRM) had nothing to stop lock-ups, and nothing limited wheelspin.
* **Traction: what changed (generic keys, used only when the course sets `arcade`; no per-car code).**
  * Grip share for driving and braking while cornering: 1 - 0.35 (ay / ayCap)^2, so at least 65 % (was down to 5 %).
  * Friction circle: aMax x sqrt(1 - 0.35 (ax / mu g)^2), at least 84 % of aMax (was 39 %). It uses the drive / brake acceleration smoothed over 0.25 s, without the scrub feedback. Lifting off still pulls you in a little.
  * Traction and stability aid: js/road.js hands `inp.tc` (a throttle share) to the engine model.
    * It trims the throttle when the driven tyres pass 10 % slip: x (1 - 10 (slip - 0.1)), at least 0.3.
    * It also trims it when the car is driven past its cornering limit: x (1 - 10 (over - 0.03)), at least 0.1.
    * It acts within 0.05 s and recovers in 0.35 s. It never touches the brakes.
  * ABS aid: `inp.abs` uses the engine model's existing ABS clamp (0.97 x muPeak x axle load) for every car on the stage.
  * js/physics.js reads the two new keys only when they are present (`inp.tc`, `inp.abs`). The drag strip, cruise and the old lap do not pass them, so they are bit-identical.
* **Scripted hairpin drives (lotus-notes/tools/traction.js; v1.13.35 -> v1.13.36).**

  | Car / drive | Squeal (wheelspin or lock-up) | Line loss | Exit or brake |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | Beetle, full throttle from the apex | 0 -> 0 s | 0.16 -> 0.13 m | 52 -> 52 km/h |
  | Beetle, full brake from 75 km/h | 1.05 -> 0 s | 0.11 -> 0.11 m | 0.64 -> 0.76 g |
  | Beetle, at the limit, brake 0.5 in the bend | 0 -> 0 s | 0.10 -> 0.06 m | 0.45 -> 0.44 g |
  | 911, full throttle from the apex | 2.58 -> 0 s | 1.13 -> 0.95 m | 76 -> 73 km/h |
  | 911, full throttle from turn-in + 15 m | 4.53 -> 0 s | 1.52 -> 1.52 m | 76 -> 74 km/h |
  | 911, full brake from 90 km/h | 0 -> 0 s (ABS) | 0.13 -> 0.13 m | 1.07 -> 1.07 g |
  | 911, at the limit, brake 0.5 in the bend | 0 -> 0 s | 0.17 -> 0.08 m | 0.64 -> 0.63 g |
  | GT350, full throttle from the apex | 5.35 -> 0 s | 0.45 -> 0.80 m | 62 -> 67 km/h |
  | GT350, full brake from 90 km/h | 1.45 -> 0 s | 0.12 -> 0.13 m | 0.77 -> 0.94 g |
  | BRM, full throttle from the apex | 5.67 -> 0 s | 0.15 -> 0.59 m | 66 -> 68 km/h |

  The cars now accelerate out of the bend instead of spinning their tyres, so they reach the exit faster and give up a little line (at most 1.5 m, always on the asphalt). The hold / pass speeds from v1.13.35 are unchanged (cornerSpeed is the same).
* **Stage runs (test/stage_test.js, no traffic).** Every car finishes with 0 hits, 0 cars inside obstacles and no NaN.

  | Car | v1.13.35 | v1.13.36 |
  |---|---|---|
  | GT350 | 3:06.47 | 3:05.43 |
  | Caddy | 3:34.15 | 3:33.36 |
  | BRM | 2:55.49 | 2:51.17 |
  | 911 | 2:56.06 | 2:56.03 |
  | Beetle | 4:27.98 | 4:27.91 |
  | Model X | 2:49.00 | 2:48.27 |

* **Drive controls (road course only; drag strip and cruise keep their pedals).** See lotus-notes/CONTROLS.md for the audit.
  * Left thumb steers, right thumb drives. The steering pad is bottom left. GAS (lowest) and BRAKE are bottom right, with a gear chip. Phone portrait uses the deck under the scene, and the Tesla uses the band under the scene. Phone landscape and desktop place them on the bottom corners, clear of the speed panel and the HUD.
  * Steering pad: the outer third of each half is full lock. Toward the middle it follows a u^1.2 curve with a 5 % dead zone. Dragging on the scene no longer steers on the course.
  * GAS and BRAKE are full anywhere on the button. They were pressure-by-height, so a resting thumb gave 20-50 %.
  * Gears shift themselves by default (AUTO, saved). Up at 93 % of the limiter; down when the lower gear has room and the revs are low, or earlier on the brakes. Tap AUTO or press O for manual (- / + on the chip; . / , or PageUp / PageDown).
  * Keyboard on the course: arrows or WASD. Left and right steer: they used to shift gears. S brakes: it used to switch the engine off. Gas and brake reach full in 0.25 s (were 0.7 / 0.6 s).
  * Short entry message.
* **Tests.** New test/traction_test.js (13 checks). The arcade, collide, road (old lap 2:51.93), drag, physics, gearbox, old-model, syntax, release-check, fit and audio results are in the v1.13.36 report.


## v1.13.35 (local, not tagged or pushed), 2026-11-27 (Stage course: Lotus 2-style handling on the real engine)

* **Why.** Wim drove v1.13.34. The course and the other cars are fine, but the handling felt off. His choice: Lotus Turbo Challenge 2 handling, with our real engine underneath. Public descriptions (lotus-notes/HANDLING.md: Amiga Format #29, the Lemon Amiga reviews, Retro Gamer, MobyGames) say the handling is responsive, that lifting off briefly "pulls you in" on a corner, that you brake for the sharper ones, and that collisions are "friendlier, bouncier".
* **Arcade lateral model (js/road.js stepArcade, course key `arcade`, default off; on for Mesa Run only).**
  * The car stays pointed down the road: its heading is 0.6 x its direction of travel, and never more than 20 degrees.
  * Steering sets a sideways rate of 1.6 + 0.17 v m/s at full lock (at most 6.2 m/s and 0.8 v), and the car follows it with a 0.11 s lag.
  * A bend carries the car to the outside at the full-lock rate x v^2 k / aMax, capped at 2.5x. aMax is the car's own lateral grip: tyre muPeak x latK x speed factor x surface, reduced by the friction circle for what driving or braking uses, so lifting off pulls you in.
  * Full lock into the bend therefore holds the line up to the tyre grip limit. Beyond it the car drifts wide, and the excess lateral demand scrubs speed (0.55 per m/s^2, at most 0.8 x muSlide x surface x g). It never spins.
  * The verge, dirt and sand have lower grip, so you go wider, and their rolling drag slows you as before.
* **Longitudinal motion is unchanged.** The real engine, gearbox, drag, rolling and grade model (js/physics.js) still drives the car, through the same generic inp.grip / inp.resist / inp.grade. No per-car code and no new physics keys.
* **Collisions (same engine, softer for the player).**
  * The impulse still sets the speed along the road and the bounce backwards.
  * The sideways part becomes a kick of at most 5 m/s that dies away in 0.35 s.
  * The yaw kick becomes a short heading wobble (at most 0.22 rad, damped in 0.28 s), with half steering for 0.45 s. No spins; the heading stays within 20 degrees.
  * Offset hits glance off. When the overlap is 0.8 m or less, the car is knocked aside, straight and clear of the object, keeping up to 0.6 x its speed (scaled by how little the bodies overlapped). A square hit still stops the car and bounces it back.
  * Beetle at 75 km/h into a boulder (test/stage_test.js, a node probe), with the speed kept after the first hit:

    | Lateral offset | Speed kept |
    |---|---|
    | 0 m | 0 km/h (bounce) |
    | 0.5 m | 0 km/h |
    | 0.9 m | 22 km/h |
    | 1.3 m | 34 km/h |
    | 1.0 m the other side | 25 km/h |

    The car drives on in every case, with no spin.
* **Controls.** In the stage, keys and the steering pad reach full lock in 0.17 s and centre or reverse in 0.1 s (was 0.25 s both ways). Keyboard, the touch pad and the Tesla screen use the same input path. The HUD says TOO FAST instead of SLIDING, and the tyre screech follows how far over the limit you are and the hit kicks.
* **Numbers.** The grip-limit (hold) speed is the steady speed with full lock that keeps the line. The constant-speed pass speed is the fastest a car can go through the whole bend at constant speed from the right-lane line, using the road width, without leaving the asphalt (lotus-notes/tools/arc_speeds.js).

  | Car | R30 hairpin: hold | R30 hairpin: pass | R90 esses: hold | R90 esses: pass | R150: hold | R150: pass |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | Beetle (stock) | 48.5 km/h | 53.9 km/h | 82.0 km/h | 94.3 km/h | 104.1 km/h | 110.0 km/h |
  | 911 (997) | 57.3 km/h | 63.9 km/h | 96.3 km/h | 113.2 km/h | 121.8 km/h | 130.6 km/h |
  | BRM | 56.0 km/h | 62.5 km/h | 94.3 km/h | 110.4 km/h | 119.3 km/h | 127.7 km/h |

  Tyre mu x 1.15 holds the hairpin 5 % faster (test/arcade_test.js). Test driver, no traffic: Beetle 4:27.98, GT350 3:06.47, Cadillac 3:34.15, BRM 2:55.49, 911 2:56.06, Model X 2:49.00 (v1.13.34: 4:30.00 / 3:06.52 / 3:34.90 / 2:58.30 / 3:08.68 / 3:18.25). Every car finishes with 13.9-25.5 s in hand, with no hits and never inside an object. The stage allowances are unchanged (par x 1.3 + 3 s).
* **AI / run fixes found while testing.**
  * The line chooser no longer moves across a boulder it would reach while changing lines.
  * Civilians jammed for more than 7 s are cleared even in sight, so the rock-fall gridlock is gone.
  * Being put back at the same spot twice within 6 s moves you up to 40 m past the obstacle.
  * The object list is re-sorted while knocked objects slide, so collision lookups cannot skip a boulder. This fixes a slow creep into a boulder after a post was knocked.
* **Unchanged.** Every sound (111 audio hashes bit-identical), the old lap (?course=mesa, test/road_test.js Beetle 2:51.93), drag strip, cruising, parts, the course, the rivals and traffic.

## v1.13.34 (local prototype, not tagged or pushed), 2026-11-27 (Road course: Lotus-style stage with hills, traffic, rivals and collisions)

* **Why.** Wim (t27u): the road course should be more in the style of Lotus Turbo Challenge 2 (Amiga, 1991), with a collision engine, but with up-to-date graphics. Research notes (public sources only, no ROMs / ADFs run, no assets used): Lotus 2 is a commercial game (Gremlin / Magnetic Fields, 1991), not freeware; its makers tolerated non-commercial copying, and the Lotus name was licensed separately. Taken from it: the behind-the-car view, stages of checkpoints with a countdown whose spare time carries on, coasting when time runs out, traffic, roadside objects, and crashes that bounce and slow you down rather than end the race.
* **Course (js/road.js, data).** Mesa Run, original: 6.24 km, 4 checkpoints and the finish (1154 / 2338 / 3305 / 4975 / 6237 m), hills from smooth cosine ramps (+16 / -7 m, steepest grade 9.4 %), a rock fall with five boulders that block half a lane, posts every 50 m, chevron boards on the outside of bends, a gantry at each checkpoint, cactus, Joshua trees and rocks. The v1.13.32 flat lap is unchanged and still available with ?course=mesa (test/road_test.js: stock Beetle 2:51.93 as before).
* **Stage clock (js/road.js Stage).** Each section has a par time from a point-mass speed profile of the car you drive (0.8 x peak power, traction, aero, rolling, grade, corner limit, braking). The time added is par x 1.3 + 3 s (+5 s for the first section; 1.3 so that the AI rivals, which pass traffic carefully, finish inside the total). Time left carries on. At 0 the engine is cut and you coast; reaching the next checkpoint while still rolling puts you back in. Best times are kept per car under the course id mesa-run.
* **Collision engine (js/collide.js, generic).** Oriented boxes in road space (station, offset): a separating-axis test, swept sub-steps (each step at most 40 % of the smallest half-size, including rotation) so nothing passes through at any speed, time-of-impact positions, push-out by inverse mass, and impulses with restitution, Coulomb friction and a yaw kick from the contact lever arm. Sweep-and-prune is used between cars, and a binary search over props sorted by station. Posts and boards are light (6 / 18 kg) and get knocked flying; boulders, gantry legs and trees are fixed. After a hit, the car keeps its new speed along its heading (and a short rebound backwards). The sideways part becomes a slide that the tyres scrub off at their lateral grip. The yaw kick becomes a spin that decays (0.55 s), with a game aid that never leaves you facing backwards. Steering and grip are reduced for 0.7 s ("the control goes wibbly"). If the car is pinned or stopped off the road, it is put back on the clearest line after 1.2 / 2.5 s. test/collide_test.js covers a thin post at 300 km/h at 20 / 30 / 60 fps, head-on momentum, rear-end, rock bounce, glancing scrape, corner into a tree, a knocked post, and 20 cars + 3000 props (about 0.2 ms per frame).
* **Other cars (js/road-traffic.js).** Three rivals (Red / Yellow / Blue) drive 0.90 / 0.95 / 0.99 of your car's own speed profile and race you for position. Slower traffic in your lane (50-75 km/h: sedans, pickups, vans, lorries) and oncoming cars with headlights share one line chooser: pass on the other lane only when the oncoming side is clear, and abort the pass otherwise. They spawn only where the lane is free, are recycled ahead of you, and get re-placed if stuck.
* **Physics hook (js/physics.js, generic, default off).** inp.grade (rise / run, + uphill): the ICE solver adds m g grade to the speed-dependent body forces (moving only); the EV solver adds it to its drag sum. It is only sent on hilly courses, so the drag strip, cruising and the old lap course see no change. No per-car code.
* **Rendering (js/road3d.js, painted styles).** A pseudo-3D road in true plan perspective, pitched with the grade under the car. Crest clipping means what lies behind a hill top stays hidden. Ground and road are drawn in batched fills in six haze bands to 640 m, with red / white rumble strips in bends and a near-plane polygon clip for when the car is turned across the road. The painted desert sky is cached and slid with heading and pitch. Objects and cars are procedural sprites with ink outlines in the Ink style (rear / front views, brake lamps, headlights), drawn far to near; cars just beside or behind yours are drawn over it. 60 fps in headless Chromium (Mac 1440 x 900, Tesla 1920 x 1200) and WebKit (iPhone portrait / landscape).
* **HUD.** Countdown (it pulses under 10 s; TIME UP), race clock, checkpoint n / 5 and the distance to it, position P n / 4, the next bend, CHECKPOINT +N s, Crash! / Head-on! / Contact!, and a short camera shake on hits. The time-up and finish cards show hits and position. Tyre screech also comes from collision slides.
* **Numbers (sim driver, test/stage_test.js --no-traffic, total of the section pars in brackets).** Beetle 4:30.00 (264.8 s), GT350 3:06.52 (178.2), 911 3:08.68 (165.2), Model X 3:18.25 (164.3), Cadillac 3:34.90 (201.9), BRM 2:58.30 (158.8). Every car finishes with 14.1-25.5 s in hand at the tightest checkpoint, with no NaN and never ending a frame inside a fixed object. With traffic, the rivals finish in 1.2-1.4x par (Beetle 278-302 s against a 364 s total allowance; BRM 199-229 s against 226 s). The simple test driver passes traffic poorly (5 of 6 cars finish, some on coasting time), which is a limit of the test driver, not a target. The rivals start staggered (12 / 24 / 36 m, Red in the other lane), so nobody is directly in front of you at the line.
* **Unchanged.** Every sound (111 audio hashes bit-identical), drag strip, garage, engine bay, parts, the v1.13.32 lap course (?course=mesa).

## v1.13.33 (tag `v1.13.33`), 2026-11-27 (Swap parts: purpose notes, config below the list)

* **Why.** Wim (t26u): some parts have no impact on performance — mention their purpose so they can be picked up later; the build/config panel at the top of Swap parts eats the screen and hides the part list.
* **No-effect parts.** 28 catalogue parts were dyno-diffed against stock and against 1600 DP / stroker / big-bore bases (peak power and torque, the full torque curve, CR, displacement, limiter, mass, tyre grip and rolling drag, gears, inertia). They do not change those figures. Each now has a generic `purpose` key (data only, no per-car code) shown in the picker as a short grey line: “No performance effect — purpose: …”. The list, with what a later model could pick up, is in `tools/parts/NO_EFFECT_PARTS.md`. Cooling, oil, bearings, case savers, spacers, lifters, pushrods, springs, pumps, filters, pulleys, fuel pumps, the 12 V conversion (starter sound only), Super petrol (knock warning only) and the GT/Monza styling muffler (Seume p.114 restriction not in the model: `tm.k_exh` is unused while the exhaust side is driven by sound geometry, and this part sets no geometry). Clutches are not in the list: `clutchMax` is used by the physics when the clutch slips.
* **Layout.** The build/config summary (displacement, CR, peaks, price total, hard/soft notes) is no longer sticky over the slot list. It sits at the bottom of the scrolled list, below the parts, so the selects are visible first on iPhone Safari portrait, the Tesla browser and a Mac. The Apply / Stock / Close footer stays pinned. Other screens are unchanged.
* **Unchanged.** Every sound (111 audio hashes bit-identical), torque model, parts compatibility and prices, road course.

## v1.13.32 (tag `v1.13.32`), 2026-11-26 (Road course draft: one winding road, solo)

* **Why.** Wim (t19u, t24s1): a road-course race like the 1989 Street Rod, first step "one curved road, driving solo". Feasibility report D: a light lateral grip budget on the existing longitudinal solver, not a second physics engine.
* **Mode.** New opt-in mode next to the drag strip: Road course button / key C. Drag strip, cruising, garage and engine bay are unchanged when it is off (the straight-road renderer and the A / D lane wander are untouched; entering one mode leaves the other).
* **Course (js/road.js, data only).** Mesa Road, original: 60 m run-up, start line, 600 m start straight, long sweeper R260 35° right, R130 55° left, esses R90 45° / 50°, 700 m back straight, Mesa hairpin R30 160° right, R65 80° left, R180 40° right, R110 45° left, finish straight; 4023 m start to finish (2.5 miles, a nod to the 1989 manual's "about two and a half miles"), 350 m run-off. Bends have linear curvature ramps (clothoids, 20–50 m). Three split points, best time per car in localStorage (`streetrod.road.best.<car>.mesa`).
* **Lateral layer (js/road.js, generic).** State: station s, lateral offset y, heading to the road psi, path curvature (first-order, 0.15 s, estimate). Cornering limit = tyre muPeak (vehicle or tyre part) × latK × speed factor × surface. latK 0.79 = 0.617 g skidpad (Road & Track, March 1971, VW Super Beetle on 5.60-15, 100 ft radius) / the Beetle's 0.78 muPeak (estimate); used for every car (estimate beyond the Beetle), optional vehicle key `latK`. Speed factor from the Bosch handbook dry-asphalt figures (0.85 / 0.80 / 0.75 at 50 / 90 / 130 km/h; Wong, Theory of Ground Vehicles 5th ed. Table 1.6). Friction circle: ay_max = mu_lat g √(1 − (ax_tyre / muPeak g)²). Over the limit the car follows ay_max (runs wide), grip falls to muSlide / muPeak at 30 % over, and the slip angle (7° at the limit, estimate) gives cornering drag m·ay·sin α. Surfaces by wheel position: asphalt to 3.6 m, gravel shoulder to 4.6 m (crr 0.02, mu 0.60), dirt to 10 m (crr 0.05, mu 0.68), sand beyond (crr 0.30) (Bosch / Wong Tables 1.1, 1.2, 1.4), fence at 14 m.
* **Physics hooks (js/physics.js, generic, default off).** `inp.grip` scales the tyre normal loads used for traction / brake-lock limits (what the corner and the surface leave); `inp.resist` adds a body resistance (cornering drag + off-road rolling). Absent = unchanged (physics, drag and gearbox tests identical). The EV path takes `resist` only.
* **Steering.** A / D (keys ramp at 4 / s in this mode), or the on-screen steering pad (touch: iPhone, Tesla; mouse too): finger position = input, release = straight. Full input asks for 110 % of the grip limit at the current speed (a steering aid), never more than the turning-circle lock (generic key turnCircle, default 11 m kerb to kerb, ESTIMATE). Left / Right stay gearshift.
* **Rendering (js/visuals-painted.js).** In this mode the road is drawn along the course in true plan geometry from the same pitched cameras (cabin eye, chase camera behind the car): asphalt, shoulders, edge lines, centre dashes, start / split / finish lines, roadside bushes and posts by station, yellow chevron boards on the outside of bends under R200. The painted sky turns with the heading (one full turn = the painting and its mirror image; the mirrored sun is painted out so a turn shows one sun).
* **HUD.** Time, sector, split delta to the best, next bend with its grip-limit speed, surface / sliding warnings, mini map; result card with sectors, average, top speed and time off the road.
* **Numbers (stock 1956 Beetle, sim driver, test/road_test.js).** Cornering limits: hairpin 48.5 km/h, R65 70 km/h, R90 82 km/h, R130 97 km/h, R260 134 km/h (top speed ~105 km/h). Clean run 2:51.9 (84 km/h average); digital key-style steering 2:53.1; with 10 % margin 2:54.1. Michelin X radial part: 2:47.5.
* **Unchanged.** Every sound (111 audio hashes bit-identical), parts data and gate (v1.13.31 catalogue), drag strip. Built on v1.13.31: the tyre parts (Michelin X radial and the rest) feed the road grip through the merged vehicle muPeak / muSlide.
## v1.13.31 (tag `v1.13.31`), 2026-11-26 (Beetle parts: book catalogue, compatibility, prices)

* **Why.** Wim gave the go to put the Seume *Aircooled VW Engine Interchange Manual* (1996) parts, compatibility rules and prices into the game, on live main `dc4a2ca` / tag v1.13.30. Another worker is doing a curved-road prototype in a separate clone; this release stays in parts / compat / price / picker UI (plus generic default-off torque-model keys). Stock sound and the 111 audio hashes stay bit-identical. Not pushed.
* **Follow-up (t25u, same unpushed release).** (1) CB 044 CNC chamber is now a sourced 65 cc (`tm.vch`, CB Performance 1432, 42 × 37.5 on 90.5/92; 66 cc also appears on the CSP 94 mm listing) plus a 1.0 mm assembled deck estimate (`tm.deck`). `tmOf` computes clearance volume as chamber + bore-area × deck, so CR follows the current bore and stroke instead of a packed 72 cc that was a 94 × 82 estimate (that combo is now CR 8.91; on a 1600 it is a correctly low 6.60, with a 90.5 mm-minimum warning). The same split is used for the 042 (54 cc sourced, p.40), the 1996 044 Magnum (54 cc as the 042, estimate), and the Scat / GBE / Pauter heads that only had a packed chamber+deck. (2) Seume clearance / fitment is implemented generically: crankcase and cam clearancing and reduced journals above 78 mm (p.16, p.21); barrel spacers above 82 mm (p.86); case/head machining from 88 mm and 10 mm savers at 94 mm (p.74, p.85); heater boxes vs valves past 1600 size (p.113); valve-to-piston / lift and duration street cautions (p.56, p.58); rocker geometry and pushrod length (p.58, p.60); oil pump 26/30 mm vs gallery size (p.134) and short vs long shaft vs 3-bolt / 4-bolt cam gears (p.141); 8-dowel flywheel vs factory pattern (p.12) — the Okrasa TSV kit now includes `fw.dowel8`; counterweighted crank clearance (p.16–20); turbo CR and turbo cam (p.109, p.87); 92 mm thin-wall CR (p.87); 009 points bounce above 5500 (p.119); power-pulley cooling (p.141); fuel pump sizing (p.111). Hard vs soft follows the book: 84 mm stroke, 30 mm pump on a small-gallery case, shaft/gear mismatch and flywheel pattern are hard; the rest are warnings. (3) Thirteen new slots where they carry compatibility or a modellable effect (compat-only is labelled, no invented power): crankcase, bearings, flywheel hardware, case savers, cylinder spacers, lifters, pushrods, valve springs, oil pump, oil filter, sump, crank pulley, fuel pump. Many more book parts in existing slots (FK and Scat C cams, 67/69/74/Rabbit-78 cranks, 87/88 mm barrels, 040 / Round-Port / GBE / Pauter A heads, Bugspray carbs, VE4, GBE 1.50 in merged header, Monza muffler, factory 50 mm / 55 mm rods). Missing prices still show as “no price found”. Stock and all 111 audio hashes stay bit-identical.
* **Reconciliations (sourced).** Okrasa stroker is 69 mm / 1285 cc on 77 mm barrels (Seume p.17); the earlier 69.5 mm catalogue figure was the separate EMPI crank, now `crank.empi695`. `heads.cb044` stays the late-2003 CNC Wedge Port (42 × 37.5); the 1996 Super Sport / Magnum 44 (40 × 35.5, 3/4 in plugs, Seume p.40) is `heads.cb044mag`. Engle W-series lifts in the book are at the cam; the game stores valve lift × 1.1 (W110 0.392 → 0.431 in, which is what 0.430 already was).
* **New parts (existing slots, enough data to model).** Crank: EMPI 69.5. Heads: CB 042 (54 cc, 40 × 35.5), 044 Magnum 44 (1996), VW 041 (39 × 32), Scat Mini-D and Wedge-Port. Cams: Engle W130, W140, TCS-10 turbo (LSA 112). Carbs: 44/48 IDF, DRLA 40, 48 IDA (44 mm venturi sourced p.105), dual 34 ICT, Holley-Weber 32/36. Ignition: Bosch 031, CB Magnaspark (009 curve). Cooling: VW dog-house tin.
* **New model categories (generic, default off).** `conrod` and `rocker` slots with factory placeholders. Keys: `tm.rodj` / `tm.rodw` (mm) in PNH rod-bearing friction, `tm.rod_l` (m) in blowdown kinematics, `tm.rkr` / `tm.rkr0` to scale valve lift vs the ratio the cam was published at (stock 1200 rockers 1.0, later engines 1.1). Parts: 311 rods, Porsche R616 (53 mm), Scat Pro-Moly 311 length; 1.25:1 and 1.4:1 rockers.
* **Still skipped (no usable data, or a special manifold/case the catalogue cannot yet mate).** Intake manifold as its own slot (carb already carries `carbport`); rings, wrist pins, valve covers, coatings, hide-away routing, breathers, serpentine looks, drag buttons; magnetos (need a rigid support, curve not usefully sourced); hydraulic / roller *cams* (no usable timing; hydraulic *lifters* are in as compat); Engle VZ (mixed lift header on p.70); Auto-Craft / Superflo SF-1/SF-2 / Angle-Flo / Autocraft 910 / Scat Split-Port / Pauter Super Pro heads (own manifolds and flanges, no matching carb in the catalogue); Bugpack / GBE / Scat flavour-duplicates of 78/82/84 cranks already listed; Berg 58 and Terminator 62 carbs (flow figures uncertain); Bosch 050 (curve not published); Ignitor conversion; AS21 fuel-pump-less case; Ford 351 mains / Pauter Super Pro case.
* **Compatibility (generic).** One part per slot as before; `tkit` on the Rajay carb kit is unchanged. New `rules.match` for crank journals vs rods (VW 55 mm vs Porsche 53 mm: the 356 and Scat 82 mm cranks need Porsche rods). `rules.limits`: stroke over 84 mm hard-blocks on a stock Type 1 case, over 78 mm warns (Seume p.16); 94 mm bore warns about 10 mm case savers (p.85); high-ratio rockers on a stock cam warn (p.60). `rules.requireWhen`: twin carbs with a vacuum-only distributor warn, they do not hard-block (period kits that leave the factory dizzy stay selectable; Seume p.118). Dog-house cooling headroom 2.2× (estimate). The picker still shows the rule text as the red / amber reason.
* **Prices in the picker.** Every part shows a price line: vendor currency + EUR (FX 26 Nov 2026, 1 EUR = 1.094 USD, 1 EUR = 0.8372 GBP), an `est.` marker when the figure is a nearest-SKU or band estimate, `no price found` when none was sourced, and a period price when one is known (1963 EMPI / Okrasa figures). An optional build total sums EUR (heads ×2 and tyres ×4 when priced each; fuel per litre omitted; a count of unpriced parts is noted). WW’s 69.5 mm crank is on `crank.empi695`; the 044 Special (40 × 35.5) price is on `heads.cb044mag`, not the later CNC head.


## v1.13.30 (tag `v1.13.30`), 2026-11-26 (Beetle parts: turbo lag in the drivetrain, turbo charge physics)

* **Why.** Wim (t16u): the turbo builds came out 20–40 % under the period claims, and turbo lag existed only in the sound. Suspected cause: the sound's ignition retard under boost feeding the torque. Finding: it does not. The game torque comes only from js/parts.js, whose WOT advance already ignores the vacuum unit under boost (27.5° at 4500, 1.8 % below MBT). The retard (about −8° at 10 psi) lived in the sound kernel only. The real causes were in the charge model, in the Rajay spool estimate, and in the claim itself (see below). All new terms are generic and default off (keys on the turbo / blower part).
* **Charge temperature law (key `tsq` 0.5, turbos and the Judson / Shorrock blowers).** The trapped charge now falls as T^-0.5 of the compressor outlet temperature instead of 1/T (a hotter charge picks up less heat from the walls: Heywood 1988 §6.2.2, VE ∝ √T; the same exponent as the DIN 70020 / SAE J1349 inlet-temperature power correction behind the DIN PS figures). Rajay (old spool) 75.0 → 85.7 PS; Schwitzer 67.6 → 76.1; Garrett 70.4 → 78.2; Judson 40.9 → 42.8; Shorrock 46.9 → 48.8.
* **Header ahead of the turbine (key `xpd`).** Back-pressure elements now use the gas density at their own static pressure, walked up from the tail (the turbine adds k_bp × boost). Before, the header ahead of a turbine (about 2 bar) was counted at atmospheric density, which doubled its ½ρv² loss. About +0.5 PS.
* **Compressor rise, not plenum gauge (key `pgc`).** The spool law is now the compressor's pressure rise, scaled so the boost gauge reads the sourced figure at n1 (the carb / filter loss there is added: dpu1, calibrated in tmOf). Below the crossover the plenum shows the carburettor's vacuum, as an NA engine does. Before, the plenum was held at atmospheric pressure with no boost, so the turbo cancelled the carb loss for free below n0 (+3 N·m at 2000 rpm). The extra evaporation under boost can at most take back the compression heat. A plenum below P0 counts as intake loss (the NA 0.5 share).
* **Rajay spool (sourced).** n1 4000 (estimate) → 5400: there is no wastegate (Rajay manual, via ShopTalk 2001), and Hot Rod (Oct 1971) saw "a high of 10 pounds" with shifts at 5400, so the 10 psi is the top of the rev range, not 4000. n0 2000 stays an estimate (a 1641 with the kit "makes boost around 4000", TheSamba 2016). Desc / src now quote the stock-carb figure (+50 %) apart from the carb-kit figure (close to 120 hp).
* **Turbo lag in the drivetrain (keys `lag`, `lag_flow`).** Parts.vehicle now also gives the anchored torque at zero boost and at the wastegate boost (`v.boost`). js/physics.js keeps the boost as a state with the spool law the sound uses (dp_max·x²·throttle², capped at dp_wg) and the sound's own tau_up / tau_dn. Build-up is slower at low exhaust flow (tau_up·n1/rpm, at most 3×; estimate). Torque is interpolated in boost through (0, zero-boost curve), (steady boost, table) and (dp_wg, full), so a settled pull returns the steady table exactly. Time to 90 % of the steady boost: Rajay 3.3 s at 3000, 2.5 s at 4000, 2.2 s at 4500; Garrett 1.9 / 1.6 / 1.6 s.
* **Shared with the sound.** main.js sends the physics boost as ctl `bst`; the kernel's charge block follows it (new Q_CHGX / Q_CHGXB, 30 ms smoothing) instead of its own spool (still used offline). In a 3rd-gear pull the sound boost stays within 0.3–0.5 psi of the drivetrain's, where v1.13.29 showed up to 9.8 psi in the sound with no lag in the drivetrain.
* **No ignition retard under boost (sound).** With a charge block (Q_CHG > 0), the kernel's vacuum term stops at 0 under boost: a vacuum diaphragm at rest cannot retard. This is the torque model's rule. NA sets take the unchanged arithmetic.
* **Dyno (menu figures, 1600 DP, game limiter 4600).** NA 51.2 PS. Rajay 75.0 PS @ 4000 / 132.6 N·m → 67.5 PS @ 4400 / 118.7 N·m (Hot Rod: about +50 % on the stock carb, i.e. ~77 PS; nearly +100 %, close to 120 hp, only with the extra carb / advance kit, which is not in the catalogue). With the physics alone (old spool) it would be 86.3 PS. Schwitzer 67.6 → 75.6 PS (45 → 90 hp claim). Garrett 0.5 bar 70.4 → 78.1 PS (90 PS). CB RAD 75.2 → 85.5 PS (big-bore kit, not comparable). Judson 40.9 → 42.8 PS; Shorrock on the 34 PS 46.9 → 48.8 PS (40 → 72 hp). All NA builds are unchanged.
* **Why still under the claims.** At 10 psi the pressure ratio is 1.68. With a period compressor (η 0.6, estimate) the charge reaches about 360 K even with the draw-through fuel cooling, so the density gain is about 1.5×, not 2×. The Rajay's boost also arrives at 4500–5400, where the 1600 DP's own breathing falls away (stock cam's early inlet closing: VE factor 0.78 at 4600, 0.52 at 5400, the same NA or boosted). A doubling at 10 psi would need an isothermal charge and no extra pumping. The model will not get there by tuning numbers.
* **Rajay carb & advance kit (new part `carb.pict3_rajay`, preset "Rajay turbo kit with the carb & advance kit").** Wim (t23u): the kit was not in the catalogue. Sources: Hot Rod Oct 1971 ("a special kit to open up the carburetor and revise the distributor advance curve", nearly +100 %, close to 120 hp, a Rajay claim) and Hot Rod Feb 1972 (kit carb mods: main jet change, plugged power-enrichment air bleeds, Loctite on the pump nozzle, throttle lever change; stock '71 1600 55 → 119 hp, dyno type and SAE / DIN basis not stated). Venturi 26 → 28 mm and the recurve to 32° total by 3000 rpm (the top of the VW 28–32° WOT window) are estimates; the stock vacuum unit stays. Needs the Rajay turbo (generic attr `tkit`) and the factory distributor slot; dual-port manifold only. Result 69.8 PS @ 4500 / 120.7 N·m (Rajay on the stock carb 67.5 PS; NA 51.2 PS = ×1.36 vs the claimed ×2.16, ~111 PS). Why it stays short: the jet change is what keeps a draw-through carb rich under boost, and the turbo block already assumes boost AFR 11.5 for every kit; what enrichment and a careful curve really buy is knock margin, which the torque model does not have; the gauge is pinned to the sourced 10 psi at 5400; and the 1600's breathing and the 4600 limiter cap the top end (not reworked here). 3rd-gear 2000 → 4400 rpm 9.10 → 8.88 s.
* Stock and all 111 renders bit-identical; parts gate PASS.

## v1.13.29 (tag `v1.13.29`), 2026-11-23 (Beetle parts: high-rpm breathing and friction)

* **Why.** v1.13.28 left the engines that rev short of their factory figures (Type 4 2.0 kit 81 PS @ 4000 vs 100 PS @ 5000; Denzel 1300 47 vs 54; 1300 S 53 vs 65). Wim (t13s18): rework the high-rpm breathing and friction with sourced physics, no per-engine tuning, stock engines keep their claims. All new terms are generic, data-driven and default off (tm keys); the VW long blocks inherit them from the 30 PS block.
* **Friction (tm key `fric`, `fan`).** Rubbing friction from Patton, Nitschke & Heywood (SAE 890836) as revised by Sandoval & Heywood (SAE 2003-01-0725): crank seals, main bearings and oil pumping from the journal sizes, piston skirt, rings, rod bearings and gas loading, the cam-in-block flat-tappet valvetrain (type IV constants) with the cam lift, and auxiliaries. Journal sizes sourced (Type 1 mains 55/55/55/40, rods 55; Type 4 mains 60/60/60/40, rods 55 for the 1.8 and 50 for the 2.0); bearing widths estimated. The fan is an absolute power (0.51 m³/s at 3300 rpm sourced, 800 Pa and efficiency 0.35 estimated) ∝ n³, so it costs a bigger engine less mean pressure; the Type 4 long blocks lose their rescaled-fan quadratic. Barnes-Moss rose to 3.7 bar at 6000 rpm on the 30 PS; PNH + fan give 2.4 bar at 5000. The level factor kf stays 1 (pure PNH).
* **Game friction.** Builds with `fric`: the closed-throttle friction is the shipped one plus (model build − model stock) as torque, fitted as a quadratic in krpm; tyre-only or electrical-only builds keep the shipped friction exactly.
* **Mach index (tm key `ci`).** Taylor's inlet Mach index now uses a flow coefficient derived from the sound kernel's valve law (Cd × the mean of min(π d L, 0.85 π d²/4) over the event / the valve head area, cam lift as the inlet lift, estimate) instead of a constant 0.35: 30 PS 0.39, 1600 0.35, Type 4 2.0 0.37, Denzel 0.32.
* **Late inlet closing on gas speed (tm key `ivz`).** The best inlet closing angle now moves with the inlet Mach index (× 20 m/s, so the 30 PS behaves as before) instead of piston speed: big valves keep a moderate gas speed at high revs, which is what keeps the cylinder filling after BDC. Closing too late now saturates (exp(−a x²)) instead of the parabola's collapse, so hot cams keep a believable low end.
* **Intake runner tuning (tm key `ram`).** Helmholtz tuning (Engelman 1973; Heywood §7.6.2): runner + port inertance (new chain entry 7: length, estimates) against half the cylinder volume, peak at K = 2.1 × piston frequency, +8 % VE with a 25 % width (estimates). Tuned speeds: 30 PS 4400, 1600 4700, twin carbs 5900–6900, Type 4 2.0 5800.
