* **Anchoring (tm key `anc`).** Builds that rev past the stock 3800 rpm limiter are now extended along the model from the last table point at or below it (3700): the shipped 4000 / 4200 points are the game's over-rev run-off, not dyno shape, and pulled every high-revving build down ~10 %.
* **Refit.** `a 4.75e-4, k_ivc 5.7, ksp 4, kc 0.69569, kf 1` on the same anchors (stock 30 PS curve, Okrasa TSV), fit error 0.38 → 0.27.
* **Dyno vs claims (menu figures).** Stock 30.0 PS / 75.5 N·m (unchanged). 34 PS 35.6 (34), 1300 41.6 (40), 1500 45.9 (44), TSV 50.6 @ 4200 (50 @ 4200), 1600 DP 51.2 (50). Type 4 2.0 kit 81.0 → 98.6 PS @ 4900, 159 N·m @ 3700 (100 @ 5000, 157 @ 3500). Type 4 1.8 kit 68.7 → 82.4 PS (76). Denzel 1300 47.4 → 55.7 PS @ 4900 (54 @ 4700). Denzel 1300 S 52.8 → 70.8 PS @ 5500 (65 @ 5400). W110 + Kadron 1600 53 → 69 PS.
* **Stinger tailpipe.** Vintage Speed stinger `tail_d` 45 mm (estimate) → 40 mm, the 1-5/8 in tube of its collector (heritagepartscentre SSP AC251708; ipconlinestore EMPI). About 1 dB quieter; power −0.1 PS.
* **Unchanged.** The stock car, every shipped sound set (111 audio hashes bit-identical) and every part's sound except the stinger builds.

## v1.13.28 (tag `v1.13.28`), 2026-11-23 (Beetle parts: ignition curves, fuel injection and the Type 4 swap)

* **Why.** Until now the distributor changed nothing: every Beetle part used the same advance law in the sound engine and the torque model had no timing term. Phase 3 of the parts plan (Wim, t13s4): modern ignition, period and modern injection, and the Type 4 engine-case swap with its compatibility rules.
* **Generic advance curve (car key `ign`, default off).** `ign: { cent: [[rpm, deg], ...], vac: deg }` on top of `spark0`: static + centrifugal curve (up to 6 points) + vacuum × (1 − p_man / P0). The sound kernel uses it in place of the shared law when present (Q slots 95–109); without it the shared law runs unchanged, so all 111 shipped renders are bit-identical.
* **Spark timing in the torque model (tm key `spk`, default off; every VW long block sets it).** The WOT advance of the composed distributor (the kernel's own law when no curve is set) is compared with MBT: heat released at crank angle θ does work δQ·(1 − (V(θ)/V_bdc)^(γ−1)) (derived, ideal gas), weighted by a Wiebe burn (a 5, m 2; Heywood §9.2) over a burn angle that grows as rpm^(1/3) (estimate) and is set so MBT is 30° BTDC at 3000 rpm for CR 6.6 (VW WOT total-advance window 28–32°, vw-resource.com). The ideal loss is doubled (estimate between MIT 2.61 lecture 9, about 1 % at 5–7° retard, and CRC CM-137-11-1b, 5.1 % at 10°): 0.9 % at 5°, 3.7 % at 10° off MBT. Model MBT: 21° at 1000, 26° at 2000, 30° at 3000, 33° at 4000 rpm. A vacuum unit gives no advance at WOT or under boost (the sound kernel's shared law still retards under boost; unchanged). Refit of the four constants on the same anchors: fit error 0.33 → 0.38.
* **Distributors.** Bosch 010 (0 231 129 010; 36 hp Transporter from 1959): 10° static + 20° centrifugal from about 1200 to 2600 rpm, curved (start, end and amount sourced; curve shape estimate). Bosch 009 (0 231 178 009; 1971): 7° static, 12° at idle to 28° at 2600, linear (123ignition manual example of the 009 curve). 123\\TUNE+ (123ignition, contactless, programmable): curve set to the model's MBT capped at 32° plus a 10° vacuum curve (estimate). 009 / 010 are Type 1 only. On a 1600 dual-port: 010 +2.0 N·m at 2000 rpm, 009 +1.4, 123\\TUNE+ +2.2; peak power within ±0.2 PS (all curves are near MBT at the power peak).
* **Fuel injection (carb slot, carbkind `efi`).** Bosch L-Jetronic of the 1975–79 US Beetle (AJ: 50 PS DIN / 48 hp SAE net @ 4200, CR 7.3): air-flow-meter flap and 40 mm throttle on the factory manifold layout. Foerster Performance FIS2-001 (c. 2023): sequential, throttle body on the stock dual-port manifold. The Dub Shop dual throttle-body EFI (c. 2020): four 40 mm throttles on IDF-style manifolds, MS2 ECU with crank-trigger ignition (its spark map = the 123 curve, estimate; refuses a separate 009 / 010 / 123).
* **Type 4 swap.** VW-Porsche 914 2.0 (GB, 1973: 94 × 71 mm, 1971 cc, CR 8.0, D-Jetronic, 100 PS @ 5000, 157 N·m @ 3500; VW Newsroom, 914 technical data) and 914 1.8 (EC, US 1974–75: 93 × 66 mm, CR 7.3, L-Jetronic, 76 PS @ 4800). 914 distributor curve 4–9° at 1500, 22° at 3000, 27° at 3500 (039 905 205A). Compatibility (hard rules, data only): 12 V; the CSP upright fan conversion (198 230 400); a 200 mm Type 4 flywheel (Kennedy ACC-C20-5100) so it bolts to a 12 V Type 1 bellhousing (1967 or later gearbox; gearboxes now carry a bell attribute); a Type 4 exhaust (new generic match engine exhaust ports vs exhaust flanges; CSP Python Type 4 header 42 mm and silencer); Type 1 cams, cylinders, heads, cranks, distributors and manifolds refused by the case tag. New kit: Type 4 2.0 swap.
* **Dyno (anchored, menu figures; claims in brackets).** 1600 dual-port: Solex 34 PICT-3 49.2 PS (50), L-Jetronic 53.2 (AJ 50 PS DIN at CR 7.3), FIS2-001 53.4 (dealer: +8 whp vs a tuned carb), Dub Shop ITB 59.0. Type 4 2.0 kit 81.0 PS @ 4000, 153 N·m @ 3400 (100 PS @ 5000, 157 N·m @ 3500); 1.8 68.7 PS, 130 N·m (76 PS, 123 N·m). The model falls short above about 4000 rpm (as for the Denzel 1300 S). Validation after the refit: 34 PS 34.7, 1300 40.2, 1500 44.5, Okrasa TS 43.4, Denzel 47.4, Denzel S 52.8.
* **Sound.** Builds with a new distributor fire on its curve (level within 0.1 dB, the pulse timing changes); EFI builds are louder through the intake (no oil-bath cleaner, open throttles); the Type 4 kit is much louder from behind (2.0 litres, header and a compact silencer; silencer data are estimates). The stock car and the 111 shipped renders are bit-identical.

## v1.13.27 (tag `v1.13.27`), 2026-11-23 (Beetle parts: the exhaust side makes torque)

* **Why.** Until now the exhaust barely mattered to torque: one back-pressure term from the mean tailpipe velocity, so headers, the stinger and the heat exchangers changed only the sound, and every exhaust stayed within ±0.5 PS.
* **New generic exhaust block in the parts torque model (key `exm`, default off; every VW long block sets it).** It uses the sound model's own exhaust geometry (primaries, bank junction, head pipe, heat exchanger, inter pipe, muffler chambers, tailpipes, valve and cam data) as the kernel receives it, so a part changes torque and sound from the same numbers. No per-car code.
  * **Back pressure** = Σ K·½ρv² along that chain at the local gas temperature, × a pulsating-flow factor per path (mean square of a half-sine pulse train with the duty set by the exhaust duration and the cylinders sharing the path; derived). K: pipe friction f·L/d (f 0.025), bends 0.4, junctions 0.5, heat exchanger 1.0, Borda–Carnot expansion into a muffler chamber, contraction 0.5(1 − area ratio) out of it, muffler internals 20 × pack, exit 1.0 (sourced forms; values marked estimates). Stock 1956 exhaust: about 5 kPa at 3400 rpm; the stock exhaust on a 1600 about 14 kPa at 4000 rpm.
  * **Gas temperature** along the pipes follows the sound kernel's EGT law (t_prim0 + t_prima × load; tailpipe 0.45 of the rise when warm).
  * **Header tuning:** a VE gain or loss around n_t = 850 × (180 + EVO) / (L + 3) (Smith & Morrison / Vizard rule; L in inches to each area step), with the reflection coefficient of each step from the pipe areas (Benson's constant-pressure junction), scaled by valve overlap (amplitude up to 8 % VE, Heywood's range; estimate).
  * **Residual gas:** Fox, Cheng & Heywood (SAE 931025) from intake and exhaust pressure, overlap factor, rpm and CR.
  * **Exhaust cam timing:** a 0-D blowdown from EVO through the kernel's own valve curtain: early opening loses expansion work, late opening or small valves leave pressure for the exhaust stroke to push out.
* **Refit.** The four torque-model constants were refitted on the same sourced anchors (stock 75.5 N·m @ 2000, 62 N·m @ 3400, Okrasa TSV 50 PS @ 4200); fit error 0.55 → 0.33. Validation: 34 PS 34.8 PS (claim 34), 1300 40.3 (40), 1500 44.6 (44), 1600 dual-port 49.4 (50), Okrasa TS 1200 46.0 (about 45). The stock car is unchanged by construction.
* **Headers get their real collector (generic car key `coll_d`, default off).** Until now a header's collector and head pipes used the stock 32 mm pipe size. A header part can now set its own collector bore; the muffler chambers keep their size (they stay on `pipe_d`). Vintage Speed merged header: 40 mm collector (1-5/8 in merged header, 40 mm ID tubing, stinger the same size; SSP / EMPI listings). EMPI extractor: 44.5 mm collector (small 3-bolt muffler flange, 1-3/4 in ID) and 31.6 mm primaries (1-3/8 in 16-gauge tubing; was 35 mm). The turbo header keeps its estimates (no source found). Sound: the merged header is about 2.5 dB louder on a full-throttle pull from behind (+3.3 dB at 3800 rpm, +2 dB at idle; in the car +0.7 to +2 dB); the EMPI about +1.5 dB from behind under load.
* **Dyno (anchored, menu figures).** Stock 30 PS: heat exchangers 30.0 PS, EMPI header 30.3, merged header + stinger 30.7, Abarth 30.1. Stock 1600 dual-port: heat exchangers 49.4, EMPI 50.6, merged + stinger 51.0 (period and dyno reports: a header gains about 1.4 to 3 hp on a stock 1600). Hot 1600 (dual-port, Kadron, W110, 12 V): heat exchangers 54.0 PS (was 60.6), EMPI 56.9, merged + stinger 56.5, Abarth 54.7; the 1956 heat exchangers and muffler now hold a hot engine back (about 20 kPa at 4600 rpm).
* Stock sound is unchanged: only the EMPI and merged header builds sound different; all 111 reference renders are bit-identical; parts gate PASS.

## v1.13.26 (tag `v1.13.26`), 2026-11-23 (The garage, seen from the drive)

* **New garage view (after the Fast & Furious garage).** "In the garage" now looks from the concrete drive into an open one-car garage at dusk: a whitewashed clapboard front with a lamp over the door, inside a hanging lamp, pegboard and workbench with a stool, a door and tyre shelf at the back, a red tool cart and a jack. The view is closer than a street shot: the door opening and the car fill the screen on the iPhone; a wide screen shows the whole front.
* **Engine end towards you, lid / hood up.** New art for every car, parked about 0.6 m inside the door: the Beetle and the 911 (rear engines) show their tails with the engine lid up; the GT350, Cadillac and Model X show their noses with the hood (frunk) up; the BRM has its bonnet off. The car is drawn at its real width against a 3.4 m door (estimate), in the garage's shade (darker, a warm top light from the lamp, dark low down) with a contact shadow on the floor.
* **Brake lights only when braking.** The garage no longer holds the brake lamps on (the car is still held in place). When you brake, the red light fills the garage: on the floor, door posts and drive in front of a rear-facing car (its lamps face you), on the back and side walls behind a front-facing car.
* **The garage cabin view is gone** (Position menu and the V key); an old link to view 4 plays as the garage.
* **Garage sound from the microphone on the drive (generic `garage` listener, default off).** Only the garage view uses it, so every other position and all 111 reference renders are bit-identical. The microphone stands 4.5 m in front of the door, 1.5 m up (estimates). Direct sound: the open engine end is unshadowed; the tailpipes of a front-engined car, at the far end inside the garage, are behind the body (shadow 0.3, as the rear view's engine shadow). Early reflections: image sources up to order 3 in a 6.0 × 3.6 × 2.4 m room (estimates) whose last leg leaves through the 3.4 × 1.97 m door (front-wall bounces count with its solid share); the 6 strongest per source for the two tailpipes and the intake (pressure reflection factors: back wall 0.89 with clutter, side walls 0.94, ceiling 0.92, concrete floor 0.99; estimates); room paths of a shadowed source get the square root of its shadow (over the body; estimate). Late tail (Web Audio convolver): Sabine RT60 0.44 s from the surfaces, the open door (6.7 m², fully absorbing) and 3 m² of clutter and car (estimate); level from diffuse-field theory (power into the room minus the share that leaves through the door directly, radiated from the door into the half space in front of it), from the first back-wall round trip on. Each car's own length and engine end (sourced) set the geometry. Listening level: the rear fit + 12 dB (estimate, one value; the Model X, whose synth has no geometry, gets the rear fit − 4 dB). Sets without a garage listener (BRM, older models) keep the previous fixed garage room. Cost: 58 instead of 22 kernel paths in the garage view only.
* Art: chroma-key generated masters in the game's ink style (not committed), cut to transparent WebP (29–90 KB each) and a 1280×720 backdrop (ink 113 KB, flat 70 KB). The garage art loads on the first garage visit, so the start-up download is unchanged.

## v1.13.25 (tag `v1.13.25`), 2026-11-22 (Beetle: parts from the 1950s to today, boost and turbos you can hear)

* **Parts catalogue 1950s to today (phase 1).** The Beetle's Swap parts menu grows from 33 to 75 parts in 18 slots (was 15) (47 up to 1969, 15 from the 1970s, 6 from 1980 to 1999, 7 from 2000 to today), plus 9 kits. New: Denzel 1300 and 1300 S engines; VW 1600 dual-port (AD/AE, 50 PS); 78, 82 and 84 mm stroker cranks; 83, 85.5, 90.5, 92 and 94 mm barrels; VW dual-port and CB Performance 044 heads; Engle W100, W120 and W125 cams; Zenith twin-choke, Speedwell twin SU, Solex 34 PICT-3, Kadron, Weber 40 IDF and Dellorto DRLA 36 carburettors; Shorrock vane supercharger; Rajay, Schwitzer (Crane), Garrett and CB Performance turbo kits with a turbo header; Bosch 009 distributor; Vintage Speed merged header with single stinger; Vintage Speed and EMPI mufflers; Kennedy clutch; 1961–65, AC, AS and Freeway Flyer gearbox ratios; Michelin X radial tyres. Specs come from period and maker sources (VW manuals, Denzel, Okrasa, Engle, KEP gearbox chart, CB Performance, Vintage Speed); missing figures are estimates and are marked in the data.
* **Year in every option, era filter.** Each option shows its year; uncertain years are marked (for example "c. 1975?"). A menu at the top filters parts up to 1969 (default), 1979, 1999 or today. A part already chosen stays visible. The period warning now follows the chosen era. Kits show their newest part's year.
* **Compatibility: engine cases and voltage.** The info line shows the engine case (36 hp, 40 hp, doghouse 1600, Type 4) and 6 V / 12 V. Hard rules check them: the long-stroke cranks need the 40 hp or doghouse case, and the 1600 needs 12 V. Heads and carburettor manifolds must match their port type (single-port, VW dual-port, Okrasa dual-port) through one generic attribute rule. Turbos need the turbo header.
* **Boost you can hear (phase 2, default off).** A new generic charge block lifts the atmospheric manifold-pressure cap only when a part sets it. Positive-displacement blowers build boost with engine speed and throttle; turbos build it as ((rpm − n0) / (n1 − n0))² × throttle², with a wastegate limit and spool lag (about 0.8 s up, 0.3 s down; estimates). The Judson now reaches about 4.9 psi and about 4 dB more at full throttle (rear, 3600 rpm); the Shorrock about 5.6 psi.
* **Turbo block.** Compressor whine at blade-pass frequency from the impeller tip speed needed for the boost (6 blades, 55 mm wheel; estimates). The turbine sits in the exhaust as a volute plus a narrow, lossy wheel passage (21 mm, estimate), which damps 150–1500 Hz by about 3–6 dB.
* **Boost makes torque: charge model (generic key `chg`, forced-induction parts only).** Torque follows the trapped charge: charge density = manifold pressure / (R × charge temperature), with compressor heating from the compressor efficiency, an optional intercooler (`ic_eff`, none of the period kits has one) and extra fuel evaporation under boost (`evap_x` 0.3, estimate). Fuel follows the air at the boost mixture (`afr` 11.5, richer than the NA 12.5; estimate), through a fuel-air-cycle efficiency curve (estimate fit to Heywood's shape), so the richer mixture cools the charge but adds no free power. The wastegate holds the plenum at the set boost, so the carburettor ahead of a draw-through compressor costs compressor work instead of charge pressure. Pumping uses the real pressures: boost above the exhaust back pressure gives pumping work back, the turbine's back pressure (1.2 × boost, estimate) takes it away, and the Judson and Shorrock pay their belt drive power. Builds without the key, all naturally aspirated builds and the stock car are unchanged. The anchored curve has no ceiling at the stock torque: the build curve is the stock table × model(build) / model(stock) up to 5×, out to the build's own rev limit.
* **Dyno (model vs period claim; claims are mostly SAE gross).** Rajay on the 1600: 79.6 PS @ 4000, 140 N·m (was 69.4 PS; claim 115–120 hp). Garrett 0.5 bar on the 1600: 73.6 PS (was 65.2; claim 90 PS). Schwitzer on the 1300: 54.5 PS @ 7.1 psi (was 47.9; claim 90 hp at 12 psi @ 5000, above the 1300's rev limit). Judson: 42.1 PS, 99 N·m (was 40.4; claim 48 PS @ 4000, above the 30 PS rev limit). Shorrock on the 1200: 47.0 PS (was 45.1; claim 72 hp gross). Denzel 1300 S unchanged at 56.0 PS (claim 65 PS). The remaining turbo gap comes from the stock engines' breathing above 4000 rpm (cam, valves, the factory manifolds kept by the kits) and friction; a higher rev limit adds nothing in the model. Not fudged to hit the claims.
* **Single tailpipe.** The 2-into-1 shared exhaust can end in one tail (`tail_n: 1`), used by the Vintage Speed merged header with its stinger. It is quieter at idle and much louder at full throttle (about +16 dB A, rear, 3600 rpm).
* **Clutch, gearbox and tyres on the vehicle.** New slots change gear ratios, final drive, clutch capacity, tyre radius, grip and rolling resistance through existing vehicle keys (radial tyre grip and rolling resistance are estimates).
* Not in this version (phase 3): Type 4 engine swap, Bosch L-Jetronic 1600i, modern fuel injection and ignition presets.
* Stock builds send nothing new: all 111 reference renders are bit-identical, the other cars are unchanged, and the parts gate passes.

## v1.13.24 (tag `v1.13.24`), 2026-11-22 (Swap parts menu fits the iPhone screen; Apply always visible)

* **What was wrong.** On touch screens (iPhone, Tesla) the Swap parts list could not be scrolled with a finger: the game blocks finger drags on the stage (for steering and pedals), and only the About and Specs boxes were exempt. The Apply and Stock buttons sat at the bottom of the list, below the visible area (on an iPhone in portrait about 300 px below the screen edge), so they could not be reached.
* **Fix.** The panel now fits the visible screen (Safari's toolbars, notch and home bar included: `dvh`/`svh`, safe-area insets). The header stays at the top and the footer with **Apply · Stock · Close** at the bottom, always visible. Only the list of parts scrolls in between, and it scrolls by finger.
* **Apply is obvious.** The footer says "N changes – press Apply" and the Apply button turns green while the menu differs from the running build ("Running this build" otherwise). Apply stays explicit, so a half-chosen build never restarts the engine sound.
* **Phones.** In portrait, labels sit above the menus and the menus use 16 px text (no Safari zoom). Short screens (landscape phone) get a compact header and rows. The panel runs from the top edge. The descriptions no longer repeat the part name, so the list is shorter. The parts' native pickers are the phone's or the browser's own popups and are not clipped.
* Checked in WebKit at 390×844 and 390×664 (portrait, Safari toolbars) and 844×390 and 750×342 (landscape), and in Chromium with a Tesla user agent at 1920×1200 and 1200×900. Every slot and the Apply button can be reached, and Apply by tap applies the Okrasa TSV kit. Sound and the stock car are unchanged: 111 of 111 renders are bit-identical.

## v1.13.23 (tag `v1.13.23`), 2026-11-22 (Beetle: part swaps you can hear)

* **Why v1.13.22 swaps sounded the same.** The parts reached the sound engine, but only as small geometry changes: engine swaps moved the level by about 1 dB at full throttle and made idle 1 to 3 dB *quieter* (the idle air bleed was not scaled with the bigger engine), twin carbs changed nothing you hear, and the Judson had no sound at all. Only the Abarth and EMPI changed the sound, and only around some engine speeds.
* **Idle air scales with displacement.** For a non-stock build the idle air bleed is the sound set's own value × swept volume / stock (the idle screw is reset for the same idle speed), so bigger engines no longer idle quieter. A generic `spec.parts.scale` multiplies the selected sound set's own value, so it works with every Beetle set in the drop-down.
* **Valve sizes from the VW data.** 1200 34 PS 31.5/30 mm, 1300 33/30 mm, 1500 35.5/32 mm (VW manual, flat4.de). The 1500 is about 1.5 dB louder and darker at 2500 rpm full throttle.
* **Okrasa twin carbs breathe through two small mesh filters instead of the oil-bath cleaner** (insertion loss 10 dB to 1 dB, estimate). The intake is about 2 dB louder in the cabin at 2500 rpm, about 6 dB louder at 4000 rpm, and about 6 dB louder at part throttle.
* **Abarth 4-tip exhaust:** the four 28 mm tips are now in the sound as well (two per side, as one pipe of the same area). It is about 11 dB louder around 2500 rpm full throttle and about 1 to 3 dB louder at 4000 rpm.
* **Judson vane supercharger whine.** A new generic positive-displacement blower block (default off) reuses the rotor source of the cooling fan. The whine is at 4 vanes × 1.3 × crank speed (about 350 Hz at 4000 rpm). The acoustic power is 1e-4 of the gas power (estimate), with boost building with throttle. The blower's own tone is about 33 to 42 dB above the stock spectrum at full throttle.
* Stock builds send nothing new: all 111 reference renders are bit-identical; parts gate PASS.

## v1.13.22 (tag `v1.13.22`), 2026-11-22 (Beetle: swap 1960s parts in the engine bay)

* **Interchangeable parts, starting with the 1956 Beetle.** The car is now described as parts in module slots: engine (long block), crankshaft, cylinders/pistons, cylinder heads, camshaft, carburettor/manifold, forced induction, distributor, exhaust manifold, muffler, oil/cooling, electrics/starter and fuel. The stock parts reproduce the previous Beetle sound data and vehicle data exactly; a build gate (`tools/parts/check-parts.mjs`) proves it.
* **Swap parts menu, engine bay only.** A *Swap parts* button appears only in the Beetle's engine bay view (the bay's own *Parts* button still shows the parameters). Parts are named by brand and product type: VW 1200 34 PS (D-series), VW 1300 40 PS (F-series) and VW 1500 44 PS (H-series) engines; Okrasa TS 1200 and TSV 1300 kits; Okrasa 69.5 mm stroker crankshaft, dual-port cylinder heads, twin Solex 32 PBIC carburettors, extra oil cooler and Fram filter; Porsche 356 74 mm crankshaft; Judson vane supercharger; Engle W110 camshaft; Bosch 010 centrifugal distributor; EMPI extractor header; Abarth 4-tip exhaust; VW 12 V conversion; generic or modern big-bore barrels marked as such. Stock items are named as the factory VW, Solex or Bosch part.
* **Compatibility rules as data.** Hard rules block Apply (for example Okrasa heads need the Okrasa twin carbs, the Judson draws through the single stock carb, a 12 V 1500 needs the 12 V conversion, the Abarth muffler does not fit the EMPI extractor). Soft rules warn: knock (effective compression vs fuel octane, from VW's own CR/octane pairs), cooling headroom, the stock 36 hp crank, clutch capacity, period (outside the 1960s), and parts that add no gain with the rest of the build.
* **Torque from a lean physics model.** Flow chain (venturi, manifold, ports), cam ram term, valve Mach index, positive-displacement blower, and the merged friction. Constants fitted once on the sourced stock figures and the Okrasa TSV rating. Every build's curve is the shipped stock curve × model(build) / model(stock), so the stock car is unchanged. Check: Okrasa TS 1200 46 PS (period about 45); Judson about 5 psi at peak revs.
* **One friction model for the Beetle (game).** Barnes-Moss FMEP correlation 0.97 + 0.15k + 0.05k² bar (k = krpm), plus the cooling fan (Q 0.51 m³/s @ 3300 sourced; Δp and efficiency estimated), giving 9.20 + 1.42k + 0.78k² N·m (estimate). Full-throttle acceleration and top speed are unchanged (0–80 km/h 17.6 s, 110 km/h). There is less engine braking (coast 100→60 km/h in 4th 16.8 s vs 15.3 s). Generic `fmep` vehicle key; other cars keep their N·m friction.
* **Sound.** Parts builds pass only the changed geometry to the shared v12 kernel (`spec.parts`, default off). No new sound set, no scoring. Sound v21 · Beetle (default) and Sound v24 · Beetle, and every other set, stay bit-identical (111 of 111 renders). The Judson has no sound block yet.

## v1.13.21 (tag `v1.13.21`), 2026-11-22 (Model X visuals redrawn to official 2021+ pictures)

* **Model X redrawn in the same illustrated technique as the Cadillac, 997 and Beetle.** The first Model X ship used simple filled polygons, which read as flat 2D drawings. This version uses the same green-screen illustrated path as the other cars (smooth shading, highlights, accurate curvature; ink adds even outlines). Official 2021+ refresh direction: two wraparound rear lamps with dark TESLA trim between them (not a full-width light bar), a single raked glass hatch, spoiler lip, wide hips, near-flush 20-inch wheels, matte-black wrap with low-sheen shading. Falcon-wing doors stay **closed** in the garage.
* **Cabin and yoke.** Cream seats, lower dash and door cards; dark upper dash and headliner; walnut strip; upright 17-inch centre screen and a thin cluster visor. The steering control is a separate rotating sprite of the official 2021+ Tesla yoke: no top rim, open top, a flat bottom bar, two upright side grips with rounded tops, a large inverted-trapezoid TESLA hub, and scroll wheels on the spokes — not a closed round wheel.
* **Specs dialog.** The Model X Specs page now has an EV path (motors, sourced 7.56 / 9.04 reductions, chassis, tyre/wind sound) so it opens. It no longer assumes an ICE torque curve.
* **Unchanged.** Sound v1 · Model X remains tyre/road and wind only. Every older set stays bit-identical. Cadillac, GT350, 911, BRM and Beetle are untouched.

## v1.13.20 (tag `v1.13.20`), 2026-11-22 (2023 Model X Long Range Dual Motor)

* **Sixth car: 2023 Tesla Model X Long Range Dual Motor.** Matt-black wrap, Cream interior, yoke, drawn through the existing ink and flat paths (no per-car shader). Garage uses the falcon-wing rear; the EV bay is the generic motor / reduction / inverter / frunk / floor-pack drawing (ICE parts off). Official proportions: length 5,057 mm, width 1,999 mm, height 1,680 mm, wheelbase 2,965 mm, rear track 1,710 mm.
* **Sound v1 · Model X is tyre/road and wind only.** The shared sound model still has the generic EV blocks (PMSM orders, gear mesh, PWM, AVAS, regen sign), but this car's k_mot, k_mesh, k_pwm and k_avas are 0, so you hear the existing CNOSSOS-EU tyre/road and wind block. Combustion and waveguides are not built for an EV. One Model X picker entry (no extra URL). Older sets stay bit-identical.

## v1.13.19 (tag `v1.13.19`), 2026-11-22 (Sound v24 · Beetle: confirmed-ref hot idle)

* **Model X gear UI.** Rev-match is hidden (same as the automatic). The garage flash reads “In the garage — start the car.” The shift buttons cycle P → R → N → D.
* **Sound v24 · Beetle** is Sound v21 (same engine, 2-1-shared exhaust, heat exchangers, cooling fan, and the 0.40 m muffler gas-path ESTIMATE) with one physical change: the **hot idle** is **750 rpm** (ESTIMATE, mid of the confirmed 1956 cabin landings at about 650–800 rpm). `a_idle` is re-sized in proportion (2.47e-5, ESTIMATE). The 1,550 rpm rear cold-start in the Griots clip is choke fast idle and is not the hot target. The muffler can's sourced transverse width 0.679 m is still only the shell drawing; it is not the gas-path length (an earlier unshipped trial that used 0.679 m as L was about 5–6 dB too quiet). Nothing is fitted to the scorecard. It is picker-only, right after Sound v21 · Beetle on the Beetle's one drop-down. Sound v21 · Beetle stays the default. There is no `?sound=v24` or `?beetle=v24` alias and no new URL. Generic parameters only (`idle_rpm` on a set, and `idleRpmBySet` on the vehicle); no Beetle-only code path.
* **Official scorecard, scorer v2.4** (cabin primary; combined = pooled + 0.25×ring; lower is closer). Confirmed 1956 refs (11 cabin windows; phXM and clipped dlD excluded; 0eq only to 20:31). Floor (cabin recording vs recording) combined 1.628. Headline cabin: v24 4.371, v21 4.348. Bootstrap v24 − v21 +0.023 (95% −0.010…+0.055), well inside the ~0.3 noise band — effectively tied. Cabin idle is slightly closer on v24 (4.141 vs 4.252) but that move is also under 0.3. Nothing was fitted to the scorecard.
* **CPU.** No new waveguides or blocks; the cost is the same as Sound v21 · Beetle. Still reasonable for iPhone and the Tesla browser.
* **Unchanged.** Every older sound, including Sound v21 · Beetle, stays bit-identical. Cadillac, GT350, 911 and BRM are untouched.

## v1.13.18 (tag `v1.13.18`), 2026-11-21 (Sound v23 · Cadillac: v22 with a thin packing layer against the resonator shell)

* **Sound v23 · Cadillac** is Sound v22 (same engine, pipes, 21158 muffler, gas cooling, outside path, noise block, 4 s acoustic-temperature lag, Neutral inertia 0.43 kg·m², and packSigma 2,500) plus a thin packing layer on the Walker 21159 resonator. Nothing is fitted to the scorecard. It is picker-only, right after Sound v22 · Cadillac on Cadillac's one drop-down. p1 stays the default. There is no `?caddy=v23` or `?sound=v23` alias and no new URL. Generic parameters only; no Cadillac-only code. Sound v22 stays in the picker.
* **Packing-layer thickness (new generic TMM field `packThick`).** Optional lining thickness in metres, trailing after `packSigma` on `perf_through` / `perf_through3d`. Missing or ≤ 0 = fill the whole annulus when packSigma is set (current v22 behaviour, bit-identical). A positive value is a fibrous lining against the outer shell only; the rest of the annulus is empty gas. If the layer reaches the perforated tube, it is treated as a full fill. The 3-D path (what v22 and v23 use) shrinks the chamber to the remaining empty gap and applies a rigid-backed Delany & Bazley wall impedance. v23 uses **packThick = 3 mm, ESTIMATE**. No 1950s stock-resonator (Walker 21159) cutaway thickness was found; period glasspacks fill the whole annulus. 3 mm was chosen after a 3 / 6 / 10 mm comparison (no wider grid).
* **Official scorecard, scorer v2.4** (cabin+tail, combined = pooled + 0.25×ring, lower is closer). Floor unchanged: combined 2.269 (211 windows). Practical noise band about 0.3.
  * Combined: v23 3.809, v22 4.026, v21 3.528. Bootstrap v23 − v21 +0.280 (95 % +0.141…+0.438): still worse than v21, at the edge of the ~0.3 noise band, because part-throttle load remains somewhat darker (cabin tilt_lo −7.51 vs v21 −5.29 vs v22 −9.83). v23 − v22 −0.217 (95 % −0.332…−0.054): clearly less dark than the full-annulus fill.
  * Ring and closed-throttle coast-down stay clearly better than v21 (ring 3.54 vs v21 4.30 vs v22 2.52).
* Every older sound, including Sound v22 · Cadillac, stays bit-identical. The Beetle drop-down remains one entry, Sound v21 · Beetle.

## v1.13.17 (tag `v1.13.17`), 2026-11-21 (Sound v22 · Cadillac: sourced Neutral inertia and a packed Walker 21159 resonator)

* **Sound v22 · Cadillac** is Sound v21 (same engine, pipes, 21158 muffler, gas cooling, outside path, noise block, and the 4 s acoustic-temperature lag) plus two sourced physical changes. Nothing is fitted to the scorecard. It is picker-only, right after Sound v21 · Cadillac on Cadillac's one drop-down. p1 stays the default. There is no `?caddy=v22` or `?sound=v22` alias and no new URL. Generic parameters only; no Cadillac-only code. Sound v21 stays in the picker.
* **Rotating mass (set key `engineInertia`).** Used only by v22; older sets keep the vehicle default 0.45 kg·m². In Neutral, the 1959 390 + Controlled Coupling Hydra-Matic has a sourced mid estimate of **0.43 kg·m²** (band about 0.32–0.58; the previous 0.45 sits in that band). Always at engine speed: crank, rod rotating ends, balancer, flexplate, main torus cover, front-unit annulus, empty controlled-coupling impeller, front oil pump, belt accessories. Referred through the 1.55 front-unit ratio: main impeller + oil, and the front carrier. The main turbine, rear unit, output and wheels are not locked to the crank. Most component inertias are estimates from published masses and typical radii; the torus cover is the dominant uncertainty.
* **Resonator packing (new generic TMM field `packSigma`).** Optional flow resistivity (Pa·s/m²) on `perf_through` / `perf_through3d`, trailing after `rev`. Missing or ≤ 0 = empty annulus, current behaviour, bit-identical. The packed annulus uses the Delany & Bazley 1970 equivalent-fluid model. v22 uses **σ = 2,500 Pa·s/m², ESTIMATE**, filling the Walker 21159 annulus. That value sits at or just below the light edge of typical 1950s automotive glass-fibre packing (OSU/Selamet-type papers report about 4,900 at 100 g/L; typical band about 5,000–25,000), and is still plausible for a sparse wrap or a steel-wool lining.
* **Official scorecard, scorer v2.4** (cabin+tail, combined = pooled + 0.25×ring, lower is closer). Floor unchanged: combined 2.269 (211 windows). Practical noise band about 0.3.
  * Combined: v22 4.026, v21 3.528, v20 3.316, p1 3.591. Bootstrap v22 − v21 +0.498 (95 % +0.330…+0.622): v22 is clearly worse than v21 on the official combined yardstick, because part-throttle load goes darker (cabin tilt_lo −9.83 vs v21 −5.29).
  * Ring and closed-throttle coast-down are much better (ring 2.52 vs v21 4.30; mid/high shut cells improve).
* Every older sound stays bit-identical. The Beetle drop-down remains one entry, Sound v21 · Beetle.

## v1.13.16 (tag `v1.13.16`), 2026-11-21 (Beetle engine-bay view matches the stock 36 hp Type 1 layout)

* **Beetle engine bay.** The 3D engine view was drawn as a generic V8: long tubular headers the wrong way (0.75 m toward the transaxle), an axial water-pump fan, a side-mounted generator, and a four-barrel carb with a huge round cleaner. The 4-cyl boxer crankcase was already correct. The bay now follows the stock 1956 36 hp (30 PS, 1192 cc) Type 1 layout: upright dynamo and a shrouded centrifugal fan at the pulley end, a single Solex 28 PCI on the central single-port manifold with an oil-bath air cleaner, distributor and oil filler on the crankcase, heat exchangers along each bank, and a transverse 2-tip muffler at the rear apron. Layout from period 36 hp / 30 PS bay photographs (Wikimedia: 1953 VW 1200 engine, CC0; 1959 30 PS engine, CC BY-SA 3.0). Private reference photos are not in the repo.
* **Generic drawing, no per-car path.** Headers of type `2-1-shared` draw short primaries into heat-exchanger jackets, then a shared transverse can and two tails, using the sound-model lengths. `P.fan.type === 'centrifugal'` draws the upright dynamo and shrouded impeller instead of the V8 axial fan and side generator. A `1×1` carb with `cleaner: 'oil-bath'` draws a single downdraft and a small oil-bath pot. A flat engine with a centrifugal fan gets transaxle, belt, distributor, oil filler and a light rear-apron opening. The GT350, Cadillac, BRM and 911 views are unchanged.
* **Muffler can width (visual only).** A sourced figure of **0.679 m** (26.75 in, VW logo NOS; Dansk 26 in / earlier 27 in; [TheSamba t=485463](https://www.thesamba.com/vw/forum/viewtopic.php?t=485463)) is the **transverse width** of the OE 111 251 051 G can, tip to tip, not the streamwise gas path. The bay draws the shell at that width (`P.headers.mufWidth`, visual only). Sound v21 is unchanged: the kernel still uses the **0.40 m estimate** of the internal path (`muf` chamber length). Putting the 0.68 m width into that sound-model slot would overstate low-frequency transmission loss (a longer inline expansion chamber). Heater-box length, pipe ID, packing and tail length remain estimates. There is no Sound v22 on this release.
* Every older sound stays bit-identical. The Beetle sound drop-down remains one entry, **Sound v21 · Beetle** (default). No new URL.

## v1.13.15 (tag `v1.13.15`), 2026-11-21 (UI hotfix: one sound drop-down; Beetle cabin and rear-wheel fixes; Sound v21 · Cadillac coast-down lag)

* **UI fix:** the site again has **exactly one sound drop-down** on every car. v1.13.14 added the Beetle picker (`#segBeetleSound`, class `sel brmsel`) and correctly toggled the `hidden` class, but never added a CSS rule to hide it. `.sel { display: inline-flex }` therefore kept the Beetle list visible on the GT350, Cadillac, BRM and 911 — the same class of bug as v1.12.8 (then it was the Cadillac picker).
* **Fix:** add a generic rule `.sel.brmsel.hidden, #segSound.hidden { display: none; }`, and keep the existing specific rules for `#segBrmSound`, `#segP997Sound`, `#segCaddySound` and `#segSound`. Any future per-car picker that uses `sel brmsel` will hide correctly.
* **Cadillac picker title:** the `#segCaddySound` `title` attribute was stale (it still stopped at Sound v14). It now lists the full history: Sound v1–v4, v12 · Cadillac p1–p10, Sound v13, v13.1, v13.2, v13.4, v14, v14.1, v15, v16, v17, v19, v20 and v21; p1 stays the default; p7 and everything after it is picker-only; v18 is not on the site.
* **Beetle cabin view.** The interior is redrawn from real 1953–57 oval-window driver’s-seat and dash photographs (private references, not in the repo). The view keeps the game’s HUD framing (canvas 2560×1440, same hub and wheel sprite). The front lid is omitted for now: the windscreen glass is transparent down to the cowl and wiper line, with no hood hump or fender hints in the opening. The wipers, the interior mirror and the visors stay. The dash is the factory oval layout: a flat body-colour panel, one speedometer slightly left of centre, a small clock, one central speaker grille, glovebox and passenger grab handle. A period accessory sits on the right-of-centre dash, as in the 1957 Ovali photograph: a small white porcelain bud vase with a blue floral motif and red roses, clipped at the right edge of the speaker grille. The extra left-hand grille and the painted-in steering-wheel hub are gone (the game overlays the wheel sprite). Paint stays L240 Agave, mid-tone **#979A84** (highlight **#A5A893**, shadow **#7E816C**), sampled from the owner photo (private), mid-tone panels.
* **Beetle rear wheels.** In the outside (rear) view the chrome hubcaps with VW badges were drawn face-on, as if the wheel faces were visible from directly behind. Hubcaps face sideways and cannot be seen from that camera, so the badges are removed; only the tyres’ tread and sidewall remain. The rest of the rear art is unchanged.
* **Sound v21 · Cadillac** is Sound v20 plus a generic set key `t_ac` = 4 s: after each pipe’s acoustic temperature is chosen (including `t_axial`), that T is first-order lagged and used only for waveguide delay / c. Motive: gas travel and wall thermal mass, so pipe delay must not follow cycle-resolved gas temperature every 128-sample block, especially at closed throttle when mass flow is small (the leftover ~310–330 Hz coast-down whistle on v20). Unset `t_ac` is the current behaviour, so every older set stays bit-identical. It is picker-only, right after Sound v20 · Cadillac on Cadillac’s one drop-down. **p1 stays the default.** There is no `?caddy=v21` or `?sound=v21` alias and no new URL. Generic parameters only; no Cadillac-only code. Sound v20 stays in the picker.
* **Test.** A repeatable headless check now walks every car via the Car menu and keys 1–5, every Position view and both Visuals modes, including after switching away and back: exactly one visible sound `<select>` (computed display not `none`, nonzero size), the full chronological list for that car (Cadillac now ends v19, v20, v21), the right default (GT350 p1, Cadillac p1, BRM p11, 911 p7, Beetle v21), no Beetle entry in the other lists, and that changing the picker switches the sound model. It fails on live v1.13.14 (the Beetle picker stays visible on the other cars) and passes on this build.
* **Unchanged.** Every older sound set stays bit-identical to v1.13.14. A renderer `acc -= 128` bug found in an offline coast harness is not in the game’s AudioWorklet loop (it already processes 128-sample quanta) and is not included.

## v1.13.14 (tag `v1.13.14`), 2026-11-21 (1956 VW Beetle Type 1: Sound v21, L240 Agave art, and a fast-idle fix)

* **1956 VW Beetle Type 1** is now in the game, next to the GT350, Cadillac, BRM and 911. Key `5` selects it. One sound drop-down, one entry: **Sound v21 · Beetle**. No additional URL alias (`?beetle=` was not added; the existing generic `?car=beetle` still works).
* **Sound v21 · Beetle** is the v20 kernel plus new generic blocks that any car can use: an engine-driven centrifugal cooling fan, a heat-exchanger muffler item, and a boxer `2-1-shared` exhaust network. There is no Beetle-only sound path and no fitted equaliser. Missing specs are marked ESTIMATE.
* **Art.** Rear and cabin views are in the same Flat (v4) and Ink (v5) styles as the other cars, with period 1956 oval-window details (split decklid louvres, small round tail lamps, chrome bumper with overriders, plate light, chrome hubcaps, blackwall tyres). Body colour is L240 Agave, game mid-tone **#979A84** (highlight **#A5A893**, shadow **#7E816C**), sampled from the owner photo (private), mid-tone panels (median of the 30–70th percentile luminance, overcast daylight). The photo is not in the repository.
* **Fast-idle fix.** After starter handoff, the HUD no longer holds the choke fast-idle override at the flare rpm. The game now requires the starter not to have handed off before applying that override, so the idle settles to the configured idle rpm. This is game-side wiring only; every sound set is unchanged.
* **Info page.** The 1956 Beetle is listed; the Beetle uses sound model v21; the drop-down inventory includes Sound v21 · Beetle. Next work is a Cadillac coast-down fix, then rotating mass and resonator packing, then a Tesla Model X.
* **Unchanged.** Every older sound set stays bit-identical. GT350, Cadillac, BRM and 911 sounds are untouched.

## v1.13.13 (tag `v1.13.13`), 2026-11-21 (Sound v20 · Cadillac: v19 with the Walker 21159 resonator as a perforated straight-through can)

* **Sound v20 · Cadillac** is Sound v19 (same engine, pipes, 21158 muffler, gas cooling, outside path and noise block) except the Walker 21159 resonator is no longer a plain expansion chamber. It uses the existing generic `perf_through3d` transfer-matrix block: a straight-through perforated can, with the same sourced shell, gas path and idle gas state as in v14.1 / v15 (519.6 K, M=0.0087, rev 4). The 21158 muffler stays the v16 packed long chamber (p9 pack). Nothing is fitted to the scorecard. It is picker-only, right after Sound v19 · Cadillac on Cadillac's one drop-down. p1 stays the default. There is no `?caddy=v20` or `?sound=v20` alias and no new URL. Generic parameters only; no Cadillac-only code. Sound v19 stays in the picker.
* **Why.** Two similar-length plain chambers (v16 onward) plus the 0.5 m tail share a pass near c/(2L) ≈ 400 Hz. Plane-wave expansion-chamber transmission loss (NACA 1192 / Munjal) is zero at kL=π, and that pass made closed-throttle cabin sound too bright (2jGo lift tilt −2.82 vs the recording −10.66). Walker 21159 is a Direct-Flow (straight-through perforated) resonator, which fills the plane-wave pass. A wholesale return to v15 TMM on both cans over-darkens part, idle and full much more, so only the resonator is changed.
* **Official scorecard, scorer v2.4** (cabin+tail, combined = pooled + 0.25×ring, lower is closer). Floor unchanged: pooled 1.835, ring 1.74, combined 2.269 (211 windows).
  * Combined: v20 3.316, v19 2.950, v17 2.959, p1 3.591. Bootstrap v20 − v19 +0.366 (95 % +0.303…+0.435): v20 is clearly worse than v19 on the official combined yardstick, above the ~0.3 noise band, because part-throttle, full load and idle go darker.
  * Closed-throttle mid-rev overrun is clearly better (mid shut combined 2.742 vs v19 3.228; cabin tilt −9.18 vs the recording −9.75, where v19 was −3.68). The 2jGo lift tilt is −9.78 against the recording −10.66 (v19 −2.82).
  * This is a real tradeoff, not noise: the shut brightness target moves the right way; load and idle darkening dominates the headline.
* **CPU.** Same class as v19. A perforated TMM resonator replaces one plain chamber (24 pipes vs 26). Official full v2.4 render was 28.5 % realtime; short listen jobs were 21.4 % vs v19 21.0 %. On the release tree, node, 48 kHz, 2000 rpm / 0.4, best of 3: v19 184.6 / 189.2 / 188.7 ms per audio second in the cabin / at the tail / outside far; v20 190.2 / 195.0 / 195.6 (ratio 1.03). Reasonable for iPhone and Tesla.
* **Info page.** How the sound model works now says v20 is live, v19 stays in the picker, v18 is shelved, p1 stays the default, scoring is v2.4, and the next work is the engine and exhaust with swappable mufflers.
* **Unchanged.** Every older entry, including Sound v19 · Cadillac, stays bit-identical. BRM, GT350 and 911 are untouched.

## v1.13.12 (tag `v1.13.12`), 2026-11-21 (Sound v19 · Cadillac: the outside path and a tyre and wind noise block)

* **Sound v19 · Cadillac** is Sound v17 (same engine, pipes, cans, gas cooling and listener) plus two generic physical blocks. Nothing is fitted to the scorecard. It is picker-only, right after Sound v17 · Cadillac on Cadillac's one drop-down. p1 stays the default. There is no `?caddy=v19` or `?sound=v19` alias and no new URL. Generic parameters only; no Cadillac-only code.
* **Outside path (new generic set key `out_path`).** Used only by v19; older sets are unchanged.
  * Pipe-outlet directivity: Levine & Schwinger (1948) / Ando (1968).
  * Ground reflection: Delany & Bazley (1970) impedance, Nord2000 class G dense asphalt / concrete, σ = 20 000 kPa s/m² (Plovsing 2006; the surface in the recordings is unknown: estimate), plus a coherence factor for the ground image from the uncertainty of a hand-held microphone (height sd 0.3 m, distance sd 0.5 m: estimates). The reflection filter is fitted in magnitude and then has its low-frequency group delay removed, so the ground image keeps the right phase.
  * Air absorption: ISO 9613-1, 20 °C, 70 % RH (RH is an estimate).
* **Tyre and wind noise block (new generic set key `noise`).** Used only by v19; older sets are unchanged. When a set has its own noise, the old mixer road roar is switched off (skid stays in the mixer). Scoring renders keep `NON_ENGINE_NOISE=0` and `MIXER_WIND=0`.
  * Rolling noise: CNOSSOS-EU 2015/996 category 1, with tyre width. 8.20-15 bias-ply, section about 208 mm, rim 15 in, overall about 0.72 m, cavity mean diameter about 0.55 m (tyre size tables: estimate). Wheelbase 130 in = 3.30 m (AMA 1959).
  * Tyre-cavity tone: c/(π d_mean) (Sakata et al. 1990); peak +6 dB re 250 Hz (estimate).
  * Wind: 65 dB(A) at 100 km/h, with speed law v^5.5 (George 1990, SAE 900315, exponent range 5–6; anchor estimate).
  * Cabin insulation: mass-law floor, 9 kg/m² (0.9 mm steel + trim: estimate). The block follows the game's road speed.
* **Scorecard, scorer v2.3** (cleaned throttle-off labels, idle in its own row, lower is closer):
  * Combined: v19 2.940, v17 2.949, p1 3.584, recordings floor 2.274. v19 and v17 are effectively tied (0.009 apart, well inside the 0.3 noise level).
  * Pooled: v19 2.103, v17 2.110, p1 2.300. Ring: v19 3.35 dB, v17 3.35, p1 5.14.
  * Cabin is identical in v19 and v17 (pooled 2.057, ring 3.32). The small gap is at the tail (v19 2.924 vs v17 3.048).
  * On the earlier scorer v2, which still scored the outside path: combined v19 3.659 vs v17 3.715; outside far 4.97 vs 5.15; tail 2.65 vs 2.72 (Δ −0.056, bootstrap 95 % −0.075…−0.038), also well under 0.3.
* **Not shipped.** v18 (a fitted per-position output EQ and ring notches) was tried and then shelved on 21 November 2026, and was never pushed, because a correction fitted to the stock exhaust would still be applied after an aftermarket muffler or pipe swap.
* **CPU.** Same 26 waveguides as v17. Node, 48 kHz, 2000 rpm / 0.4, best of 3, on the release tree: v17 188.8 / 194.5 / 187.8 ms per audio second in the cabin / at the tail / outside far; v19 187.1 / 195.1 / 190.0. v19 with the noise block at 25 m/s stays in the same range (188–195). Reasonable for iPhone and Tesla.
* **Info page.** How the sound model works now says v19 is live, v18 is shelved, p1 stays the default, scoring is v2.3, and the roadmap order is the outside path first, then the tyre and wind noise block (both now in v19), then the engine and exhaust with swappable mufflers.
* **Unchanged.** Every older entry stays bit-identical. BRM, GT350 and 911 are untouched.

## v1.13.11 (tag `v1.13.11`), 2026-11-20 (Sound v17 · Cadillac: gas cooling along the exhaust and flow loss at the pipe steps)

* **Sound v17 · Cadillac** is v16 (same sourced Walker shells, 3.07 m connector, 0.5 m tail and p9 damping) plus two generic physical effects. Nothing is fitted to the scorecard. It is picker-only, right after Sound v16 · Cadillac on Cadillac's one drop-down. p1 stays the default. There is no `?caddy=v17` or `?sound=v17` alias and no new URL. Generic parameters only; no Cadillac-only code.
* **Axial gas cooling (new generic set key `t_axial` = outside heat-transfer coefficient h_o).**
  * Each bank's exhaust run, from the head pipe to the tail (consecutive group-1/2 pipes), loses heat by the steady 1-D balance T_out = T_amb + (T_in − T_amb)·exp(−U·πd·L/(ṁ c_p)) (Incropera & DeWitt, *Fundamentals of Heat and Mass Transfer*, ch. 8).
  * U = 1/(1/h_i + 1/h_o). Inside: Dittus–Boelter (1930), Nu = 0.023 Re^0.8 Pr^0.3 (laminar floor 3.66), Sutherland viscosity. Mass flow is the engine model's own exhaust flow per bank. Each pipe takes the gas temperature at its mid-length. The run starts at the model's existing head-pipe temperature.
  * h_o = 20 W/m²K for a car standing still: natural convection from a horizontal cylinder (Churchill & Chu 1975), 8–9 W/m²K on the 1¾ in pipe, plus radiation from oxidised steel (ε 0.79, Incropera Table A.11), 8–12 W/m²K at 420–500 K. Total 16–21 W/m²K. A moving car would see more (Hilpert cross-flow, roughly 50–80 W/m²K); 20 is the conservative still-air value.
  * Warm idle, model gas temperatures, v16 → v17: head pipe 616 → 594 K, muffler 616 → 488 K, connector 505 → 428 K, resonator 505 → 387 K, tail 505 → 380 K. At 2000 rpm and 0.4 throttle: muffler 761 → 713 K, connector, resonator and tail 617 → 681 / 651 / 641 K (v16's single tail temperature was below this run's cooling curve).
  * Effect: the 0.5 m tail pipe's half-wave and the two cans' pass bands (n·c/2L) no longer coincide, so the 210 / 470 / 930 Hz ring drops.
* **Flow loss at pipe steps (set key `port_bc`, generic block from the unreleased v16.1 work).** Every exhaust area step of at least 1.2 is a resistive junction carrying the linearised mean-flow loss Δp = K ρ U u (Borda–Carnot; Ronneberger 1967; Munjal, *Acoustics of Ducts and Mufflers*, 2014, ch. 2). Expansion K = (1 − A1/A2)², contraction K = 0.5 (1 − A2/A1) (Idelchik).
* **Cleaned scorecard (scorer v2, 183 windows, lower is closer):**
  * Combined (pooled + 0.25 × ring): v17 3.715, v16 4.191, p8 4.231. Segment bootstrap v17 − v16 −0.48 (95 % −0.59…−0.37).
  * Pooled: v17 2.880, v16 3.172, p8 3.063. By path, cabin / outside far / tail: v17 2.02 / 5.15 / 2.72; v16 2.09 / 5.82 / 3.70.
  * Ring: v17 3.34 dB, v16 4.08, p8 4.67.
  * Cabin tilt below 1 kHz, model − recording: v17 −2.18 dB/oct, v16 −2.21.
  * Recordings' own floor: pooled 2.37, combined 2.79.
* **Also tried, not shipped** (scorer v2 combined):
  * the perforated straight-through 21159 resonator, 4.86, and +26 waveguides;
  * design 1 three-pass louvred tubes, 4.70, and +35 ms/s;
  * design 2 reversing layout, 4.44, and +11 ms/s;
  * Kirchhoff wall loss added to v17, 3.88, with a darker cabin;
  * h_o 15 / 40 / 50: 3.78 / 3.62 / 3.65. The value was not chosen on score.
  * The dual system and the connector routing stay as in the 1959 shop manual (Fig. 10-23, one per bank).
* CPU: 26 waveguides, the same as v16. Node, 48 kHz, 2000 rpm / 0.4, same run: v17 190.5, v16 190.6, p8 189.9 ms per audio second.
* Bit-identity: every existing v9p set of every car × 2 positions, v1.13.10 tree vs v1.13.11 tree: 54/54 identical. NON_ENGINE_NOISE / MIXER_WIND stay 0.

## v1.13.10 (tag `v1.13.10`), 2026-11-19 (Sound v16 · Cadillac: plain long chambers in the real Walker shells, from the inverse-cans analysis)

* **Sound v16 · Cadillac** is Sound v15 (same engine, pipes, radiation, listener and cabin) with its four TMM cans replaced by one plain long chamber per can in the sourced Walker shells. It is picker-only, appended after Sound v15 · Cadillac on Cadillac's one drop-down. p1 stays the default. There is no `?caddy=v16` or `?sound=v16`.
* **Why.** Working backwards (v15 with the cans swapped for straight pipe, compared with the 142 dry recording windows) shows that the recordings want the cans to cut about evenly from 125 Hz to 2 kHz, relative to 500 Hz. v15's cans acted as a strong low-pass instead: about −20 dB at 63–125 Hz and +11 to +17 dB at 1–2 kHz.
* **Cans, per bank** (generic `chamber` / `pipe` items):
  * muffler (Walker 21158): 20.25 in shell; 3.25 × 7.75 in oval over the 2 in pipe, area ratio 6.296875. Walker catalog, sourced.
  * connector: 3.07 m of 1¾ in pipe. Sourced p5 lengths.
  * resonator (Walker 21159): 22.5 in shell; 5⅛ in round, area ratio 6.56640625. Walker catalog, sourced.
  * tail: 0.5 m of 1¾ in (p5).
  * No extended tubes and no reverse-flow internals. No Helmholtz branch, because none is sourced.
* **Damping.** p9's physics (Rao & Munjal 1986 louvre/perforate resistance, Kirchhoff wall loss, idle/cruise midpoint gas state), not fitted: chamber `pack` 0.25 (muffler) / 0.13 (resonator), set `pack` 1.743 unchanged, `pack_r` 0.30.
* **New generic key `muf_t1`.** Plain muffler-list items up to and including the first chamber take the group-1 gas temperature (`tm`). This is what a first TMM can fed by the inter pipe already got (`tools/can_gas.cjs`). Without the key, every plain item stays at the tail temperature, so older sets are bit-identical. In v16 the muffler is at about 508 m/s sound speed instead of 461 m/s at 700 rpm.
* **Pipe–can coupling trace.** The question was why plain chambers in the model's pipe chain gave about +15 dB at 250 Hz (relative to 500 Hz) where a standalone can gives about +7.
  * Method: an exact frequency-domain solver of the kernel's own exhaust graph (same pipes, Kelly–Lochbaum junctions, valve and open-end conditions, per-pipe losses). Run time-domain, it reproduces the kernel's impulse response to 6e-16.
  * Fractional-delay interpolation versus exact delays: under 1 dB below 2 kHz.
  * Mean-flow loss at the area steps: changes the shape by under 0.5 dB.
  * Junction end corrections (Karal): about 1 cm, negligible.
  * Result: no numerical or junction error. With anechoic ends the in-chain chambers give +7.7 dB at 250 Hz, matching the standalone figure. With the real ends (closed valves 0.98, open tail), the loss at 500 Hz collapses from 8.4 dB to 0.7 dB. The 0.5 m tail pipe's half-wave resonance (about 450 Hz with end correction) coincides with the chambers' first pass bands, so the other bands look high when measured relative to 500 Hz. That is real in-system physics, so the kernel's coupling is unchanged.
* **Scores (142 dry windows, lower is closer):** v16 3.292, p9 3.207, p8 2.950, v15 5.660.
  * By path (cabin / outside far / tailpipe / engine bay): v16 2.971 / 4.383 / 4.381 / 7.485.
  * v16 is the closest of v15, p8 and p9 in the 2,400–3,600 rpm full-throttle cell (2.769) and part-throttle cell (2.413). It is behind p8 and p9 at 350–2,400 rpm, and far ahead of v15 everywhere.
  * Can-loss shape, 125 Hz–2 kHz (rms dB against the inverse-cans curve, pooled): v16 4.0, p10 4.5, p9 4.7, p8 5.5, v15 12.4. This is the best of every set tried.
  * 1.5–6 kHz share, model − recording: v16 −7.9 dB (p9 −7.0, p8 −4.4, v15 −33.1). Slope: −1.55 dB/oct (p9 −1.38, p8 −0.37, v15 −6.96).
* **Cost.** Same 26 waveguides as p8 and p9. Desktop CPU (node, ms per audio second, 48 kHz, 2000 rpm, same run): v16 180.4, p8 182.7, p9 178.2, v15 184.5.
* **Unchanged.** Every older entry stays bit-identical (52/52 offline renders, including p8–p10 and v15). BRM is untouched.

## v1.13.9 (tag `v1.13.9`), 2026-11-19 (v12 · Cadillac p10: p9 with damping at the upper end of the plausible range)

* **v12 · Cadillac p10** is p9 (same plain single-chamber cans, sourced sizes, v14.1 engine and pipes) with more damping. It is set by the same physics as p9, at the upper end of the plausible range, and not fitted to the scorecard. It is picker-only, right after p9 on Cadillac's one drop-down. p1 stays the default. There is no `?caddy=p10` or `?sound=p10` alias and no new URL. Generic parameters only; no packing material; no Cadillac-only code.
* **Upper-end assumptions** (p9's are given in brackets):
  * Gas state: the engine model's cruise state, muffler 701.9 K, Mach 0.0634; resonator 576.1 K, Mach 0.0750 [idle/cruise midpoint]. Sourced from the model (`can_gas`).
  * Rao & Munjal (1986) flow resistance × 1.25 [× 1.0]: estimate, allowing for the scatter of their empirical fit.
  * Muffler louvre porosity 0.08 [0.11]: the lower bound of the US2182945 drawing, which shows 14 louvres on the drawn arc. 0.11 assumes 20 louvres all round.
  * Muffler: Sullivan (1979) cross-flow resistance 0.514 d M/(l σ) added [not used], because all the TriFlow's flow crosses its louvres.
  * Resonator porosity 0.15 with 1.5 mm holes [0.20, ⅛ in]: estimate.
  * A sweep of 38 assumption sets (porosity, state, coefficient, hole size, cross-flow) bounds the range. A resonator porosity of 0.10 was left out: there the plane-wave model breaks down above about 2 kHz, near the shell's first cross mode.
* **Dissipative attenuation per octave band**, 125 Hz–4 kHz, in dB:
  * muffler 1.0 / 1.6 / 1.9 / 2.6 / 3.4 / 6.0 (p9: 0.5 / 0.9 / 1.1 / 1.4 / 1.9 / 2.6);
  * resonator 0.7 / 1.2 / 1.3 / 1.8 / 2.4 / 3.4 (p9: 0.4 / 0.5 / 0.7 / 0.9 / 1.2 / 2.1).
  * This is about twice p9 and the realistic upper bound for these cans without packing.
* **Mapped as in p9**, by least squares on octave bands:
  * muffler chamber `pack` 0.39 (p9 0.25);
  * resonator chamber `pack` 0.24 (p9 0.13);
  * set `pack` 1.743 unchanged;
  * `pack_r` 0.31 (p9 0.30).
  * Mapped attenuation: muffler 0.8 / 0.4 / 2.2 / 2.4 / 4.6 / 5.3 dB; resonator 0.3 / 0.2 / 1.5 / 1.8 / 2.2 / 3.6 dB.
* **Scores, for information (142 dry windows, lower is closer):** p10 3.663, p9 3.207, p8 2.950, p1 3.622, p2 3.554.
  * By path (cabin / outside far / tailpipe / engine bay): p10 3.354 / 4.646 / 4.764 / 7.951.
  * p10 is the closest of p8, p9 and p10 in all three 2,400–3,600 rpm cells: shut 2.529, part 2.286, full 2.590. It is behind both at 350–2,400 rpm.
  * 1.5–6 kHz share, model − recording: p10 −9.3 dB (p9 −7.0, p8 −4.4). Slope: −2.42 dB/oct (p9 −1.38, p8 −0.37).
* **Cost.** Same 26 waveguides as p8 and p9. Desktop CPU (node, ms per audio second, 48 kHz, 2000 rpm, same run): p10 173.9, p9 175.0, p8 175.5.
* **Unchanged.** Every older entry stays bit-identical (52/52 offline renders, including p8 and p9). BRM is untouched.

## v1.13.8 (tag `v1.13.8`), 2026-11-18 (v12 · Cadillac p9: p8 with a realistic amount of damping)

* **v12 · Cadillac p9** is p8 (same plain single-chamber cans, same sourced sizes, same v14.1 engine and pipes) plus damping set from physics and sources, not from the scorecard. It is picker-only, placed right after p8 on Cadillac's one drop-down. p1 stays the default. There is no `?caddy=p9` or `?sound=p9` alias and no new URL. Generic parameters only; no Cadillac-only code.
* **What real cans of this type damp.**
  * Plain reactive chambers barely dissipate without flow: NACA Report 1192 (Davis, Stokes, Moore & Stevens, 1954) measured 77 mufflers at room temperature without flow and found expansion chambers "in good agreement with the plane-wave theory", which has no losses; viscous effects only limit the peaks at resonance. The same report notes that flow tends to increase low-frequency attenuation.
  * Louvred or perforated cans with flow do dissipate: Sullivan (*JASA* 66, 1979, 779–788) found that such components "can be very dissipative, even though they contain no recognizable dissipative materials", because of the flow-induced resistance of the perforation.
  * No packing: the Walker patents (US2182945, US3841435, US3498406) describe reactive cans with no fibrous packing.
* **How the amount was set.** For each can, a segmented concentric perforated-duct model (Sullivan & Crocker 1978; Munjal, *Acoustics of Ducts and Mufflers*, 2nd ed. 2014, ch. 4) was built with:
  * the real shell and a 1¾ in louvred tube (sourced);
  * louvre porosity 0.11 for the muffler (sourced, US2182945 / US1931736) and 0.20 with ⅛ in holes for the resonator (carried over from p4, estimate);
  * Rao & Munjal (*J. Sound Vib.* 108, 1986) grazing-flow resistance 7.337·10⁻³ (1 + 72.23 M)/σ, plus the orifice's own visco-thermal resistance;
  * Kirchhoff wall losses at the real gas temperature (Sutherland viscosity, γ 1.30, Pr 0.70);
  * the gas state midway between the engine model's warm idle and cruise (`can_gas`; v14.1/v15): muffler 662 K, Mach 0.022; resonator 548 K, Mach 0.026 (estimate; Rao–Munjal is fitted for Mach 0.05–0.2, so this is a slight extrapolation).
* **Result.** Dissipative attenuation per octave band from 125 Hz to 4 kHz, in dB:
  * muffler 0.5 / 0.9 / 1.1 / 1.4 / 1.9 / 2.6;
  * resonator 0.4 / 0.5 / 0.7 / 0.9 / 1.2 / 2.1;
  * p8's chambers: about 0.05.
* **Mapping onto the model's existing generic terms**, by least squares on those octave bands (not on recordings):
  * muffler chamber `pack` 0.25;
  * resonator chamber `pack` 0.13;
  * the set's `pack` coefficient 1.743 unchanged (the one-pole, frequency-rising part);
  * new set value `pack_r` 0.30 (the broadband part; it was already a generic parameter, used by the 911).
  * Mapped attenuation: muffler 0.5 / 0.2 / 1.4 / 1.2 / 1.6 / 2.8 dB; resonator 0.2 / 0.1 / 0.9 / 1.0 / 1.3 / 2.0 dB. A plain chamber cannot dissipate much below its first length mode, so the lowest two bands come out under the target.
* **Scores, for information only (142 dry windows, lower is closer):** p9 3.207, p8 2.950, p1 3.622, p2 3.554, v14.1 5.263, v15 5.660.
  * By path (cabin / outside far / tailpipe / engine bay): p9 2.894 / 4.280 / 4.256 / 7.600.
  * 1.5–6 kHz share, model − recording: p9 −7.0 dB (p8 −4.4, p1 −7.9, p2 −9.1, v14.1 −29.7).
  * Slope: p9 −1.38 dB/oct (p8 −0.37, p1 −0.78, p2 −2.38, v14.1 −6.33).
  * p9 is closer than p8 at 2,400–3,600 rpm (all three throttle settings), and further at lower rpm.
* **Cost.** Same 26 waveguides as p8. Desktop CPU (node, ms per audio second, 48 kHz, 2000 rpm): p9 185.6, p8 188.1, v14.1 194.0 (same run).
* **Unchanged.** Every older entry stays bit-identical (50/50 offline renders, including p8). BRM is untouched.

## v1.13.7 (tag `v1.13.7`), 2026-11-18 (v12 · Cadillac p8: four plain 1-D cans from a small layout search)

* **v12 · Cadillac p8** is a new picker-only entry in the v12 family, placed right after p7 on Cadillac's one drop-down. p1 stays the default. There is no `?caddy=p8` or `?sound=p8` alias, and no new URL; `?sound=p8` on the Cadillac does not fall through to kernel v1. The full history stays on the drop-down.
* **What it is.** Sound v14.1's parameters (sourced engine and pipes, gas_dyn, k_jet = 0, k_vf = 0, r_ac 3900, tail 0.5 m), with the four cans back on the plain v12 1-D muffler list (`chamber` / `pipe` items, as in p1–p7 and the 911). No TMM filters and no 3-D shells. Generic items only; no Cadillac-only code.
* **Layout per bank** (sourced = from a source; searched = chosen by the search):
  * Muffler (Walker 21158 TriFlow): **1 chamber** (searched, 1–3 tried), 20¼ in long (sourced, Walker catalog), section = the 3¼ × 7¾ in oval over the 2 in pipe, area ratio 6.297 (sourced), packing 0 (searched, 0/0.1/0.3 tried; also consistent with US3841435 / US3498406, which describe a reactive TriFlow with no packing).
  * Connector: 3.07 m of 1¾ in pipe (sourced p5 lengths, as v13–v15).
  * Resonator (Walker 21159 Direct Flow): **1 chamber** (searched, 1–3 tried), 22½ in long, 5⅛ in round (sourced, Walker replacement-line spec), area ratio 6.566, packing 0 (searched, 0/0.1/0.3/1.0 tried).
* **Search (owner-authorised, small and structured).** 29 candidates in three stages: muffler 1/2/3 chambers (sourced US2182945 partition positions; internal tube 0.1 or 0.3 m, estimate) × resonator 1/2/3 chambers (½-½ or 0.22/0.45/0.33) at p2's packing; then a packing grid on the best layout; then three multi-chamber checks without packing. Scored on the 4×3 matrix against the same 142 dry windows. The simplest layout won overall, in every leave-one-source-out fold, on all four listening paths and on all six recordings.
* **Scores (142 dry windows, lower is better):** p8 **2.950**, p1 3.622, p2 3.554, v14.1 5.263, v15 5.660. By path (cabin / outside far / tailpipe / engine bay): p8 2.634 / 4.066 / 3.979 / 7.349; p1 3.337 / 4.616 / 4.561 / 7.571. 1.5–6 kHz share, model − recording: p8 −4.4 dB (p1 −7.9, p2 −9.1, v14.1 −29.7, v15 −33.1). Slope: p8 −0.37 dB/oct (p1 −0.78, p2 −2.38, v14.1 −6.33, v15 −6.96). p8 is behind p1 in 2 of 9 cells (2.4–3.6k full and part throttle, 6 windows).
* **Caveat.** Every model in this family is still short of the recordings' treble, so the score favours the least-damping can layout. p8 is the simplest plausible layout, not a sourced cut-open geometry; the real 21159's chamber count is still unknown.
* **Cost.** 26 waveguides, no can filters to build. Desktop CPU (node, ms per audio second, 48 kHz, 2000 rpm): p8 188.1, v14.1 210.8, v15 206.7, p2 201.1. Within the Tesla/iPhone budget.
* **Unchanged.** Every older entry stays bit-identical (48/48 offline renders checked): p1–p7, v13–v15, the GT350 and the 911. BRM is untouched.

## v1.13.6 (tag `v1.13.6`), 2026-11-18 (Sound v15 · Cadillac: cruise-flow can filters; causal perforated-tube model)

* **Sound v15 · Cadillac** = Sound v14.1 with a second, prebuilt gas state for the muffler and resonator. A new generic parameter, `muf_cruise`, holds the cruise state. Nothing is fitted to recordings, and there is no Cadillac-only code.
* **Cruise state, from the engine model** (`tools/can_gas.cjs cruise`). Cruise means 0.4 × redline = 2000 rpm at quarter throttle, which gives p_man 0.607 atm and T_prim 922.1 K.
  * Muffler: 701.9 K, Mach 0.0634.
  * Resonator: 576.1 K, Mach 0.0750.
  * For comparison, warm idle (v14.1) is 621.7 K / Mach 0.0073 for the muffler and 519.6 K / Mach 0.0087 for the resonator.
* **Choosing the state while driving.**
  * Each can computes its live mean-flow Mach number from the engine model's live mass flow and gas temperature. It uses the same formula as `can_gas`.
  * The can uses the design state nearest in log Mach. The switch point is the geometric mean of the two Mach numbers, with ±20 % hysteresis.
  * The mean-flow terms of the transfer-matrix model are set by Mach and temperature, so this is the closer of the two exact models. It is not a blend fitted to anything.
  * On a change, the new filter runs for 512 samples to warm up, then crosses over linearly in 1024 samples. Steady driving uses the plain single filter, the same code path as v14.1.
* **Cost.** Both states are prebuilt for 44.1/48 kHz (`tools/prebuild_cans.cjs`, 8 filters, 25 KB), so there is no compile at load and no stall on a switch.
  * Construction takes 2–4 ms, against v14.1's 1 ms and v14's ~1.4 s.
  * Desktop CPU (node, ms per audio second):
    * steady cruise: v14.1 189.0 / 188.4, v15 189.5 / 188.9 (+0.3 %);
    * switching state twice a second: 184.8 / 185.8 vs 186.9 / 187.4 (+1 %).
* **Generic fix: 1-D perforated tube (TMM rev 5).**
  * The old concentric-perforate cascade (rev 1–4) mixed left-to-right tube matrices with a right-to-left perforate link, then inverted the product. The effect was a negative-admittance perforate and waves running backwards in time.
    * On v14's cans, 85 % (muffler) and 100 % (resonator) of the S21 impulse-response energy fell at negative time.
    * Refined to fine segments, it did not converge to the textbook limit: 48.5 dB at 400 Hz for a 5 %-open tube.
  * Rev 5 is the segmented coupled-duct model (Sullivan & Crocker, *JASA* 64 (1978) 207–215; Munjal, *Acoustics of Ducts and Mufflers*, 2nd ed. 2014, ch. 4).
    * Segments are 1.5 cm or shorter. The annulus is closed at both ends.
    * It is solved as a nodal network, because a 4×4 matrix power overflows when the perforate is strongly coupled.
  * Checks:
    * It matches the independent 3-D plane-wave model within 0.02 dB from 100 to 1000 Hz (σ 0.2, L 0.3 m, d 1¾ in, D 0.13 m).
    * It converges with refinement.
    * 0 % of its impulse-response energy is at negative time.
  * The old path is kept unchanged for rev ≤ 4, so every older set stays bit-identical.
  * **v14.1 and v15 use the 3-D cans (`triflow3d`, `perf_through3d`), which never went through the 1-D cascade. The fix therefore does not change their sound.** It applies to any set that uses the 1-D kinds at rev 5.
* **Unchanged.** Every older entry stays bit-identical (offline renders checked): p1–p7, v13, v13.1, v13.2, v13.3, v13.4, v14, v14.1, the GT350 and the 911.
* v15 is added at the end of Cadillac's one drop-down, picker-only. p1 stays the default. There is no ?caddy= or ?sound= alias.

## v1.13.5 (tag `v1.13.5`), 2026-11-18 (Sound v14.1 · Cadillac: 3-D muffler and resonator shells)

* **Sound v14.1 · Cadillac** = Sound v14 with each can shell modelled in 3-D. Generic kinds `triflow3d` (Walker three-tube reverse-flow muffler, US2182945 topology) and `perf_through3d` (straight-through perforated resonator, US3648803). Any car can use them through its muffler parameters; there is no Cadillac-only code.
* **Model.** Each chamber is a rigid box with the real shell length. Its section is an equal-area rectangle with the oval's 3.25 × 7.75 in aspect ratio, or a square for the round resonator, minus the tubes that pass through it. Pressure follows from the modal Green's function: transverse modes cos(mπy/Ly)·cos(nπz/Lz) and the axial direction in closed form for every mode, propagating or evanescent (Morse & Ingard, *Theoretical Acoustics*, 1968, ch. 9; Venkatesham, Tiwari & Munjal, *J. Sound Vib.* 323 (2009) 1032–1044). Wall loss is the same Kirchhoff boundary-layer term as the tubes. The internal tubes stay 1-D waveguides. Louvres and holes use the same perforate impedance as v13/v14, in 1.5 cm segments. Tube layout: inlet, return and outlet side by side, "symmetrically arranged" (US3841435). The two-port of the can comes from nodal analysis.
* **Checks.** A plain expansion chamber below the first cross mode matches the textbook 1-D result (Munjal, *Acoustics of Ducts and Mufflers*, 2nd ed. 2014) within 0.24 dB mean, 50–800 Hz. The remaining difference is the evanescent-mode end correction. A high-porosity concentric resonator tends to the expansion-chamber limit, as it should. Cross modes in the muffler appear from about 1.25 kHz.
* **Numbers changed, each from a source.**
  * Louvre porosity 0.10 → 0.11. US2182945 Fig. 3 shows about 20 louvres round the 1¾ in tube, with 0.030 in openings (US1931736): 20 × 0.030 / (π × 1.75) = 0.109.
  * Gas temperature and flow come from the engine model at warm idle (`tools/can_gas.cjs`). Muffler: 621.7 K, Mach 0.0073. Resonator: 519.6 K, Mach 0.0087. These replace the estimates 650 K / 0.08 and 550 K / 0.06.
* **Filter (TMM rev 4).** The 3-D responses have narrow cross-mode peaks that the 256-point rev-3 fit under-sampled, losing up to 10 dB of band energy. Rev 4 takes the exact causal impulse response from a dense grid and splits it into the same hybrid filter: a 16-tap FIR head plus an order-8 IIR tail on each of the four paths, at fs/4. That is no larger than the v14 cans (34 multiply-adds per path; v14 uses 30–42). Octave-band fit error is mostly within 1.5 dB, worst 2.5 dB.
* **Load time.** The v14.1 can filters are prebuilt for 44.1 and 48 kHz (`tools/prebuild_cans.cjs`). They are bit-identical to compiling at load, so picking v14.1 does not pause to compile. Other sample rates compile at load. Identical cans are now compiled once and shared (both banks, and repeat picks), which roughly halves the first-pick pause of v13.x/v14 with the same output.
* **Unchanged.** Every older entry (p1–p7, v13, v13.1, v13.2, v13.3, v13.4, v14, the GT350, the 911) stays bit-identical; 44 offline renders were checked. v14.1 is added at the end of Cadillac's one drop-down, picker-only. p1 stays the default; there is no ?caddy= / ?sound= alias.
* **Caveats.** The 3-D cans still run at fs/4, like v13/v14, so nothing above about 5 kHz passes through them. The gas state is warm idle; at cruise the engine model gives a Mach number about 9 × higher. The tube layout inside the oval is "symmetrically arranged" (US3841435); the exact spacing is not stated.

## v1.13.4 (tag `v1.13.4`), 2026-11-17 (Sound v14 · Cadillac: flow noise removed)

* **Sound v14 · Cadillac** = Sound v13.4 with flow noise removed from the model: `k_jet = 0` (tailpipe jet noise) and `k_vf = 0` (exhaust-valve flow noise). Both were measured about 80 dB under the radiated exhaust sound (switching either off changed a 2000 rpm throttle step by 0.0 dB).
* **Generic kernel change.** The open-end (typ 2) node now skips the jet computation when `k_jet = 0` (no RNG draw, no per-sample `exp` / `sqrt`). Every shipped set has `k_jet > 0`, so p1–p7, v13, v13.1, v13.2, v13.4, the GT350 and the 911 stay bit-identical. Valve flow noise was already skipped at `k_vf = 0`.
* Added at the end of Cadillac's one drop-down, picker-only. p1 stays the default; no ?caddy= / ?sound= alias.

* **Per-car lists:**
  * GT350: Sound v1 (procedural), v2 (physical exhaust), v3 (start-up sequence), v4 (pop/bang fix), v12 · GT350 p1 (default).
  * Cadillac: Sound v1–v4, then v12 · Cadillac p1 (default), p2, p3, p4, p5, p6 (test), p7 (picker-only), Sound v13 · Cadillac, Sound v13.1 · Cadillac, Sound v13.2 · Cadillac, Sound v13.4 · Cadillac and Sound v14 · Cadillac (all picker-only).
  * BRM: v12 · BRM p1, p2, p3, p4, p6, p8, p9, p10, p11 (default). Not listed: p5, p7, v9.0, v8.
  * 911: v12 · 911 p1–p7 (p7 default). The 911 was never on v1–v4.

## v1.13.3 (tag `v1.13.3`), 2026-11-17 (Sound v13.4 · Cadillac: throttle lag fix)

* **Diagnosis.** The throttle lag on v13.x was not filter delay or CPU. On a throttle step 0.1→1 at fixed rpm, most of v13's rise came through the exhaust-temperature model: three lags in series (load 1.5 s → primary gas 0.4 s → tail gas 1.0 s, unsourced, from the v9.11 work-in-progress). With the temperature frozen, v13.3 at 2000 rpm rose +0.6 dB instead of +3.8 dB.
* **Generic fix `gas_dyn` (parameter, off by default).** The exhaust gas temperature follows each engine cycle (Heywood 1988, ch. 6: exhaust gas temperature is set per cycle; slow EGT readings are thermocouple and wall lag). Port gas and load: one engine cycle (120/rpm s). Tail gas: plug-flow transport time = exhaust volume downstream of the ports (pipes + TMM can shells, 0.048 m³ for v13.4) / exhaust volume flow (η_v · MAP/P_atm · V_d · rpm/120 · T_prim/T_amb), bounded to one cycle … 2 s. Wall warm-up (25 s) is unchanged, so steady-state temperatures and levels are the same.
* **TMM checked, not changed.** Fitted S21 matches the frequency-domain TL within about 1–3 dB up to 3 kHz; S21 → 1 at low frequency; passive; `scalePassive` = 1. The remaining 2000 rpm gap to p2 is the TriFlow's real 400 Hz–4 kHz TL removing the steepened blowdown energy that carries p2's instant throttle cue. Not EQ'd or level-matched.
* **Sound v13.4 · Cadillac** = v13.1 geometry, TMM `rev = 3` (the v13.3 upsampler fix: continuous fs/4 output interpolation, 12 kHz whine −37 dB → −81 dB; v13.3 itself was never shipped), plus `gas_dyn = 1`. Added at the end of Cadillac's one drop-down, picker-only. v13, v13.1 and v13.2 stay bit-identical. p1 stays the default; no ?caddy= / ?sound= alias.
* **Step response (dB vs part throttle; 60–120 / 120–250 / 250–500 ms; steady).** 2000 rpm: v13.3 +3.7 / +1.5 / +1.8; +3.8 → v13.4 +3.9 / +4.4 / +4.1; +4.2. 3500 rpm: v13.3 +5.1 / +4.9 / +5.1; +7.2 → v13.4 +6.8 / +7.5 / +6.3; +6.6. CPU (node, one core): v13.3 19.7 / 18.7 %, v13.4 18.6 / 17.9 %, p2 about 19 %.

* **Per-car lists:**
  * GT350: Sound v1 (procedural), v2 (physical exhaust), v3 (start-up sequence), v4 (pop/bang fix), v12 · GT350 p1 (default).
  * Cadillac: Sound v1–v4, then v12 · Cadillac p1 (default), p2, p3, p4, p5, p6 (test), p7 (picker-only), Sound v13 · Cadillac, Sound v13.1 · Cadillac, Sound v13.2 · Cadillac and Sound v13.4 · Cadillac (all picker-only).
  * BRM: v12 · BRM p1, p2, p3, p4, p6, p8, p9, p10, p11 (default). Not listed: p5, p7, v9.0, v8.
  * 911: v12 · 911 p1–p7 (p7 default). The 911 was never on v1–v4.

## v1.13.2 (tag `v1.13.2`), 2026-11-16 (Sound v13.2 · Cadillac: high-rpm jump fix and cheaper TMM)

* **Diagnosis, not a retune.** On v13.1 the engine suddenly got loud at higher rpm (part +8.0 dB from 3200 −13.6 to 3700 −5.6; full +7.3 dB from 3200 −9.9 to 3800 −2.6) and felt laggy on iPhone. The IIR-fit quality gate only covered 20–2000 Hz, so HF errors were unchecked. Triflow S21 under-TL vs the frequency-domain TMM was 5.56 dB at 349 Hz and about 21 dB at 7.5 kHz; S11 was 3.25 dB too strong at 1237 Hz; poles sat at 0.990–0.997. At 3600–3700 rpm a V8’s 5th harmonic is ~1233 Hz and the 6th at 3200 rpm is 1280 Hz (the real 21158 passband, fd TL 5.21 dB). Those stacked. The passband itself is physics and was not EQ’d or level-matched away, and louvre `dHole` 0.030 in / σ = 0.10 were not retuned.
* **Fit fixes behind `rev >= 2`.** Mid-band (20–2 kHz) now also caps under-TL; above 2 kHz, only under-TL where the FD can is not already a deep notch (stopband TL-ratio errors are ignored). If the first-arrival IR window misses the main energy (typical for S11, whose IFFT peak sits just before t=0), the hybrid/FIR window is aligned to that peak. TMM filters are designed and run at fs/4 (boxcar in, linear interpolate out; per-can state, banks not shared), with the target tapered to 0 toward the design Nyquist so the fit does not leak at 6 kHz. v13 (`rev` 0) and v13.1 (`rev` 1) stay bit-identical (SNR ~3000 dB vs the v1.13.1 tree). Generic; no Cadillac-only code.
* **Fit error before / after (triflow, 48 kHz).** S21 mid-band under-TL 5.56 dB @ 349 Hz → 5.73 dB @ 349 Hz (still inside the 6 dB mid gate). 1280 Hz passband: fd TL 5.21, v13.1 fit 6.21, v13.2 fit 5.34 (not boosted). S11 under-TL at 1237 Hz 3.25 → 2.18 dB. The old 6 kHz S21 leak (fit much louder than FD) is gone; the tapered fit is quieter than FD there.
* **CPU and iPhone path.** Chromium, 8 s at 2000 rpm part throttle, 48 kHz, this session: p2 24.5 %, v13.1 40.2 %, v13.2 23.8 % (peak 1.0676, −13.6 dB vs p2 5.1318; not level-matched). v13.2 is at or under this session’s p2. The same box reported p2 19.2 % and v13.1 30.0 % for v1.13.1; scaled 23.8/24.5 × 19.2 ≈ 18.7 %. The worklet already uses `latencyHint: 'interactive'`, loads from an inlined Blob first, and the status line already shows CPU % and overruns; those were not changed.
* **Level vs rpm.** A focused 3000–4000 rpm sweep of v13.2: part 3200→3800 +0.8 dB (−9.9 → −9.0), full +2.8 dB (−6.8 → −4.0), gradual. No sudden jump.
