(A slower 600–5000 sweep was +3.5 / +4.5 dB over that same 600 rpm, still well under v13.1’s +8.0 / +7.3.) Stable at 44.1 / 48 / 96 kHz (0 NaNs, 0 resets, no pole shrink).
* **Sound v13.2 · Cadillac** is the v13.1 geometry with `rev = 2` on both TMM specs. It is added after v13.1 on Cadillac’s one drop-down, picker-only. The v13 and v13.1 entries stay. p1 stays the default. There is no `?caddy=v13.2` / `?sound=v13.2` alias — those tokens do not select v13.2 and do not fall through to kernel v1. No new URLs. Defaults unchanged (GT350 v12 p1, Cadillac p1, BRM p11, 911 p7). Official 4×3 dry scores vs p2 / v13.1 follow after this release.

* **Per-car lists:**
  * GT350: Sound v1 (procedural), v2 (physical exhaust), v3 (start-up sequence), v4 (pop/bang fix), v12 · GT350 p1 (default).
  * Cadillac: Sound v1–v4, then v12 · Cadillac p1 (default), p2, p3, p4, p5, p6 (test), p7 (picker-only), Sound v13 · Cadillac (picker-only), Sound v13.1 · Cadillac (picker-only), and Sound v13.2 · Cadillac (picker-only).
  * BRM: v12 · BRM p1, p2, p3, p4, p6, p8, p9, p10, p11 (default). Not listed: p5, p7, v9.0, v8.
  * 911: v12 · 911 p1–p7 (p7 default). The 911 was never on v1–v4.

## v1.13.1 (tag `v1.13.1`), 2026-11-16 (Sound v13.1 · Cadillac: TMM physics fixes and a cheaper filter)

* **Diagnosis, not a retune.** v13 was about 23 dB too quiet: Walker 21158 mid-band TL was 44–54 dB (640 Hz 44.1, 1280 Hz 54.0) against a realistic 20–30 dB for a stock 1959 muffler. Textbook checks still held (expansion-chamber TL MAE 0.04 dB vs Munjal; Helmholtz notch 59.4 dB at 136 Hz). The cause was two generic TMM bugs, not the louvre-size or porosity estimates, and not FIR path-delay peel, impedance units or the sign of the Mach terms. A dHole/σ sweep on the broken concentric barely moved mid-band TL, because the waveguides had already collapsed.
* **Bug A — shadowed Mach in the perforated concentric.** `var M = m4id()` inside `tPerforatedConcentric` hid the mean-flow parameter (and, being hoisted, left `zPerforate` with `M === undefined`). The inner and annulus `tTube` calls then received a 4×4 as Mach; `1-M*M` was NaN; `cx(r, i || 0)` turned that NaN imaginary wavenumber into 0, so those ducts had no acoustic length. The Sullivan 4×4 quietly became perforate shunts between zero-length tubes — a lumped-cavity short. v13 keeps this legacy path so the released listening test stays bit-identical; the fix is opt-in (`rev >= 1`). `cx()` itself is not changed globally, because legacy v13 depends on the NaN-swallow.
* **Bug B — wrong S22 signs.** For the typ-0 convention (U both +x), `N22` must be `−A Z2 + B − Z1 C Z2 + Z1 D`. The code used the opposite Z1 terms, so even an identity matrix with equal areas gave `S22 = −1`, `r22+t12 ≈ 2`, and `scalePassive ≈ 0.71` (−3 dB on every Sij, including S21). After both fixes the 21158 can has scale = 1; 21159 still has a mild scale ≈ 0.917 (~0.75 dB) and that safety stays. Identity 2 in / 1.75 in area change is now P1=P2=1.
* **TL after the fixes (frequency-domain, not level-matched).** 21158 at 80/160/320/640/1000/1280/2000 Hz: 4.1 / 9.6 / 24.7 / 27.4 / 14.0 / 5.8 / 33.6 dB (640 Hz 44.1 → 27.4, now in the 20–30 dB band; the old 54 dB at 1280 Hz was a 5.8 dB passband). 21159: 9.4 / 12.4 / 12.4 / 23.2 / 20.5 / 23.3 / 22.1 dB. Louvre `dHole` 0.030 in (US1931736 width used as a Lee–Ih equivalent diameter) and σ = 0.10 stay estimates and were not retuned.
* **Cheaper runtime filter.** The 512-tap FIRs are replaced, when `rev >= 1`, by a short causal FIR head plus a stable low-order IIR fit of the delayed tail (Steiglitz–McBride, poles pulled inside the unit circle, passivity checked). A fit is accepted only when LF-to-mid |S| and TL stay close to the frequency-domain can (so the passband just restored is not punched out again). Legacy v13 still uses the 512-tap FIR. Any car can use the block; parameters only; no Cadillac-only code.
* **Sound v13.1 · Cadillac** is the same v13 geometry (p2 pack, four TMM cans, 22 pipes, p7 `r_ac`) with `rev = 1` on both TMM specs. It is added after v13 on Cadillac’s one drop-down, picker-only. The v13 entry stays. p1 stays the default. There is no `?caddy=v13.1` / `?sound=v13.1` alias — those tokens do not select v13.1 and do not fall through to kernel v1. No new URLs. Defaults unchanged (GT350 v12 p1, Cadillac p1, BRM p11, 911 p7). Under-budget networks stay bit-identical with v1.13.0.
* **Measured (not fitted).** Official 4×3 JS dry scorecard (326 windows, new_dry n=142 / 168.9 s, same cells and `ref_feat` as v1.13.0): pooled mean p1 3.622, p2 3.554, v13 5.943, v13.1 6.318. High-rpm 2.4–3.6k shut/part/full: p1 3.158 / 2.834 / 2.345, p2 2.442 / 2.669 / 2.418, v13 4.677 / 7.343 / 7.920, v13.1 4.351 / 5.477 / 5.638. v13.1 improves those high-rpm cells vs v13 but does not beat p2; the 3.6–5k band is empty. (v1.13.0 published means on the same target were p1 3.590, p2 3.498, v13 6.058; small offset from a reconstructed JS renderer, same window set.) Chromium CPU (8 s, 2000 rpm, part, 48 kHz): p1 19.2 %, p2 19.2 %, v13 42.6 %, v13.1 30.0 % — cheaper than v13, not down to ~p2 20 %. Level vs p2 in that same test: v13 peak 0.364 (−23.0 dB), v13.1 peak 0.6875 (−17.5 dB vs p2, +5.5 dB vs v13); not level-matched. Mouth impulse at 44.1 kHz (4–40 ms): p1 t0 10.84 ms max_2_6/max_10_20 1.917 / 0.921; p2 10.84 / 2.383 / 1.564; v13 21.84 / 8.418 / 1.685 (a0 ~3×10⁻⁹); v13.1 23.45 / 12.211 / 5.801 (a0 ~4×10⁻⁹; later t0, so the first detected peak may be a precursor). Blip energy after throttle release decays for v13.1 (−8.2 … −2.2 dB, no runaway); p1 decays, p2 and v13 linger near −2 dB.

* **Per-car lists:**
  * GT350: Sound v1 (procedural), v2 (physical exhaust), v3 (start-up sequence), v4 (pop/bang fix), v12 · GT350 p1 (default).
  * Cadillac: Sound v1–v4, then v12 · Cadillac p1 (default), p2, p3, p4, p5, p6 (test), p7 (picker-only), Sound v13 · Cadillac (picker-only), and Sound v13.1 · Cadillac (picker-only).
  * BRM: v12 · BRM p1, p2, p3, p4, p6, p8, p9, p10, p11 (default). Not listed: p5, p7, v9.0, v8.
  * 911: v12 · 911 p1–p7 (p7 default). The 911 was never on v1–v4.

## v1.13.0 (tag `v1.13.0`), 2026-11-16 (Sound v13 · Cadillac: generic TMM muffler block)

* **New model version, not a p8 set.** Cadillac gains a picker-only **Sound v13 · Cadillac** entry after p7. It is a variant of the v12 (v9p) kernel, not a new engine id. p1 stays the default. There is no `?caddy=v13`, `?cadillac=v13` or `?sound=v13` alias — those tokens do not select v13 and do not fall through to kernel v1. All existing v12 Cadillac entries stay on the same drop-down. No new URLs. Defaults unchanged (GT350 v12 p1, Cadillac p1, BRM p11, 911 p7).
* **Generic TMM muffler block.** The time-domain waveguide still models headers, logs, connectors and tails (pulse dynamics and engine coupling). Each muffler or resonator can is a generic transfer-matrix-method two-port: standard 2×2 element matrices (straight tube with visco-thermal loss and mean flow, expansion/contraction, chamber, Helmholtz / side-branch, perforated concentric tube with Sullivan / Lee–Ih perforate impedance, including a louvre end-correction) are cascaded at load time, converted to a causal short FIR (or a stable low-order IIR when that fit is good) for transmission *and* input reflection, then run as a typ-6 node. Passivity is enforced by a global scale if the sampled power gain exceeds 1. Any car can use the block; parameters only; no Cadillac-only DSP. Networks already under the 56-pipe budget stay bit-identical.
* **Cadillac v13** is the v12 Cadillac p2 base (sourced engine and pipes, no H-pipe, 2 in then 1¾ in) with four TMM cans (muffler + resonator per bank) at the sourced p5 lengths (`pipe` 3.07 m between cans, `tail_len` 0.5 m, `cabin_len` 2.19, `cabin_h` 1.11) and the generic p7 `r_ac` of 3900. It builds 22 waveguides, under p2’s 30 and well inside the Tesla/iPhone budget. The 1951 SAE paper “Walker Bulldog” by Edward Cole is about the M41 tank built at Cadillac’s Cleveland Tank Plant, not Walker Manufacturing mufflers; it was not used for dimensions. Wim has not found a cut-open period Cadillac muffler; every geometry value is sourced or marked estimate in the table below.
* **Sources (Walker 21158 TriFlow muffler and 21159 resonator):**
  * 21158 catalog shell: 20¼ in long, oval 3¼ × 7¾ in; 2 in side inlet; 1¾ in centre outlet (Walker catalog).
  * 21158 inner chambers L1/L2/L3 = 8.323 / 7.266 / 4.661 in: digitized from US2182945 Fig. 1 and scaled so the inner-chamber sum equals the 20.25 in catalog shell.
  * 21158 return-tube diameter 1.75 in: **ESTIMATE** (same as the outlet / 1¾ in line).
  * Louver porosity σ = 0.10: **ESTIMATE**. Wall 1.5 mm (existing perforated-can default, about 16 ga). Louver width / hole diameter 0.030 in (0.762 mm) from related Walker US1931736 (US2182945 shows tangential louvers but states no width). Crossover length 0.11 m: **ESTIMATE** from the Fig. 1 bend. Helmholtz neck 1 in: **ESTIMATE**. Gas T = 650 K, Mach 0.08: **ESTIMATE**.
  * 21159 catalog shell: 5⅛ in Ø, 22½ in long. Through-tube 1.75 in: **ESTIMATE** (continuous with the 1¾ in line). Porosity 0.20 and hole diameter 1/8 in (3.175 mm) from the v12 p4 sourced Walker damping. Three chambers with length fractions 0.22 / 0.45 / 0.33: **ESTIMATE**, informed by US3648803 “chambers of various volumes,” not a cut-open 21159. Gas T = 550 K, Mach 0.06: **ESTIMATE**.
  * Patents consulted: US2182945 (three-tube, louvred tubes, resonator chamber); US3141518 (1964, louvred offset inlet, transverse partitions → inlet/expansion/outlet chambers); US3648803 (1969, oval shell pinched around a perforated straight-through, tuned chambers; resonator). US2990028 is a real Walker patent (Walter H. Powers, filed 1 Apr 1955, Ser. No. 498,638, dual-flow transverse muffler) and is not the 21158 TriFlow geometry.
* **Validation** (a check, not a fit; v13 was not retuned to the score). Textbook expansion-chamber TL matches 10 log10(1+0.25(m−1/m)² sin²(kL)) to 0.04 dB MAE (max 0.20 dB); a Helmholtz notch is 59.4 dB at the predicted 136 Hz. 21158 triflow TL at 80/160/320/640/2000 Hz is 3.0 / 7.3 / 29.6 / 44.1 / 27.3 dB; 21159 concentric TL is 8.3 / 13.7 / 19.4 / 24.6 / 28.9 dB. Under-budget networks stay bit-identical with v1.12.13 (Cadillac p1/p2, GT350 p1, 911 p7). v13 is stable and finite at 22.05–96 kHz (22 pipes, four TMM cans, no NaN). Mouth-impulse and blip-release figures are in the release report.
* **Official 4 × 3 scorecard** (comb-recut windows, stored ref_feat, JS p1/p2/v13, engine-only, duration × source-weight; lower is better). The 3,600–5,000 rpm band is empty. On the new dry target, pooled means are p1 3.590, p2 3.498, v13 6.058. Wim noted that Cadillac p2 (the v13 base) is decent at low rpm and really bad at higher rpm; this was checked, not fitted. The TMM cans do **not** improve the high-rpm cells versus p2: dry pooled 2.4–3.6k shut/part/full is p1 3.038 / 2.741 / 2.326, p2 2.298 / 2.262 / 2.430, v13 4.744 / 8.103 / 7.863. v13 is slightly better only in some low-rpm shut cells (dry pooled 0.35–1.2k|shut: p1 4.015, p2 4.033, v13 3.764) and is worse everywhere under part and full throttle, including mid and high rpm. Official Python p1/p2 headlines are cited in the report for context; the p1/p2/v13 comparison above is JS-to-JS on the same windows.
* **CPU / pipes (Chromium desktop, 8 s at 2,000 rpm part throttle):** p1 19.3% of real time, 31 pipes; p2 19.6%, 30 pipes; v13 58.0%, 22 pipes and four TMM cans. No NaN. Worklet sha256 `54899b06328abd2226b822847ecaa6f5659c948cb925cedb6f04c3f3071d37c2`.
* **Per-car lists:**
  * GT350: Sound v1 (procedural), v2 (physical exhaust), v3 (start-up sequence), v4 (pop/bang fix), v12 · GT350 p1 (default).
  * Cadillac: Sound v1–v4, then v12 · Cadillac p1 (default), p2, p3, p4, p5, p6 (test), p7 (picker-only), and Sound v13 · Cadillac (picker-only).
  * BRM: v12 · BRM p1, p2, p3, p4, p6, p8, p9, p10, p11 (default). Not listed: p5, p7, v9.0, v8.
  * 911: v12 · 911 p1–p7 (p7 default). The 911 was never on v1–v4.

## v1.12.13 (tag `v1.12.13`), 2026-11-16 (Cadillac p3–p7 play on a slower CPU; Tesla status line)

* **Cause:** On the Tesla in-car browser, every car and sound model already played except Cadillac p3–p7 (p1 and p2 were fine). Those sets use two Walker perforated cans and build 80–88 waveguides, about 1.6× the CPU of Cadillac p1 (31 pipes). A slower in-car SoC can overrun the AudioWorklet quantum, which Chromium may glitch or mute. Sample-rate renders of p3–p7 at 22.05–96 kHz on a desktop produced no NaN or silent output, so this is a load issue, not a 44.1 kHz / mapping bug. 911 p2 (one perforated can, 46 pipes) already played on the Tesla.
* **Fix:** A generic pipe-count budget (56). Networks already under the budget are unchanged and stay bit-identical (Cadillac p1/p2, the GT350, 911 including p2, the BRM). Only over-budget networks — today Cadillac p3–p7 — are rebuilt with a cheaper generic perforated-can representation (one segment, no 1 mm CFL-clamped end-cap stubs), which cuts the pipe count into the range that already works on the Tesla. If a worklet block goes non-finite, the delay-line network is zeroed and a NaN-reset counter increments; IEEE-754 subnormals in the packing filter are flushed so older CPUs do not stall on denormals. The on-screen Sound status is always visible (no longer hidden behind the hamburger on touch layouts) and shows the page version, worklet mode, pipe count, CPU load, overruns, NaN resets and the last error. The worklet is loaded from an inlined Blob first on every browser (file?v= is a last http fallback); this does not change the DSP. No new URLs; defaults unchanged (GT350 v12 p1, Cadillac p1, BRM p11, 911 p7). Cadillac p7 stays picker-only. No Cadillac-only code.
* **Per-car lists** (unchanged from v1.12.10):
  * GT350: Sound v1 (procedural), v2 (physical exhaust), v3 (start-up sequence), v4 (pop/bang fix), v12 · GT350 p1 (default).
  * Cadillac: Sound v1–v4, then v12 · Cadillac p1 (default), p2, p3, p4, p5, p6 (test), p7 (picker-only).
  * BRM: v12 · BRM p1, p2, p3, p4, p6, p8, p9, p10, p11 (default). Not listed: p5, p7, v9.0, v8.
  * 911: v12 · 911 p1–p7 (p7 default), in numeric order.

## v1.12.12 (tag `v1.12.12`), 2026-11-16 (engine-model switching restored on Safari / iOS / Tesla)

* **Cause:** Two WebKit / Tesla-hostile changes landed after v1.12.5. (1) v1.12.10 rebuilt the single `#selSound` list with `innerHTML = ''` whenever the car changed. iOS Safari and the Tesla in-car browser do not reliably fire `change` (or keep a stable selected value) on a native `<select>` whose options were destroyed and recreated, so picking another engine model appeared to do nothing. (2) v1.12.9 started calling `audioWorklet.addModule('engine-worklet.js?v=…')`. A query string on a worklet URL can throw, hang, or silently fail to register the processor on Safari / WebKit.
* **Fix:** Restore the v1.12.7 pattern: each car has its own `<select>`, filled once at init, and exactly one of those lists is visible. The lists still contain that car's full version history (same entries as v1.12.10, chronological, nothing invented). On Safari, iOS and Tesla, the worklet is loaded from the inlined Blob first; `engine-worklet.js?v=` is kept for desktop Chromium (GitHub Pages cache-bust). No new URLs; defaults unchanged (GT350 v12 p1, Cadillac p1, BRM p11, 911 p7). Cadillac p7 stays picker-only. DSP is unchanged from v1.12.11 (CFL waveguide floor kept).
* **Per-car lists** (unchanged from v1.12.10):
  * GT350: Sound v1 (procedural), v2 (physical exhaust), v3 (start-up sequence), v4 (pop/bang fix), v12 · GT350 p1 (default).
  * Cadillac: Sound v1–v4, then v12 · Cadillac p1 (default), p2, p3, p4, p5, p6 (test), p7 (picker-only).
  * BRM: v12 · BRM p1, p2, p3, p4, p6, p8, p9, p10, p11 (default). Not listed: p5, p7, v9.0, v8.
  * 911: v12 · 911 p1–p7 (p7 default), in numeric order.

## v1.12.11 (tag `v1.12.11`), 2026-11-16 (CFL-correct waveguide length; Cadillac p3–p7 at 44.1 kHz)

* **Cause:** Cadillac p3–p7 (and any other set that uses a perforated muffler can) built 1 mm end-caps and ~4 mm perforate necks. At 44.1 or 48 kHz those lengths are a fraction of a travel sample once the tail gas is warm (`L/c·fs` ≈ 0.10 for a cap, ≈ 0.52 for a neck). The delay slot was clamped to 1 sample, leaving a stiff one-sample closed-end loop whose effective length jumped with sample rate and temperature. p1 and p2 use only long chambers (shortest pipe ≈ 9 samples) and were not affected. The owner heard the starter (same kernel, before the exhaust is hot and fully driven) then silence once the engine should run. Renders of p3–p7 at 44.1 and 48 kHz on this box (V8 / Chromium worklet, including crank → run) were finite and audible, so the blow-up was not reproduced here; the CFL violation was real and is the generic root cause to close. WebKit was not available to test JavaScriptCore on this box. CPU for p3–p7 is about 1.6× p1 (80–88 pipes vs 31) and can underrun a slow in-car CPU, but the fix is the physics length, not a mute or a quality downgrade.
* **Fix (generic, any car, any sample rate):** `buildNet` now takes the worklet `fs` and gives every waveguide a minimum length of one travel sample at a conservative hot exhaust speed (`√(γRT)` at 900 K) or at ambient for intake runners. Caps and necks therefore stay CFL-legal as the gas heats, at 44.1 kHz and at 48 kHz. A junction whose admittance sum is not a usable number is skipped, so a 0/0 cannot latch the network to NaN. Not a sample clamp, not Cadillac-only, no new URLs, defaults unchanged, one sound drop-down per car.
* **Identity:** Cadillac p1 and p2, the GT350, 911 p1 and the BRM are bit-identical to v1.12.10 at both 44.1 and 48 kHz. Cadillac p3–p7 keep their pipe counts and stay audible through crank → run at both rates, with a minimum delay of about 1.35 samples (warm) / 1.64 (cold), not a clamped 1. 911 sets that use a perforated can (p2, p4, p6, p7) get the same generic cap/neck floor (a small level change, about 1 dB on p2, 0.3 dB on p7).

## v1.12.10 (tag `v1.12.10`), 2026-11-16 (one sound drop-down per car, with that car's version history)

* **One sound drop-down per car**, filled with that car's actual version history — every sound model that was developed for that car, in chronological order, each mapped to the real worklet mode. No new URLs. Defaults are unchanged (GT350 v12 p1, Cadillac p1, BRM p11, 911 p7). Cadillac p7 stays picker-only. There is no DSP change; the worklet is loaded as `engine-worklet.js?v=1.12.10`.
* **Cause of the two BRM lists:** the general Sound menu (`#selSound`) was hidden for the 911 and for Cadillac v12, but not for the BRM, so the BRM showed both `#selBrm` and `#selSound`. The leftover per-car `<select>` elements are now always hidden; `#selSound` is the single visible list for every car (and is no longer in the overflow "more" row, so it stays on the top bar).
* **BRM p7:** there is no shipped BRM p7 (and no p5). The picker numbers follow historic v9.N; CHANGELOG records that v9.5 was "no refit, the same numbers as v9.4" and v9.7 was "an evaluation harness, not a fit," and both were "not offered." Those gaps are historical, not a missing row. The retired v8 order-table model is not returned to the list: it was removed from the worklet in v1.11.17 / retired from the menu in v1.11.18. A never-shipped BRM query (`?car=brm&sound=p7`, `?sound=v12.brm.p7`, `?sound=v9.7`) stays on the BRM with default p11 and no longer falls through to kernel v1. A bare `?sound=p7` is not a new alias for BRM, Cadillac or the 911.
* **Per-car lists** (from CHANGELOG / history.js / the shipped registries; nothing invented):
  * GT350: Sound v1 (procedural), v2 (physical exhaust), v3 (start-up sequence), v4 (pop/bang fix), v12 · GT350 p1 (default). No later GT350 p-sets were developed. Game kernels v5–v7 were never in this picker.
  * Cadillac: Sound v1–v4 (same early kernels as the GT350; those cars share the v1–v4 engine modules), then v12 · Cadillac p1 (default), p2, p3, p4, p5, p6 (test), p7 (picker-only).
  * BRM: v12 · BRM p1, p2, p3, p4, p6, p8, p9, p10, p11 (default). Not listed: p5, p7, v9.0, v8.
  * 911: v12 · 911 p1, p2, p3, p4, p5, p6, p7 (default), in numeric (chronological) order. The 911 was never on the v1–v4 kernels. Older names (VarioCam Plus, v10, v10.1, v10.2, fixed cam) are aliases of these p-sets.

# Changelog (engine-web)

The About / History panel in the page is generated from `js/history.js`; add new versions there too.

## v1.12.9 (tag `v1.12.9`), 2026-11-16 (worklet cache-bust; Cadillac p7 audible check)

* **Worklet cache-bust.** `engine-worklet.js` is loaded with a `?v=(title version)` query so a stale GitHub Pages cache cannot keep an old worklet after a new `index.html`. GitHub Pages serves `Cache-Control: max-age=600` and does not honour the repo `_headers` no-cache rule. The DSP, Cadillac p7 (`r_ac = 3900`), the single sound drop-down, and the no-p7-URL rule are unchanged. p1 stays the default. Other cars stay bit-identical.
* **Audible-output audit** (live site https://wimbosmans1.github.io/streetrod/, v1.12.8, fresh Chromium context, analyser RMS with ~0.7 throttle). Every picker entry maps to a real worklet model or parameter set and produces audible engine output (no `voiceErr`, engine running). Cadillac p7 is **p6 + r_ac**, not a missing set: fresh load **−35.6 dB** (p6 **−34.7 dB**, p1 **−25.3 dB**); switching p1→p6→p7: p7 **−31.2 dB** (p6 **−30.9 dB**, p1 **−24.5 dB**). Direct synth at 2000 rpm / 0.4 thr: p7 **−21.2 dB**, p6 **−20.7 dB**, no NaNs, `Q_SNR` 8977. The earlier −50 dB figure was a 4 s **idle** window without throttle, not a mute. Cabin p7 matches p6 (rev about −20 dB). ScriptProcessor fallback also plays p7.

## v1.12.8 (tag `v1.12.8`), 2026-11-16 (one sound drop-down; Cadillac p7 stays a picker variation)

* **UI fix:** the site again has **exactly one sound drop-down**. Cadillac p7 remains one more entry in that list (label **Sound v12 · Cadillac p7**), as p2–p6 already were. There is still no new p7 URL; existing p1–p6 links still work; p1 stays the default. The GT350, 911 and BRM sounds and their pickers are unchanged.
* **Cause:** v1.12.7 did not add a second `<select>`. It only added a p7 option to the existing Cadillac picker (`#selCaddy`), which has been in the page since v1.12.3. That picker never received the `#segCaddySound.hidden { display: none; }` rule that `#segBrmSound` and `#segP997Sound` have. `.sel { display: inline-flex }` therefore kept it visible on every car — including the GT350 default — even when the `hidden` class was set. With p7 picker-only, both lists were in the way.
* **Fix:** add `#segCaddySound.hidden { display: none; }`, and hide the general Sound list (`#segSound`) while the Cadillac v12 picker is shown, the same pattern as the 911. The picker can select p7 (`caddyName(..., true)`); URL aliases `?caddy=p7`, `?cadillac=p7` and `?sound=v12.cadillac.p7` still fall back to Cadillac p1.

## v1.12.7 (tag `v1.12.7`), 2026-11-16 (Cadillac picker variation: v12 · Cadillac p7)

* **v12 · Cadillac p7** is a new non-default **picker variation**. It is selected from the Cadillac sound picker (label **Sound v12 · Cadillac p7**). There is no new URL or query alias (`?sound=`, `?caddy=`, `?cadillac=` are unchanged). Existing p1–p6 links still work. p1 stays the default. The GT350, 911 and BRM sounds are bit-identical to v1.12.6. No Cadillac-only code path, no echo EQ, no change to `k_int`, `ro_loss` or the open-end radiation. The 3.07 m connector is not split.
* p7 is **p6 plus a generic air-cleaner element flow resistance** in the existing lumped intake circuit (plenum–throttle–cleaner–snorkel).
  * The new generic parameter is `r_ac` (Pa·s/m³). Default is 0: the snorkel resistance stays the previous 0.1 ρc/A radiation term only, so any car that omits `r_ac` stays bit-identical. When set, it is added in series on the snorkel path (`Q_SNR`).
  * The 1959 Cadillac as fitted used a **dry-pack paper element**, not an oil-bath: official 1959 Cadillac specifications (brochure back cover), “dry-pack type air cleaner; intake silencer” (http://www2.cadillac.cz/zajimavosti/brochure1959/15.htm). That is the same dry-pack type already used for the p2 geometry (`v_ac`, `sn_len`, `sn_d`).
  * **r_ac = 3900 Pa·s/m³** is the linearised ΔP/Q of a typical clean paper element. Toma, Anghelache and Moisescu, “Replacement Period Evaluation of Petrol Engines Air Filters Based on Restriction Measurement,” WSEAS ACMOS, 2014, report SAE J726 measurements presented in Zemaitis, SAE Technical Paper 980868 (1998): classic paper filters in new condition, 550–750 Pa at 600 m³/h, element only (no casing). Midpoint 650 Pa / (600/3600 m³/s) = **3900 Pa·s/m³**.
* **Neutral blip-and-release check** (cabin, same handed game-like coast as the p6 overrun diagnosis: about 2.8 s from ~2740 to 515 rpm, throttle closed). Decay RMS **0.00406 → 0.00291** (−2.9 dB). The ~118 Hz air-cleaner Helmholtz band (108 / 118 / 128 Hz) is **3.0–3.2 dB** lower (118 Hz mean −75.2 → −78.4 dB). The 65 / 76 Hz exhaust formants, which are not the intake element, move by only −0.5 / −1.0 dB. `k_int` stays 2.0; `Q_SNR` is 5077 + 3900 = 8977 Pa·s/m³.
* **Dry-target score** (same 168.9 s pipeline as v1.12.4–v1.12.6; lower is better). p7 was not fitted. Cabin 5.788, outside far 3.310, engine bay 5.304, tailpipe 3.331, **all paths 5.249** (p6 5.634, p5 4.756, p1 3.669). Every dry path is a little closer than p6; p1 remains the better official score and stays the default. Old official all 5.681 (p6 6.077); OK only 5.628 (p6 6.027). UNSURE cabins 5.442 (p6 5.826).
* **CPU:** waveguide pipes still 88, same as p6 (`nseg` stays 3). Chromium desktop, 8 s at 2,000 rpm, 48 kHz, same method as v1.12.6: p1 17.9 % of real time, p6 27.8 %, p7 27.9 %. p7 sits with p6. (WebKit was not available in the build environment.)

## v1.12.6 (tag `v1.12.6`), 2026-11-15 (Cadillac diagnostic test: v12 · Cadillac p6)

* **v12 · Cadillac p6 (test)** (`?caddy=p6`, `?cadillac=p6`, `?sound=v12.cadillac.p6` / `v12.caddy.p6`, or `?car=caddy&sound=p6`) is a new non-default **diagnostic** set, marked as a test in the picker. It is not a candidate default. p1 stays the default. The GT350, 911 and BRM sounds are bit-identical to v1.12.5. No Cadillac-only code path, no echo EQ, no change to `ro_loss` or the open-end radiation.
* p6 is **p5 with the Walker muffler and resonator internal chambers and perforates made strongly lossy**, using only the existing generic `pack` (cavity volume and perforate annulus) and `pack_n` (perforate-neck resistance) terms. Inlet and outlet tubes, the 3.07 m muffler-to-resonator connector, the 0.50 m tail, cabin dimensions, resonator porosity, the manifolds and idle are unchanged.
  * On each of the three crossover / Helmholtz `cavity` blocks, the cavity-volume `pack` is raised from 0 to **2.0**.
  * On the 21158 perforated return and the 21159 resonator `perf` blocks, annular `pack` is raised from 0 to **2.0** and `pack_n` from 0.13 to **2.0**.
  * These values are a diagnostic choice, not sourced and not fitted. With the shipped `pack_r` = 1.0, each pass through those packed segments is attenuated by about *e*<sup>−2</sup> ≈ 0.14. The point is to hear whether the standing waves in p5 come from those internals.
* **Impulse check** (unit pulse into cylinder 0's primary, mouth volume velocity; valves held shut; |U| relative to the first arrival, same extractor as v1.12.5). First arrival is essentially unchanged (p5 18.39 ms, p6 18.55 ms); pipe count stays 88.
  * 2–6 ms: p5 2.20 ms 1.50, 3.51 ms 1.60, 4.90 ms **1.99** (and 6.21 ms 1.86 just outside this window). p6 2.68 ms **0.41**, 4.24 ms 0.23. Max in the window **1.99 → 0.41**.
  * 10–20 ms: p5 up to **0.52**; p6 up to **0.25**.
  * The strong 2–6 ms cluster collapses when only the muffler/resonator internals are made lossy, so those standing waves are confirmed as coming from the Walker chambers and perforates. A weaker residual (1.36 ms at 0.62, 2.68 ms at 0.41) remains; those lengths were not retouched (through-tubes, tees, connector, tail).
* **Dry-target score** (same 168.9 s pipeline as v1.12.4/v1.12.5; lower is better). p6 was not fitted. Cabin 6.183, outside far 3.654, engine bay 5.620, tailpipe 3.687, **all paths 5.634** (p5 4.756, p1 3.669, p2 3.608). The cabin is worse; the tailpipe and outside-far paths are closer. Old official all 6.077 (p5 5.390); OK only 6.027 (p5 5.035). UNSURE cabins 5.826 (p5 4.574). p6 is a diagnostic, not a score candidate; p1 stays the default.
* **CPU:** waveguide pipes still 88, same as p5 (`nseg` stays 3). Chromium desktop, 8 s at 2,000 rpm, 48 kHz, same method as v1.12.5: p1 18.0 % of real time, p5 28.0 %, p6 29.0 %. p6 sits with p5. (WebKit was not available in the build environment.)

## v1.12.5 (tag `v1.12.5`), 2026-11-15 (Cadillac listening test: v12 · Cadillac p5)

* **v12 · Cadillac p5** (`?caddy=p5`, `?cadillac=p5`, `?sound=v12.cadillac.p5` / `v12.caddy.p5`, or `?car=caddy&sound=p5`) is a new non-default listening set. p1 stays the default. The GT350, 911 and BRM sounds are bit-identical to v1.12.4. No Cadillac-only code path, no echo EQ, no change to `ro_loss` or the open-end radiation. Resonator porosity, the manifolds and idle are unchanged.
* p5 is **p4 plus two sourced exhaust lengths and the sourced cabin dimensions**. Walker 21158 TriFlow / 21159 Direct Flow geometry, `pack_n`, cavity damping, header-to-muffler `inter_len` and mouth positions stay as in p4.
  * **Muffler-to-resonator connector 3.07 m × 1¾ in** (p4 used a 0.35 m p1 length carry-over). This is the developed centreline of the two-section intermediate pipe (front + rear intermediate) on the 1959 Cadillac shop-manual Fig. 10-23 (p. 10-15), one bank. Length scale from the 1959 AMA Specifications L103 overall 225.0 in; width scale from AMA W103 80.2 in (the drawing is not isotropic; scale ratio ≈ 1.40). The path develops to 120.7 in = 3.07 m (horizontal span 98.7 in = 2.51 m). Uncertainty ±15 % (±0.46 m). Cross-checks: if the pale rear ovals are the axle, drawn overhang is 62.4 in vs AMA L105 60.5 in (3 %); drawn muffler shell ≈ 21.5 in vs Walker 20.25 in (6 %). The resonator can is drawn oversized vs Walker 22.5 in and was not used as a scale.
  * **Tail (outlet pipe) 0.50 m × 1¾ in** (p4 used 0.60 m, also a p1 length carry-over). The drawn resonator can would crush the bumper gap, so the resonator inlet is held at the drawn station and the sourced Walker 21159 overall length 27.125 in is placed toward the bumper, leaving ≈ 10 in horizontal (the drawn can would leave ≈ 5 in). Plus a 90° turndown from the shop-manual text on p. 10-14, “The outer pipe directs the flow of exhaust gases down and away from the rear of the car”: centreline radius ≈ 3 in on 1¾ in pipe, quarter-circle 4.7 in, stub ≈ 5 in, total ≈ 19.7 in = 0.50 m. Uncertainty ±0.23 m. p4's 0.60 m is inside that band; the central sourced value is used. Waldron's 1958–60 kit is listed with turndowns; the Swiss Veteran clips still show factory bumper-pod exits — a turndown into or at the pod is the reconciliation.
  * **Cabin length 2.19 m, cabin height 1.11 m** (p4/p1 were 2.30 m / 1.05 m). From the 1959 Cadillac AMA Specifications (issued 10-1-58) for body styles 6229/6329 (Series 62 and Sedan DeVille six-window): (L4 45.6 + L3 31.4)×cos(15°) = 74.3 in toeboard-to-rear-seatback, plus 12 in package-shelf/backlight (AMA LD is blank; ±6 in) → 86.3 in = 2.19 m, uncertainty ±0.15 m. Height: H3 10.0 + H1 34.8×cos(15°) = 43.6 in = 1.11 m, uncertainty ±0.05 m. Sixty Special 6029, same 130 in wheelbase in 1959: 85.5 in / 43.3 in — not a separate generic cabin. Cabin width is not used for a mode. First longitudinal mode 74.6 → 78.3 Hz; first vertical mode 163.3 → 154.5 Hz.
  * Generic `SET_CAR_KEYS` now also overlays `cabin_len` and `cabin_h` (they were previously only on the base car). No other set uses those keys.
* **Impulse check** (unit pulse into cylinder 0's primary, mouth volume velocity; valves held shut; not a fitting target). First arrival is 18.39 ms on p5 against 12.13 ms on p4 (Δ +6.26 ms), which matches the extra 2.72 m of intermediate pipe at about 435 m/s. Early reflections, as |U| relative to the first arrival (same extractor on both sets; this is not the v1.12.4 locked-coefficient scale, whose raw |U| amplitudes are ~3×10⁻⁸ and round to zero):
  * p4: 2.18 ms 1.53, 3.51 ms 1.85, 4.90 ms 2.26, 6.21 ms **2.39**, 8.46 ms 1.39; 10–20 ms train up to 1.04.
  * p5: 2.20 ms 1.50, 3.51 ms 1.60, 4.90 ms **1.99**, 6.21 ms 1.86, 7.55 ms 0.94; 10–20 ms train up to 0.52.
  * The 2–6 ms muffler-internal reflections remain (those lengths were not changed). The 10–20 ms train is about half as strong. The short ~3.7 ms round trip of the 0.35 m connector is replaced by a ~13.6 ms / ~73 Hz round trip of the 3.07 m intermediate. Pipe count stays 88 (`nseg` stays 3).
* **Order balance** at the trusted clips (dB vs order 4; model minus the recording). Cabin 975 rpm: order 1 drops 7.6 dB vs p4 (still +19.8 vs real, was +27.4). Cabin 1,139 rpm: order 1 −6.3 dB, orders 0.5 and 8 −8.8 dB vs p4 (order 8 still +11.5 vs real, was +20.2). Cabin 1,406 rpm: order 1 −4.4, 0.5 −5.4, 2 −7.5, 8 −3.7 dB vs p4. Tailpipe 827 rpm is mixed and worse on the low orders (0.5 +10.2, 2 +4.6 vs p4). The 1,966 rpm tail blip is close to p4; order 2 moves from −7.5 to −0.5 vs real.
* **Scores** on the same new dry target and old official windows as v1.12.4 (326-window pipeline, engine-only, duration × source-weight). p5 was not fitted to the score. Lower is better. p5 is closer than p4 on both headlines, and is essentially with p3 on the dry target; it is still not closer than p1 or p2. p1 stays the default until the owner listens.

## v1.12.4 (tag `v1.12.4`), 2026-11-15 (Cadillac listening test: v12 · Cadillac p4; retargeted scorecard)

* **v12 · Cadillac p4** (`?caddy=p4`, `?cadillac=p4`, `?sound=v12.cadillac.p4` / `v12.caddy.p4`, or `?car=caddy&sound=p4`) is a new non-default listening set. p1 stays the default. The GT350, 911 and BRM sounds are bit-identical to v1.12.3. No Cadillac-only code path, no echo EQ, no change to `ro_loss` or the open-end radiation.
* p4 keeps **p3's Walker 21158 TriFlow + 21159 Direct Flow geometry** (sourced shells, equal-quarter partition **estimate**, porosity 0.20 **estimate**, Helmholtz neck **estimate**, 0.35 m connector and 0.60 m tail as p1 length carry-overs — all as in v1.12.3) and adds damping taken from how those mufflers are built, using only the existing generic `pack` / `pack_r` / perforate / cavity terms.
  * **No fibrous packing.** Tenneco/Walker US 3,841,435 and US 3,498,406 describe a reactive three-tube TriFlow (partitions, one perforated return, optional tuning tube) and do not mention packing. Walker's "Containing Sound" page classifies louvered tubes as reactive devices and packing as a separate absorptive class (glasspacks). Some later Quiet-Flow listings mention CRF (Continuous Roving Fiberglass); 21158 is catalogued as TriFlow, 21159 as Direct Flow, so CRF is not applied. Annular `pack` stays 0.
  * **Perforate / louver neck resistance (`pack_n` = 0.13, estimate).** p3 left the generic perforate necks lossless (`pack_n` = 0). The necks already use a Munjal-type length `t_wall + 0.85 d_hole`. `pack_n` is the existing resistive term on those necks, in nepers per pass (`pack_r` stays the shipped 1.0). The number is converted from Bauer (1977) grazing-flow resistance 0.3 *M / σ, plus a nonlinear orifice term at 165 dB (the mid-point of the commonly cited 160–170 dB in-duct exhaust range), porosity 0.20, *C<sub>d</sub>* = 0.7, 2,000 rpm through one 1¾ in pipe, *c* = 450 m/s: open-area resistance θ ≈ 0.28, amplitude transmission 2/(2+θ), pack_n = −ln(T) ≈ 0.13. It was not fitted to the impulse or the score.
  * **Wall and hole estimates** on the perforate items, now stated explicitly (they were already the generic defaults): *t_wall* = 1.5 mm (16–18 ga muffler tube). *d_hole* = ⅛ in (3.175 mm), a typical perforation / louver width; the patent says only “perforate.”
  * **Crossover volumes as generic cavities, not series chambers.** The two p3 series chambers (same sourced length 5.06 in and equivalent diameter 127.5 mm) are recast as `cavity` blocks — a through tube with the can teed off it, the kernel's existing model for a reverse-flow / crossover volume (already used for the p3 Helmholtz end chamber). US 3,841,435 has the inlet opening into a crossover chamber that also feeds the return path. A series chamber is a two-port expansion with *R* = (*m*−1)/(*m*+1) = 0.73, which pack terms cannot remove and which was the first 0.84 impulse peak on p3. Neck diameter is the tube that opens into that chamber (2 in, then 1¾ in). Neck length 2 in and through-pipe 2 cm are the same estimates already used for the p3 Helmholtz cavity (no 21158 cutaway). The cavity through-pipe still uses the kernel's `pipe_d` (2 in), as in p3. Cavity packing remains 0.
* **Impulse check** (unit pulse into cylinder 0's primary, mouth volume velocity; valves held shut; not a fitting target). Early reflection amplitudes relative to the first arrival:
  * p3: 2.13 ms **0.84**, 3.67 ms 0.75, 5.26 ms 0.66, 7.17 ms 0.51, and a 11.86 ms revival at 0.51 (five peaks ≥ 0.50).
  * p4: 2.00 ms **0.36**, 3.74 ms 0.63, 5.60 ms 0.41, 7.28 ms 0.44, 12.65 ms revival **0.18** (one peak ≥ 0.50). The long flutter is in the GT350's range (12.81 ms at 0.16). The remaining 3.74 ms peak at 0.63 is still higher than the GT350's strongest early peak (0.38); it was not tuned away.
  * p1 (unchanged): strongest early 8.84 ms at 0.46. GT350: 4.15 ms at 0.38. p4 pipe count is 88 against p3 80 and p1 31 (`nseg` stays 3).
* **Retargeted Cadillac scorecard.** Same comb-recut windows and engine-only, duration × source-weight pipeline as v1.12.3. The new **dry** target drops qrEDQsa1hQg (owner description: left exhaust pipe cut/broken, fumes escaping mid-pipe), and excludes garage/showroom clips bAACfEqe0UM, 49daHSndAho and IoaRMvA-q-c as reverberant recordings. It keeps TaRrrTHbA7Y and fQibKpC2FQw (outdoor Fleetwood / Sixty Special cabins), vH2GqPSC0bE and MC-3iBZsXL4 (Swiss Veteran tailpipe, factory bumper-pod exits), vGGagYHqzfU (club Sedan DeVille, 390 / 325 hp, outside far) and ADXzj7xFcnk (outdoor stock-looking 390 bay, 0.5 s). The two UNSURE cabins (4M0RfqxLmIk, a CPR convertible with “much maintenance”; T-hP31fKwOY, undescribed) are reported separately at their official 0.5 weight and are not in the dry headline. F_Vt2OraBBI (weak/unknown outdoor) is also out of the dry target. The 3,600–5,000 rpm band remains empty and was not tuned. p1, p2, p3 and p4 were scored on both the old official windows and the new dry target; those figures are in the Scorecard Page and below. The published v1.12.2 v9p headlines (all 3.008 / OK 2.557) stay as the historical official v9p vs v2 card; this pipeline is for comparing the Cadillac p-sets with each other.

## v1.12.3 (tag `v1.12.3`), 2026-11-15 (Cadillac listening tests: v12 · Cadillac p2 and p3)

* **Two new, non-default Cadillac listening sets.** p1 stays the default. The GT350, 911 and BRM sounds are bit-identical to v1.12.2. The kernel changes are generic (a parameter set may overlay car geometry; optional intake/exhaust cam fields; optional `tail_d`) and do nothing unless a set uses them.
* **v12 · Cadillac p2** (`?caddy=p2`, `?cadillac=p2`, `?sound=v12.cadillac.p2` / `v12.caddy.p2`, or `?car=caddy&sound=p2`): sourced shop-manual and period-spec fixes, **same muffler chambers as p1** so that change can be heard by itself.
  * Connecting rod 6.50 in (1959–60 shop manual; p1 was 6.625 in).
  * Exhaust valve 1.50 in, lift 0.450 in (shop manual; p1 was 1.56 in / 0.40 in). Intake valves are 1.875 in / 0.450 in (visualisation already had these).
  * Cam timing from the shop manual (without ramp): IO 27° BTDC, IC 75° ABDC, EO 73° BBDC, EC 29° ATDC, encoded as intake advertised 282° / LSA 114° and exhaust advertised 282° / LSA 112°.
  * Dry-pack air cleaner instead of the oil-bath guess (1959 fact sheet / AMA: dry-pack + intake silencer). Element sizes from Belcher AF26 / equivalent 1959–60 Series 62 listings: height 2.19 in, ID 14.94 in, OD 17.31/18.31 in. Clean volume uses those plus an **estimated** 1.0 in lid plenum → 9.16 L (p1 was 9.0 L). Intake-silencer neck dimensions were not found: **estimate** 4 in diameter × 4 in long (shorter and wider than the oil-bath snorkel 75 mm × 120 mm).
  * True dual exhaust: the H-pipe is removed (reproduction 1957–60 Cadillac systems list no crossover). Pipes 2 in from the manifold to the muffler, 1¾ in after (forum / period system lists). The 0.35 m inter-muffler pipe in the p1 list is now 1¾ in; the tail is 1¾ in. The 0.30 m × 1.5 in *internal* muffler tube is unchanged.
  * Idle stays 520 rpm. The AMA figure is 450 rpm in Drive; Neutral idle is not clearly sourced.
* **v12 · Cadillac p3** (`?caddy=p3`, `?sound=v12.cadillac.p3`, …) is p2 plus the muffler and resonator rebuilt from Walker factory-replacement dimensions, using the existing generic pipe, chamber, cavity and perforated-tube blocks (no Cadillac-only code path).
  * Walker 21158 TriFlow (sourced shell): oval 3¼ × 7¾ in, shell 20¼ in, 2 in side inlet / 1¾ in centre outlet. Equivalent diameter 127.5 mm, area ratio vs 2 in pipe 6.30.
  * Internal layout from Tenneco/Walker US 3,841,435 (three-tube, four-chamber TriFlow): imperforate inlet tube to the far crossover chamber, perforated return, near crossover, imperforate outlet, Helmholtz chamber at the outlet end. **Estimate:** no 21158 cutaway or partition lengths were found, so the four chambers are equal quarters of the sourced 20¼ in shell (each 5.06 in / 0.129 m). Inlet and outlet tubes are taken as three quarters long; the return as two quarters.
  * Perforated return: 1¾ in tube in the oval shell, **porosity 0.20 (estimate;** typical louvered open area, no open-area spec), packing 0 (none mentioned). `nseg=3` is a cost choice, not a sourced partition.
  * Helmholtz neck **estimate:** 1.5 in × 2 in partition stub (patent tube 43, no 21158 dimensions). Cavity volume is one estimated quarter of the oval shell (1.64 L). At 500 K that Helmholtz is about 206 Hz. The cavity through-pipe uses the kernel’s `pipe_d` (2 in) on a short 2 cm section; the 1¾ in system resumes at the following connector.
  * Walker 21159 Direct Flow resonator (sourced shell): 5⅛ in Ø, shell 22½ in, 1¾ in straight through. Modelled as a perforated straight-through can, porosity 0.20 **(estimate)**, packing 0, `nseg=3`.
  * The 0.35 m muffler-to-resonator connector and the 0.60 m tail are **p1 length carry-overs** (not in the Walker shell data); both are 1¾ in.
* A Cadillac sound picker is shown when the Cadillac is on sound model v12: **Sound v12 · Cadillac p1 (default)**, **p2**, **p3**. Those `?sound=` / `?caddy=` / `?cadillac=` links open the Cadillac on the chosen set. Bare `?sound=p2` is unchanged (it is still a BRM / 911 alias, not Cadillac).
* **Official scorecard** (comb-recut of 14 Nov 2026, 326 windows, 422.2 s all / 210.0 s OK, engine-only, duration × source-weight; the 3,600–5,000 rpm band is empty and was not tuned). Lower is better. p1, p2 and p3 were scored on the same pipeline this release. That pipeline’s p1 is not identical to the published v1.12.2 v9p figures (all 3.008 / OK 2.557) — likely a later kernel revision plus hop alignment — so the numbers below are for comparing the three Cadillac sets with each other, not for rewriting the published v9p headline.

  All (422.2 s):

  | Set | Overall | Cabin | Outside far | Engine bay | Tailpipe |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | p1 | 4.402 | 3.435 | 7.086 | 5.189 | 4.647 |
  | p2 | **4.355** | **3.419** | **6.873** | 5.704 | **4.299** |
  | p3 | 5.460 | 4.496 | 7.996 | 6.575 | 5.870 |

  OK only (210.0 s):

  | Set | Overall | Cabin | Outside far | Engine bay | Tailpipe |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | p1 | 3.836 | 3.462 | 4.760 | 5.189 | 4.647 |
  | p2 | **3.817** | **3.440** | **4.543** | 5.704 | **4.299** |
  | p3 | 4.957 | 4.548 | 5.780 | 6.575 | 5.870 |

  Feature gaps on representative official windows (model − recording; `hf_db` / `low_db`): cabin and tailpipe are still too dark in the highs. p2 is close to p1 (tailpipe part-throttle blip `hf_db` −24.2 vs p1 −25.1). p3 is darker still (same blip −34.9, cabin part −30.4 vs p1 −24.7). Low-band level on the blip stays nearly matched for all three. Cabin idle stays too dark; p3 more so. Idle in the game is a few dB quieter on p2 than p1, and quieter again on p3.
* **CPU:** waveguide pipes p1 31, p2 30, p3 80 (`nseg=3` on both perforated cans). Chromium desktop, 8 s at 2,000 rpm, 48 kHz: p1 18.1 % of real time, p2 18.6 %, p3 27.5 %. p3 is heavier than p1 but still well under real time on desktop; it is a listening test, not the default. (WebKit was not available in the build environment.)


## v1.12.2 (tag `v1.12.2`), 2026-11-15 (911 default is now v12 · 911 p7)

* **The 911 default is now v12 · 911 p7**, chosen by the owner by ear. `?car=p997` opens p7, and unknown `?p997=` values fall back to p7. p4 (the default from v1.11.42 to v1.12.1) and p1, p2, p3, p5, p6 stay selectable, with all their aliases.
* No sound changes: `engine-worklet.js` is byte-identical to v1.12.1, so every car and set sounds the same.
* **Official scorecard** (round-6 stock-OK windows: 4 rpm bins × part / full / shut over cabin, outside rear and tailpipe; pooled weighted feature distance, lower is better; Python v8phys, same windows for every set):

  | Set | Overall | Cabin | Outside rear | Tailpipe |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | p1 | 3.067 | 2.796 | 3.507 | 3.840 |
  | p2 | 4.661 | 4.956 | 4.212 | 3.225 |
  | p3 | **2.925** | **2.534** | 3.552 | 4.154 |
  | p4 | 3.290 | 3.261 | 3.301 | 4.095 |
  | p5 | 3.060 | 2.890 | 3.261 | 5.053 |
  | p6 | 3.377 | 3.313 | 3.458 | 4.024 |
  | p7 | 2.959 | 2.767 | **3.211** | 4.704 |

  * p7 is second overall, behind p3; p3 was rejected by ear.
  * p7 is better than p4 by 0.33 overall, and better than p1 in the cabin and outside.
  * In the tailpipe, p7 is worse than both (4.70 against p4 4.10 and p1 3.84). That comes from its idle / low cell (4.98) and the 5–7.3k cell.
* **Per-clip checks (Rounds 6–11).** Steady renders at 2k / 3k / 4k part and full, cabin and outside, against the recordings per clip. Values are the mean absolute error in dB.
  * **Tilt:** p7 6.1, p5 6.1, p3 6.3, p1 8.3, p4 9.8, p6 10.7, p2 21.1.
  * **Order 3:** p3 4.1, p6 5.6, p4 5.7, p7 5.7, p2 6.1, p1 6.9, p5 7.3.
  * **Order 4.5:** p4 2.9, p7 2.9, p2 4.3, p6 4.7, p1 6.1, p5 6.1, p3 6.5.
  * **Not fixed:** cabin level at 2k / 3k full relative to 4k: recordings −2.2 / −1.4 dB (only 2 clips), p7 −8.0 / −9.6, p4 −8.4 / −9.0, p1 −0.9 / +6.3. p7 is still too quiet at low and mid rpm.
* **Caveat:** the old pooled cabin target of "+4.2 dB order 3 at 5–6k (rel. 3,500)" is skewed by one clip and by closed-throttle windows. Taken per clip it is −1.1 dB, so the order-3 shape numbers are only a guide.

## v1.12.1 (tag `v1.12.1`), 2026-11-15 (911 listening test: v12 · 911 p7 = p4 with internal packing)

* New, **non-default** option **Sound v12 · 911 p7** (`?p997=p7`, also `v12.911.p7` / `v11.911.p7`). The owner approved it as the last try at the low and mid-range grunt.
* **What is packed, and where:** a 996 side-box gut job (Rennlist forum, 996 "gut job for sound / performance" thread) describes a steel-wool-packed primary silencer can, then a large perforated chamber lined with fibreglass, then a perforated top chamber and the exit tube. Mapped onto the p4 box:
  * the 62 mm inlet tube, as the primary can, gets packing 0.2;
  * the middle chamber B, the large chamber where both bellmouths sit, is lined with packing 0.3;
  * chambers A and C, and the end-cap wool, are unchanged.
  * Only existing generic packing parameters are used (`pipe` / `cavity`). Both values are estimates.
  * Caveat: the 997 cut-away photo and the Fabspeed video show wool only on the end cap.
* **Variants tried (4 renders):**

  | Variant | Packing | Result |
  |---|---|---|
  | k1 | B 0.3, inlet 0.2 | Chosen |
  | k2 | B 0.5, inlet 0.3 | Cabin order 4.5 drops by 15 dB at 6,800 rpm, so the high note is lost in the cabin |
  | k3 | B 0.3 only | Darkens less |
  | k4 | B 0.5, inlet 0.2 | Note weaker than k1, no better tilt |

  p7 = k1.
* **Results**, steady renders against the recordings (per clip, then the median over clips). Tilt is 1–4 kHz minus 40–300 Hz; real cabin / outside values from Round 10.
  * **Tilt at 2,000–4,000 rpm, cabin:**

    | Point | p4 | p7 | Real |
    |---|---|---|---|
    | 2k full | −21.5 | −25.4 | −34.7 |
    | 3k part | −10.1 | −15.7 | −22.6 |
    | 3k full | −10.9 | −14.1 | −31.4 |
    | 4k part | −17.2 | −26.1 | −25.0 |
    | 4k full | −19.3 | −24.7 | −23.8 |

  * **Tilt, outside:**

    | Point | p4 | p7 | Real |
    |---|---|---|---|
    | 2k part | −15.4 | −14.1 | −11.1 |
    | 3k part | −5.7 | −9.9 | −18.0 |

  * Every cell moves toward the recordings, by 1–9 dB, except cabin 4k (part and full), where p7 now passes the recordings by about 1 dB. The balance is still brighter than the recordings at 2,000–3,000 rpm.
  * 2–8 kHz (harshness) is 10–12 dB lower than p4 everywhere.
  * Order 3 relative to the total is unchanged to +1 dB.
  * **Not fixed:** the level against rpm. The 40–4,000 Hz level at idle to 4,000 rpm is the same as p4 in the cabin (−2 to +0.2 dB) and 1–3 dB lower outside. So the low end is not louder, only less masked.
  * **High note:** outside, order 4.5 at 6,800–7,200 rpm holds its level relative to the total, but the total is 3–5 dB lower. In the cabin, order 4.5 is about 3 dB weaker relative to the total.
  * No new narrow tones: the strongest non-order line is 6 dB lower than in p4.
* **Checks:**
  * The GT350, Cadillac and 911 default / p1–p6 are bit-identical to v1.12.0 (54 renders); the BRM is untouched.
  * Python v8phys p7 matches JS: the p7 − p4 order deltas agree within 0.8 dB (2,500–6,500 rpm, both views, full throttle).
  * Safari-engine (JSC, full-worklet harness) cost per block: mean 19.0–19.2 % for p7 against 18.9 % for p4 and 21.9 % for the GT350. The worst block is at key-on (about 2.5 quanta of work, the same as p4). One run showed a single 351 % block mid-run; two repeat runs did not, so I take it for a JIT / GC blip, not p7.
  * Cold start with 6× CPU throttling in headless Chrome works; p7 starts and runs without errors.

## v1.12.0 (tag `v1.12.0`), 2026-11-15 (sound model renamed v12; sound bit-identical; 911 low-end diagnosis, no p7)

* **Sound model v11 is renamed v12** for every car, because the kernel has new parts since v1.11.39: the `par` (parallel paths) and `cavity` (side cavity, with an optional absorbent end-cap layer) muffler blocks, and the v1.11.43 kernel split.
  * Labels: Sound v12 · 911 p1–p6 (p4 default), v12 · BRM p1–p11 (p11 default), v12 · p1 for the GT350 and Cadillac. Also updated: the info panel, Specs and the History intro.
  * Every p number and parameter set is unchanged. Old query aliases (`?p997=v11.911.p4`, `v11p4`, `?sound=v11.brm.p11`, `v11.p1`, …) still work. The same forms with `v12` work too.
  * The worklet (`engine-worklet.js`) is byte-identical to v1.11.43, so the sound is bit-identical for every car and set.
* **911 "missing grunt" (owner, p4 at low and mid rpm): diagnosed, not fixed, so no p7.**
  * Method: steady renders of p1 and p4 at idle and at 2,000 / 3,000 / 4,000 rpm, part (0.3) and full throttle, cabin and outside. Recordings: per clip, then the median over clips, same windows as the exhaust checkpoint. Levels are relative to 40–4,000 Hz because of YouTube AGC.
  * p4 is 12–17 dB quieter than p1 from idle to 3,000 rpm and the same at 4,000. Its spectrum is too bright: 1–4 kHz minus 40–300 Hz at 3,000 rpm is −10 / −11 dB in the cabin (part / full) against about −23 / −31 in the recordings, and −6 dB outside (part) against −18. Its 300–1,000 Hz band is 4–9 dB too strong relative to the total.
  * In the only two recordings with full-throttle windows at both rpm (cabin only, AGC-affected), 2,000 and 3,000 rpm full throttle sit 2 dB below 4,000. In p4 they sit 8–9 dB below.
  * Stage trace by ablation:
    * Removing the box raises p4 by about 20 dB at idle to 3,000 rpm and 13 dB at 4,000. At 3,000 rpm full throttle, order 3 is about 28 dB louder without it.
    * Removing the end-cap wool raises the level 4–10 dB but makes it brighter still.
    * In the cabin, the intake supplies about +10 dB at 4,000 rpm, the resonance-flap band, and little at 2,000–3,000.
    * So the low/mid loss is the side box acting at low frequency as a volume (about 14 L) closed by the outlet path (0.16 + 0.25 + 0.12 m). Its corner is about 50 Hz, falling about 12 dB/oct, which hits orders 3–6 at 1,500–4,000 rpm. Meanwhile 300 Hz–4 kHz passes nearly unabsorbed.
  * Physically justified tweaks within the photo estimates, rendered:
    * Box depth 190 → 160 mm, i.e. the photo's m 15 and net volume after the tubes and double wall: +0–2 dB low end, but just as much brighter.
    * Plus outlet 0.25 → 0.15 m: +2–4 dB low end, but order 4.5 jumps by 10–17 dB at 6,800–7,200 rpm, which moves the high note.
    * Neither closes the 6–12 dB gap, so neither ships.
  * What it would take:
    * Sourced internal data for the 997 S box. One 996 source describes a fibreglass-lined chamber and a steel-wool primary can, which would absorb 0.3–4 kHz while passing the bass. The cut-away photo and the Fabspeed video show wool only on the end cap.
    * Possibly the PSE bypass, if the reference cars have it.
    * Calibrated (non-AGC) level-vs-rpm recordings.
    * Then a 2–3 parameter p7 with packing where the source shows it.

## v1.11.43 (tag `v1.11.43`), 2026-11-15 (sound dropouts on iPhone: kernel reaches Safari's top JIT tier; 911 start-up spike removed)

* **Symptom (owner, iPhone / Safari):** the sound cut out while playing the 911 (p4 / p6), not with the GT350.
* **Cause 1, all v11 cars, worst for the 911 sets with side boxes:** in JavaScriptCore, the JavaScript engine in Safari, the per-sample kernel `kv8` was one function of about 38,000 bytecode units. It never left the middle compiler tier (DFG) for the top tier (FTL); bun / JSC reports this. The cost per 128-sample block, as a share of the 2.67 ms real-time budget, in a full worklet harness on this box (`deploy/engine-worklet.js`, game controls at 60 Hz, start, 4 pulls with gear changes, a view switch):

  | Engine | GT350 | 911 p1 | 911 p4 | 911 p6 | BRM |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | JSC | 61 % | 57 % | 81 % | 81 % | 3 % |
  | V8 / Chrome | 23 % | 16 % | 18 % | 18 % | 5 % |

  p4 / p6 have 42 waveguide pipes against 22 for p1 and 21 for the GT350, and the pipe loops ran about 10× slower in DFG. On a phone that meant no headroom.
* **Fix 1:** the kernel body is split into five helpers: arrivals, cylinders, exhaust nodes, intake and starter. The code is verbatim, in the same operation order. Every piece now reaches FTL. New mean / p99 per block:

  | Engine | GT350 | 911 p1 | 911 p4 | 911 p6 |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | JSC | 22 % / 48 % | 17 % / 38 % | 19 % / 42 % | 19 % / 42 % |
  | V8 | 22 % | 15 % | 18 % | 18 % |

  The 911 p4 is now no more expensive than the GT350.
* **Cause 2, 911 only (`int_res`):** the 0.25 s silent intake pre-roll (`int_settle`) ran in one go inside the first audible block, at key-on, at a car or sound switch while running, and at cold start. That is about 12,000 samples of kernel work in one quantum. It measured 64 quanta of work in JSC and 15–19 quanta in V8: a guaranteed dropout.
* **Fix 2:** the pre-roll now runs one 256-sample chunk per block. The chunks, controls and order are the same, and the controller is held. The block outputs silence until the pre-roll is done, then the held block plays. Key-on worst block in JSC is 64 quanta of work before and 2.5 after. A sound switch holds its crossfade until the new source has finished its pre-roll, so there is no level dip.
* **Sound:** bit-identical to v1.11.42 for the GT350, the Cadillac and 911 p1–p6, rear and cabin, idle / pull / game start (54 renders, `test` harness in the work tree). For the 911 the identical output starts 46 blocks later (about 0.12 s at 48 kHz) after the key or a switch. The BRM is untouched.
* Not changed: `latencyHint` stays 'interactive' because a larger output buffer would delay throttle response; the per-block cost is fixed at the source instead. The worklet still posts a small `meter` / `eng` object every 32 / 8 blocks, the same for every car.

## v1.11.42 (tag `v1.11.42`), 2026-11-15 (911 default is now p4; listening test v11 · 911 p6)

* **The 911 default is now v11 · 911 p4** (`?car=p997` opens p4), chosen by the owner by ear: he preferred p4 over p5 and hears its high-rpm note as a gradual shift in a real recording too. p1 stays selectable (`?p997=p1` and its old aliases v10.1, v10itq, variocam, …); unknown `?p997=` values now fall back to p4. The scorecard did not pick p4: its known failures (harsher outside than p3, outside order-3 peak at 5,500 rpm, idle about 10 dB quieter than p1) still stand.
* New, **non-default** option **Sound v11 · 911 p6** (`?p997=p6`): p4 with that note brought in earlier. Diagnosis (steady full-throttle renders): the note is order 4.5 at about 515 Hz, strongest outside, peaking about 6,800–7,000 rpm in steady renders (the owner hears it peak at 7,200 rpm in the game). Ablations show it comes from a **pipe resonance on the inlet side of the box** (the run from the cat pipe through the connecting pipe and inlet tube to the bellmouth): a longer inlet tube moved it down, while the outlet pipe length (which moves the ~410 Hz outlet-side resonance instead) and the middle-chamber length did not.
* p6 changes **one dimension**: the 50 mm connecting pipe in front of the box, 0.20 → 0.32 m (whole cat-to-box crossing 0.80 → 0.92 m, inside the 0.9–1.1 m estimate; no sourced figure). The order-4.5 hump outside moves from about 6,800–7,000 to about 5,800–6,200 rpm (about −13 %; the target was −17 %, from 7,200 to 6,000). Everything else is p4.
* p6 checks: the note stays gradual (a broad hump over about 1,000 rpm; outside order 4.5 minus order 3 at 6,000 rpm −4.8 dB, recordings about −5 dB); no new 2,000–3,000 rpm dip (cabin order 6 minus order 3 at 2,500 rpm −2.7 dB, p4 −4.7); idle unchanged vs p4 (within 0.5 dB). **Fails / regressions:** 2–8 kHz is about 2–3 dB harsher than p4; the cabin order 3 dips at 5,200–5,600 rpm (−16 dB at 5,500 rel. 3,500, p4 −10) and order 4.5 there stands about 23 dB above the recordings relative to order 3 (p4 about 9 dB). All dimensions remain estimates.
* The GT350, Cadillac, BRM and 911 p1 / p2 / p3 / p4 / p5 parameter sets and sounds are bit-identical to v1.11.41. JS matches Python on p6 (order deltas within about 0.5 dB). CPU about +14 % over p1 (same as p4).

## v1.11.41 (tag `v1.11.41`), 2026-11-15 (911 listening test: v11 · 911 p5 with series 3-chamber side boxes)

* New, **non-default** 911 option **Sound v11 · 911 p5** (`?p997=p5`, also `v11.p5`). p1 stays the default; p2, p3 and p4 are unchanged.
* p5 is p1 with a side box per bank laid out after the flow path described in the Fabspeed video of a cut-open 996 muffler ("Porsche 996/997 911 Carrera (1999-2008) | Factory Exhaust Explained", Fabspeed Motorsport, 2021; Fabspeed says the 2005–08 997 and 997S use the same box with a longer outlet; the forum claim that the S box differs is unconfirmed): a 3-chamber reverse-flow box. The inlet tube (57 mm) is crimped shut, so all gas leaves through its perforated section (0.20 m, 10 % open) into chamber 1 (0.13 m), goes through a 0.30 m transfer tube (57 mm) into chamber 2 (0.12 m), through a perforated wall (10 % open) into chamber 3 (0.06 m), and out through a 0.30 m velocity-stack outlet tube to a 0.25 m outlet pipe. Box 0.32 m long at 18 × the pipe area. **All dimensions are estimates** (the video gives none). Packing: the video mentions none and the opened box shows none; the OEM cut-away photo shows steel wool on the end cap of the inlet chamber, so only chamber 1 is damped (modelled as packing on the whole chamber-1 section). Built from existing generic blocks (`par`); no new code.
* **Listening test.** The 2,000–3,000 rpm range stays clean (cabin order 6 minus order 3 at 2,500 rpm: recordings about −12 dB, p1 −19, p3 +17, p4 −5, p5 −18). 2–8 kHz minus order 3 is back inside the range of the recordings (cabin about −63, outside about −45 dB; recordings −46…−59 / −36…−57; p4 −51 / −35; p3 −64 / −62). **Fails:** outside, the 3rd order rises with rpm (+7 / +14 / +15 dB at 4,500 / 5,500 / 6,500 rel. 3,500) where the recordings fall a few dB; the cabin 3rd order still falls above 5,000 rpm (−6 / −8 / −14 dB; recordings about −7 / −1 / +6). Idle is about 11 dB quieter than p1 (same as p4).
* Correction to v1.11.40: **p4 also idles about 10–11 dB quieter than p1**; this was missing from its notes.
* The GT350, Cadillac, BRM and 911 p1 / p2 / p3 / p4 sounds are bit-identical to v1.11.40. JS matches Python on p5 (order deltas within about 1 dB). CPU about +5 % over p1.

## v1.11.40 (tag `v1.11.40`), 2026-11-15 (911 listening test: v11 · 911 p4 with photo-measured side boxes)

* New, **non-default** 911 option **Sound v11 · 911 p4** (`?p997=p4`, also `v11.p4`). p3 is unchanged; p1 stays the default. Each iteration now gets its own p number.
* p4 is p1 with a side box per bank re-measured from the OEM cut-away photo (inlet pipe as scale), **all dimensions estimates from the photo**: box about 0.32 m long and 280 mm high, depth taken as 190 mm (least certain); gas in through a 62 mm tube to a bellmouth in the middle chamber (about 0.12 m) and out through a second 62 mm tube; the front (about 60 mm) and rear (about 130 mm) chambers are side volumes behind perforated baffles (10 % open area, estimate); 25 mm steel wool on the rear end cap only (the only packing the photo shows); 0.25 m outlet pipe (off-frame, estimate).
* Generic block change: the side-volume block (`cavity`) takes an optional absorbent layer on its closed end (thickness, packing), Python and JS. Unused by every other set.
* **Listening test, with known failures.** Fixes p3's 2,000–3,000 rpm dip (cabin order 6 minus order 3 at 2,500 rpm: recordings about −12 dB, p1 −19, p3 +17, p4 −5). **But it is clearly harsher outside than p3**: 2–8 kHz minus order 3 outside −28…−43 dB (p3 −58…−64, recordings −36…−57). It also has an outside order-3 peak at 5,500 rpm (+10 dB rel. 3,500, recordings fall), the cabin order 3 still falls above 5,000 rpm (−3 / −10 / −12 dB at 4,500 / 5,500 / 6,500, recordings about −7 / −1 / +6), and idle is about 10 dB quieter than p1.
* The GT350, Cadillac, BRM and 911 p1 / p2 / p3 sounds are bit-identical to v1.11.39. JS matches Python on p4 (order deltas within about 1 dB). CPU about +10 % over p1.

## v1.11.39 (tag `v1.11.39`), 2026-11-15 (911 listening test: v11 · 911 p3 with 3-chamber side boxes)

* New, **non-default** 911 option **Sound v11 · 911 p3** (`?p997=p3`, also `v11.p3`; the slot freed in v1.11.38, so old p3 links now open this): p1 with a different side box on each bank, laid out after a cut-away press photo of the OEM 997 muffler supplied by the owner: three chambers in a row (0.12 / 0.16 / 0.12 m, 11 × the 57 mm pipe area, about 11 L), two perforated baffle walls (3 % open area, 4 mm holes, 1.5 mm plate), a 52 mm tube through each baffle, a small 18 mm equalising tube through baffle 1, steel-wool packing in the rear end-cap chamber and a 58 mm outlet. **Every box dimension is an estimate**: no factory figures exist, and the layout comes from the photo and forum descriptions (3 chambers, perforated baffle, pipes through the baffles). Flared tube ends are not modelled.
* New generic block `par` (Python and JS): N parallel paths (tubes, a perforated baffle's holes lumped as one short neck, a small tube) from one volume into the next chamber. Parameters only; no per-car code.
* **Listening test, with one check failure.** Better than p1 from 3,500 to 7,000 rpm: the cabin 3rd order now roughly holds (rel. 3,500 rpm at 4,500 / 5,500 / 6,500: p1 −13.5 / −15.1 / −18.3 dB, p3 +2.0 / −6.8 / −4.5; recordings, per clip: about −7 / −1 / +6), and the full-throttle level outside rises with rpm instead of falling. 2–8 kHz stays below the recordings relative to the 3rd order (not harsh; far below p2). **Fails:** a deep dip in the 3rd order at 2,000–3,000 rpm (cabin about −17 dB vs p1 at 2,500) with the 6th order about 18 dB louder, so order 6 sits 4–19 dB above order 3 there, against about −12 dB in the recordings. Outside, the 3rd order rises a few dB at 4,500–6,000 rpm where the recordings fall a few dB.
* p1 stays the default; p2 is unchanged. The GT350, Cadillac, BRM and 911 p1 / p2 sounds are bit-identical to v1.11.38. JS matches Python on p3 (order deltas within about 1 dB). CPU about +15 % over p1 (still below p2).

## v1.11.38 (tag `v1.11.38`), 2026-11-15 (911 p3 removed after listening)

* **v11 · 911 p3 (intake runners) removed after the owner listened** ("for the bin"). It is gone from the 911 picker, the info page notes and the Specs rows. Old `?p997=p3` / `v11.p3` links fall back to the default **p1**. The generic intake-runner block (`int_run`, `int_run_l`, `int_run_d`, `int_run_nseg`, `int_run_attach`) stays in the kernel, off unless a parameter set enables it.
* A half-day test of a reverse-flow side muffler (perforated inlet tube, reversal chamber, perforated return tube, about 11 L, made only from existing generic blocks) on the 911 p1 base did not pass the checks (2–8 kHz outside 8–25 dB above the real recordings, order 6 weaker, cabin full-throttle level still falls with rpm), so it was not shipped.
* p1 stays the default; p2 is unchanged. GT350, Cadillac, BRM and 911 p1 / p2 sounds are bit-identical to v1.11.37.

## v1.11.37 (tag `v1.11.37`), 2026-11-15 (911 listening test: v11 · 911 p3 with intake runners)

* New, **non-default** 911 option **Sound v11 · 911 p3** (`?p997=p3`, also `v11.p3`): p1 plus a generic intake-runner block, one 1D travelling-wave pipe per cylinder from the intake valve to its bank plenum. Runner length 0.216 m (owner-supplied M97.01 figure; no public source found to confirm it). Runner diameter 45 mm is an **estimate** (two 40.2 mm intake valves; no factory runner ID found) and the 4.5 L plenum volume is a **guess** (unchanged from p1). The 76 mm throttle body is unchanged.
* **This is a listening test, and it fails the scorecard checks.** It improves the cabin 3rd-order shape (rel. 3,500 rpm at 4,500 / 5,500 / 6,500 rpm: real −3.4 / +4.2 / −5.7 dB; p1 −13.4 / −15.1 / −18.3; p3 −11.5 / −9.9 / −3.5; shape error 41.9 → 24.4 dB), but the 5–6k rise is still missing, and it **fails** two checks: a loud cabin **4.5-order tone near 4,500 rpm**, about 24 dB above the real recording (narrow-tone rule: ≤ 6 dB), and **10–16 dB more energy between 2 and 8 kHz** at 3,500–4,500 rpm. The 997 workshop manual also describes perforated resonance chambers in the distributors that damp intake resonance at 5,000–6,000 rpm, which the runner block does not model.
* **p1 stays the default; p2 is unchanged.** The runner block is generic (parameters `int_run`, `int_run_l`, `int_run_d`, `int_run_nseg`, `int_run_attach`; no per-car code), off unless a set enables it. The GT350, Cadillac, BRM and 911 p1 / p2 sounds are bit-identical to v1.11.36. JS matches the Python v8phys kernel on the p3 order curves (order 3 within about 1 dB). CPU: about +10 % on the 911 (22 → 28 waveguide pipes).
* Sound development and tuning guidelines are now in the repo: [SOUND_GUIDELINES.md](SOUND_GUIDELINES.md).

## v1.11.36 (tag `v1.11.36`), 2026-11-15 (sound model v11 across all cars; generic physics)

* One version nomenclature for the sound model on every car: **sound model v11** (the shared physics kernel) plus a per-car parameter-set iteration, shown as **v11 · 911 p1**, **v11 · BRM p11**, and so on. The BRM's v9.1–v9.11 history maps onto **v11 · BRM p1–p11**, with p11 the default. The 911's just-shipped v10.1 / v10.2 labels become **v11 · 911 p1** (default) and **v11 · 911 p2**. The GT350 and Cadillac v9p defaults become **v11 · GT350 p1** and **v11 · Cadillac p1**. Earlier v1–v4 kernels stay in the V8 Sound menu under their old names. Old labels and query values alias to the new names (see the mapping table in the release notes / info page).
* The physics model is now fully generic: a car is only a parameter set, with no `car=="p997"` branches. VarioCam Plus is a generic variable-cam block (a phase map vs rpm/load and a two-step lift switch with thresholds), enabled by parameters. The intake flap / plenum-shrink, `int_res` settle, muffler topology (`muf` list, `muf_as_cavity`, `cat_early`) and `tick_alpha` are all parameters. The JS `engineKey` maps and the `phys_v10*` overlays are ordinary per-car parameter sets (`p1` / `p2`). Output is bit-identical to v1.11.35 for every car and every picker option.

### Old → new label mapping (v1.11.36)

| Old label / query | New label | New query |
| --- | --- | --- |
| v9.1 … v9.11 (`?sound=v9.11`, `v9_11`, …) | v11 · BRM p1 … p11 | `?sound=v11.brm.p11` (and the old values) |
| `?sound=v9` (historic name for v9.6) | v11 · BRM p6 | `?sound=v11.brm.p6` or `?sound=v9` |
| v10.1, v10itq, `?p997=v10.1` (and older aliases) | v11 · 911 p1 (default) | `?p997=p1` or `?p997=v11.p1` |
| v10.2, `?p997=v10.2` | v11 · 911 p2 | `?p997=p2` or `?p997=v11.p2` |
| v9p, “v9.11 physics + starter” | v11 · GT350 p1 / v11 · Cadillac p1 (default) | `?sound=v9p` or `?sound=v11.p1` |

## v1.11.35 (tag `v1.11.35`), 2026-11-15 (911 picker returns with v10.1 and perforated-muffler v10.2)

* The 911 sound picker is back, numbered like the BRM's: **v10.1** (default) is the previous v10 + intake tube with a quieter valvetrain and the series-chamber muffler; **v10.2** is the same except the muffler is a packed perforated straight-through can (can 0.44 m, shell Ø180 mm, core Ø57 mm, porosity 0.12, packing 0.5; those can dimensions are estimates). Query values are `?p997=v10.1` and `?p997=v10.2` (or `v10_1` / `v10_2`). Older values (`variocam`, `v10`, `v10it`, `v10nf`, `v10itq`, `fixed`, `fixedcam`) alias to v10.1. The perforated can is a generic building block any car can select by parameters; there is no 911-only muffler code. The info page mentions both versions and describes the perf muffler. The GT350, Cadillac and BRM sounds stay bit-identical.

## v1.11.34 (tag `v1.11.34`), 2026-11-15 (911 has one sound; info page matches the model)

* The 911 sound picker is removed: **v10 + intake tube (quiet valvetrain)** is the only 911 sound. Older query values (`?p997=variocam`, `v10`, `v10it`, `v10nf`, `fixed` or `fixedcam`) alias to that model, and the picker is hidden because only one option remains. The in-game info page (About / How the sound model works, and the matching Specs notes) is rewritten from the live code so it describes every component that actually makes or shapes sound — combustion and exhaust pulses, valves and pulse shape, headers and collector, the 911 catalyst, mufflers, tailpipes and radiation, intake (including the two-bank resonance tube and flap), the valvetrain tick and broad cover filters at a realistic level, the BRM two-stage supercharger, the starter, cabin acoustics, listening positions, and the remaining filters — and drops outdated statements about flow noise, a VarioCam Plus default, and a multi-option 911 picker. The sound models themselves are unchanged. The GT350, Cadillac and BRM defaults and pickers are unchanged.

## v1.11.33 (tag `v1.11.33`), 2026-11-15 (911 default: v10 + intake tube, quieter valvetrain)

* A new 911 picker option, **Sound v10 + intake tube (quiet valvetrain)** (`?car=p997&p997=v10itq`), becomes the default. It is the v1.11.32 two-bank intake-tube variant with the valve-train tick turned down (`k_tick` × 0.03, about 295) and the 2.3 / 4.3 kHz valve-cover band-passes made broad (`cover_q1` = `cover_q2` = 2), so the tick adds texture rather than a fixed whistle that was not in the real recordings. **Sound VarioCam Plus**, **Sound v10 (damped exhaust)**, **Sound v10 + intake tube** and **Sound fixed cam** stay in the picker unchanged. Official stock-OK scorecard: 3.067 (cabin 2.796, outside 3.507, tailpipe 3.840; 339 windows; lower is better) vs 2.840 for v10 + intake tube and 2.797 for v10. The GT350, Cadillac and BRM defaults and pickers are unchanged.

## v1.11.32 (tag `v1.11.32`), 2026-11-15 (911 v10 + intake tube option)

* The 911 sound picker has a new option, **Sound v10 + intake tube** (`?car=p997&p997=v10it`): the current v10 (damped exhaust) plus a two-bank intake resonance tube with a flap, as on the 997.1 M97.01 3.8. The flap is closed at throttle ≥ 0.30 between 2,600 and 5,100 rpm (997.1 Technik figures) and open otherwise; the tube length 0.30 m and diameter 45 mm are estimates. That is to bring up the cabin boxer half-orders (1.5 and 4.5). A short silent settle on the first audible block removes a brief start-up burst from the tube. The existing v10 option is unchanged. VarioCam Plus stays the default. Official stock-OK scorecard for this variant: 2.840 (cabin 2.616, outside 3.206, tailpipe 3.415; 339 windows; lower is better) vs 2.797 for v10. The GT350, Cadillac and BRM defaults and pickers are unchanged.

Packing, cat position, the sharper blow-down pulse and the rest of the v10 phys are unchanged from v1.11.29. The picker label is **Sound v10 (damped exhaust)**; `?p997=v10nf` is kept as an alias of v10. VarioCam Plus stays the default. Official stock-OK scorecard for this v10: 2.797 (cabin 2.554, outside 3.195, tailpipe 3.417; 339 windows; lower is better), the same card as the A/B. Was 2.763 with the flow noise on in v1.11.29. The GT350, Cadillac and BRM defaults and pickers are unchanged.
