The GT350, Cadillac and BRM defaults and pickers are unchanged.

## v1.11.29 (tag `v1.11.29`), 2026-11-15 (911 v10 less resonant)

* The 911 **v10 valve-flow noise** option is updated in place to cut the remaining midrange ring and the 140 Hz boom: the cascade cat chamber is moved to just after the header collector (the crossing pipe now follows the cat; total path length is unchanged), and the cat and muffler have a small resistive packing loss (`pack_r=0.2`). The high-Q valve-cover band-passes at 2.3 and 4.3 kHz were tried at lower Q and with the filters removed; that made the 2–4 kHz band harsher, so they were left as they were. VarioCam Plus stays the default; v10 stays in the picker (`?car=p997&p997=v10`). Official stock-OK scorecard: 2.763 (was 2.548 on v1.11.28, 2.715 on v1.11.27, 2.370 on the original v10, vs 3.311 for VarioCam Plus; lower is better). Engine sizes, pipe lengths and the 76 mm throttle body are unchanged. The earlier fixed-cam joint fit stays selectable with `?p997=fixed`. The GT350, Cadillac and BRM defaults and pickers are unchanged; every earlier model stays selectable.

## v1.11.28 (tag `v1.11.28`), 2026-11-15 (911 v10 quieter at high rpm, sharper boxer pulse)

* The 911 **v10 valve-flow noise** option is updated in place: above about 4,500 rpm the flow-noise gain falls as `(4500/rpm)^2.5` (about 8 dB down at 6,500 rpm, 10 dB at redline), and the exhaust blow-down pulse injected into the pipes is shorter and sharper (early 40 crank-degree boost, later pumping eased; mass flow and torque are unchanged). That is to cut the remaining high-rpm turbulence and to raise the flat-six firing harmonics, especially order 4.5. VarioCam Plus stays the default; v10 stays in the picker (`?car=p997&p997=v10`). Official stock-OK scorecard: 2.548 (was 2.715 on v1.11.27, 2.370 on the original v10, vs 3.311 for VarioCam Plus; lower is better). Engine sizes, pipe lengths and the 76 mm throttle body are unchanged. The earlier fixed-cam joint fit stays selectable with `?p997=fixed`. The GT350, Cadillac and BRM defaults and pickers are unchanged; every earlier model stays selectable.

## v1.11.27 (tag `v1.11.27`), 2026-11-15 (911 v10 flow noise is quieter and pulsed)

* The 911 **v10 valve-flow noise** option is updated in place: the exhaust-valve flow noise is 10 dB quieter and gated to a short pulse at each exhaust opening (45 crank degrees after the valve opens, silent between pulses), so it no longer sits as a steady hiss over the engine. VarioCam Plus stays the default; v10 stays in the picker (`?car=p997&p997=v10`). Official stock-OK scorecard: overall 2.72 against 2.37 for the previous v10 and 3.31 for VarioCam Plus (cabin 2.57 against 2.32 / 2.95, outside rear 2.93 against 2.47 / 3.89, tailpipe 3.60 against 2.13 / 4.44; lower is closer). The score is a little behind the previous v10, as expected: that model matched the band-energy card but sounded like a steady hiss. Engine sizes and the 76 mm throttle body are unchanged. The earlier fixed-cam joint fit stays selectable with `?p997=fixed`. The GT350, Cadillac and BRM defaults and pickers are unchanged; every earlier model stays selectable.

## v1.11.26 (tag `v1.11.26`), 2026-11-15 (911 default is VarioCam Plus again)

* The 911 default sound is **VarioCam Plus** again (the v1.11.17 default), after v10 valve-flow noise did not sound right as the starting model. v10 stays in the 911 picker; `?car=p997&p997=v10` opens it. The earlier fixed-cam joint fit stays selectable with `?p997=fixed`. Engine sizes and the 76 mm throttle body are unchanged. The GT350, Cadillac and BRM defaults and pickers are unchanged; every earlier model stays selectable.

## v1.11.25 (tag `v1.11.25`), 2026-11-14 (911 default is v10 valve-flow noise)

* The 911 default sound is now **v10 valve-flow noise**: VarioCam Plus plus exhaust-valve flow noise at each exhaust pulse, and a flat hydraulic valve-train impact law. This was added to fill the high-frequency rasp the previous model was short of below 5,000 rpm. Official stock-OK scorecard (same corrected matrices as v1.11.17): overall 2.37 against 3.31 for VarioCam Plus (cabin 2.32 against 2.95, outside rear 2.47 against 3.89, tailpipe 2.13 against 4.44). The two-fold held-out check is 2.48 against 3.31. A few low-rpm cabin full-throttle cells were slightly worse on that held-out check; they are accepted (Wim). Parameters are the better-validated fold-B fit (`tick_alpha=0`, no jet-area scaling). Engine sizes and the 76 mm throttle body are unchanged. VarioCam Plus and the earlier fixed-cam joint fit stay in the 911 picker; `?car=p997&p997=variocam` and `?p997=fixed` still open them. The GT350, Cadillac and BRM defaults and pickers are unchanged; every earlier model stays selectable.

## v1.11.24 (tag `v1.11.24`), 2026-11-14 (911 bonnet restored; engine bay still when off)

* The 911 Carrera 4S bonnet in the Ink and Flat cockpit art is restored to the v1.11.22 drawing (the v1.11.5 shape). The longer lid from v1.11.23 is withdrawn. The official 288 km/h C4S manual top speed from v1.11.23 is unchanged.
* Engine bay: with the engine off, the crank, belts, fan, pulleys and other rpm-driven parts now sit still. They only move while cranking or running, at the actual rpm. The bay had been following the audio crank angle, and the audio thread is still fed a floor rpm even when the engine is off, so the picture kept turning. The game engine state is now the source of truth. All four cars. Scrub still lets you turn the crank by hand to inspect it.

## v1.11.23 (tag `v1.11.23`), 2026-11-14 (911 bonnet length; top speed confirmed 288 km/h)

* Redrew the Porsche 911 Carrera 4S (997.1) bonnet in the Ink and Flat cockpit art. v1.11.5 had already replaced the generated centre scoop with a shallow W, but the leading edge sat at y=249, only about 11 px above the cowl (y=260), so the lid read as a thin sliver and the oval lamps sat too close. The new lid is a long, almost-flat 997.1 panel, set against driver's-eye photographs of a 997 and the official MY07 C4S coupe dimensions (length 4,427 mm, width 1,852 mm, height 1,300 mm, wheelbase 2,350 mm): a shallow centre crease, fender shoulders a little above the lid, small foreshortened oval lamps at the far front corners, and a sharp leading-edge shut line closer to the horizon (the game camera is hz 110, f 1000, camH 1.05 m). The cowl, wipers, dash and road camera are unchanged. Rebuild: `tools/paint_p997_hood.py`.
* Checked the 911 top speed against Porsche's 29 August 2006 product description for the 911 Carrera 4 / Carrera 4S Coupé (press archive P07_0041, https://press.porsche.com/prod/presse_pag/PressResources.nsf/Content?ReadForm=&archive=10&languageversionid=65899): the official C4S manual figure is 288 km/h (179 mph), with 0–100 km/h in 4.8 s. 280 km/h is the Carrera 4 (3.6 L, 325 bhp) manual figure, and also the Tiptronic S 4S figure; 295 km/h is not a factory C4S number. Physics tests still give vmax 288.1 km/h in 6th and 0–100 4.85 s, so gearing and `crr2` are unchanged.

## v1.11.22 (tag `v1.11.22`), 2026-11-14 (electric starter has the rur-rur of a real crank)

* The electric starter’s cranking rhythm now follows a real starter: a short, quiet solenoid and pinion engagement, then loud bursts as the motor struggles on each compression stroke, a quick drop as the crank comes over top dead centre, and a whine-down when the engine catches and the starter drops out. The 911, Cadillac and GT350 each have their own pulse depth behind the car and in the cabin, so the rur-rur-rur is clear without a thin click train. The first-start wait from v1.11.20 is unchanged. The BRM still has no onboard electric starter. The 911 stays on VarioCam Plus; the BRM stays on v9.11 with v8 retired. Garage free-rev, with shifts locked, is unchanged.

## v1.11.21 (tag `v1.11.21`), 2026-11-14 (electric starter sounds like a starter motor)

* The electric starter on the GT350, Cadillac and 911 now sounds like a real starter, not a train of clicks. You hear the solenoid and the pinion engaging, then the motor whine and the pinion on the ring gear, with the rur-rur-rur as crank speed drops on each compression stroke, and a short whine-down when the engine catches and the starter drops out. Levels are still fitted per car and listening path (behind the car, far behind, and in the cabin) so the starter stays clearly audible at a realistic level. The first-start wait from v1.11.20 is unchanged. The BRM still has no onboard electric starter. The 911 stays on VarioCam Plus; the BRM stays on v9.11 with v8 retired. Garage free-rev, with shifts locked, is unchanged.

## v1.11.20 (tag `v1.11.20`), 2026-11-14 (electric starter audible; garage free-rev)

* The electric starter on the GT350, Cadillac and 911 is clearly audible at a realistic level in every position (behind the car, in the cabin, and both garage views). It is synthesised on the physics sound model (the worklet path): a real starter motor, pinion on the ring gear and solenoid, with a short mechanical pulse and a body/grind band so it is heard as engine-mechanical sound, not a thin tick. From behind it sits around the same level as the idle; in the cabin it is a few dB above. The first start after the page loads waits for the sound engine to be running, so the starter is not skipped if the worklet is still opening (including in the Tesla in-car browser). The mixer’s generic starter stays silent on these cars; wind remains off (NON_ENGINE_NOISE / MIXER_WIND = 0). The BRM still has no onboard electric starter. The 911 stays on VarioCam Plus; the BRM stays on v9.11 with v8 retired.
* In both garage positions the car stays parked in neutral (N on the Cadillac). Throttle (pedal, up arrow, on-screen GAS) free-revs the engine with the no-load physics (inertia and the rev limiter). The car does not move. Shift up/down (keys, on-screen buttons, selector) is disabled and greyed out in the garage.

## v1.11.19 (tag `v1.11.19`), 2026-11-14 (garage start-up view; starter and cold idle fitted)

* A garage start-up view, from the Position menu (behind the car or in the cabin; V switches between those two). The car sits in a one-car garage. The GT350, Cadillac and 911 show their brake lights while parked; the BRM starts already running, with no brake lights and no electric starter. The garage is an acoustic chamber: image-source early reflections and a short reverb (RT60 about 0.6 s), using a native convolver so it stays light in the Tesla in-car browser. Low rpm is the priority. `?view=garage` opens it. The desert road views are unchanged.
* The physical starter (real starter specs already in the model, solenoid and pinion, cranking speed from the compression pulses, first-fire catch) and the decaying cold fast idle were fitted to the approved cold-start recordings, with the comb rpm and the jump gate (OK at weight 1, unsure at 0.5). The 911 and Cadillac had a full pass; the GT350 a lighter one on its one approved clip. Weighted overall score 0.42 → 0.50. The 911 improved 0.52 → 0.66 (catch about 1.18 s at around 144 rpm, close to the 1.20 s garage and showroom recordings; cold-idle decay about 6 s). The GT350 improved 0.71 → 0.91 (about 0.80 s and 103 rpm vs 0.68 s and 96 rpm on the approved clip; idle mae 21 rpm). The Cadillac stayed at 0.25: crank speed and time were already close on the clean cabin start (about 80 rpm and 0.8 s vs 76 rpm and 0.95 s); a two-second cabin start and a noisy post-start reading were not forced. The 1960 Cadillac clip stays blocked.

## v1.11.18 (tag `v1.11.18`), 2026-11-14 (BRM v8 sound retired; default is v9.11)

* The BRM's v8 order-table sound model is retired and is no longer in the picker, the worklet, or the page scripts. The default is now v9.11, the closest remaining physics model on the official Mason scorecard (onboard covered-cell mean 0.88, against 1.00 for v9.10 and 0.71 for the retired v8). v9.1–v9.10 stay selectable. An old `?sound=v8` or `?car=brm&sound=v8` link falls back to v9.11 and does not switch the GT350 or Cadillac onto the wrong model. Key B cycles the remaining physics models. The 911 stays on VarioCam Plus (the v1.11.17 default). The GT350 and Cadillac defaults are unchanged.
* The info button's "How the sound model works" text, the BRM Specs page, the About blurb and History now describe the current defaults: the 911 starts on VarioCam Plus (from v1.11.17), and the BRM starts on v9.11. Specs lists the v9.11 physics parameters instead of the old order-table calibration, and marks v8 as retired in History.

## v1.11.17 (tag `v1.11.17`), 2026-11-14 (911 VarioCam Plus becomes the default)

* The 911 default sound is now VarioCam Plus (official 997.1 3.6 mm / 11.0 mm intake lift, 40° vane range, and the two-stage intake flap), after a fair win on the same official stock-OK matrices as v1.11.11: overall 3.31 against 3.41 for the fixed-cam default (cabin 2.95 against 3.00, outside 3.89 against 4.09, tailpipe 4.44 against 4.56). The fixed-cam sound stays in the picker; `?car=p997&p997=fixed` opens it. Engine sizes and the 76 mm throttle body are unchanged. The BRM still started on v8 in this release.

## v1.11.16 (tag `v1.11.16`), 2026-11-14 (911 VarioCam Plus + intake flap, non-default)

* Non-default 911 sound option, next to the car menu (same idea as the BRM sound picker). The default remains the v1.11.11 fixed-cam joint fit. The option models VarioCam Plus with the official 997.1 figures: continuously variable intake phasing over a 40° crank vane range, and a 3.6 mm / 11.0 mm intake-lift switch (the 3 mm / 10 mm figures are the 996 Turbo, and are not used). At idle the small cam and low overlap are used; the exhaust cam stays fixed. The two-stage intake resonance flap closes under load between about 3,000 and 5,500 rpm — confirmed on the 997.2 3.8 DFI, treated as probable for the 997.1. The 76 mm throttle body, bay dimensions and the default 911 sound are unchanged. `?car=p997&p997=variocam` opens the option. v1.11.15 already showed the picker in the page HTML; this release also ships the worklet so the option actually changes the sound.

## v1.11.15 (tag `v1.11.15`), 2026-11-14 (911 power curve capped at the official 355 PS peak)

* Checked each car’s modelled torque and power against the manufacturer figures, with the rating standard noted: SAE gross for the 1959 Cadillac 390 (325 hp @ 4,800, 430 lb-ft @ 3,100) and the 1967 GT350 289 Hi-Po (306 hp @ 6,000, 329 lb-ft @ 4,200); DIN/ECE for the Porsche 997.1 C4S (355 PS @ 6,600, 400 N·m @ 4,600); BRM P30 as sourced (~460 hp @ 11,000, 1951). The GT350 and Cadillac curves already pass through the official points (the 0.2–0.4 hp difference is rounding from lb-ft × rpm / 5,252). The BRM is 458.7 hp at 11,000, within 1.3 hp of the sourced “about 460”. The 911 already met 355.2 PS at 6,600 and 400 N·m at 4,600, but 360 N·m at 7,000 and 345 N·m at 7,300 made a second peak of 358.8 PS at 7,000, above the official 355 PS. Those points are now 356 N·m and 338 N·m, so power does not rise after 6,600 rpm. `crr2` is 3.00e-6 (was 3.52e-6) so sixth still balances at 288 km/h (~7,150 rpm). Physics tests: 0–60 4.64 s, 0–100 4.85 s (press 4.8 s), 1/4 mile 13.13 s @ 179 km/h, vmax 288.1 km/h in 6th. The GT350 (6.51 / 6.77 / 202.8), Cadillac (9.93 / 10.50 / 198.2) and BRM (6.30 / 6.51 / 307.8) are unchanged.

## v1.11.14 (tag `v1.11.14`), 2026-11-14 (how the sound model works)

* The info button (About / History, key H) now includes a plain-language explanation of how the sound model works: combustion pulses, gas exchange through the valves, the exhaust and muffler, intake and valve-train sound, and the path into the cabin. A further section explains how fitting works: engine sizes and gearing stay fixed, the model parameters are tuned to recordings of a standard car, and the result is scored on a grid of engine speed against throttle opening. The same explanation is linked from the Specs page. No sound or physics changes.

## v1.11.13 (tag `v1.11.13`), 2026-11-14 (engine specifications page)

* Each car now has a full engine specifications page, opened from the Specs button in the top bar (in the ☰ menu on a small screen) or from the Engine specifications button under Car. It lists bore, stroke, displacement, compression, firing order, valves and cam timing, intake, exhaust, gearbox ratios, final drive, mass, Cd and frontal area, and the power and torque curve, plus the parameters of that car’s default sound model with a short plain explanation of each one. Figures come from the car and sound-model data already in the page. A short note explains that fitting the sound keeps engine sizes fixed and tunes only the model parameters. The panel follows the selected car. No sound or physics changes.

## v1.11.12 (tag `v1.11.12`), 2026-11-14 (car and position drop-down lists)

* The car picker and the listening-position picker (inside the car, outside from behind, or the engine bay) are now native drop-down lists, in the same style as the Sound and Visuals menus. They use the browser's own `<select>` menus, so they work in the Tesla in-car browser (and on phones and older iPads). The 1–4 keys still pick a car; V still switches inside/outside and E still opens the engine bay.

## v1.11.11 (tag `v1.11.11`), 2026-11-14 (911 joint cabin + outside fit is now the default)

* **911 Carrera 4S default sound is now a joint cabin-and-outside v9p fit** on the corrected stock-OK matrices (audio-primary comb rpm, jump/confidence gate, engine-only). Windows are weighted by duration × source weight; thin outside cells are down-weighted by their coverage so they cannot dominate. Engine specs stay fixed (including the 76 mm throttle body). On the same fair scorecard the new fit beats the previous default: overall 3.415 against 3.667; cabin 2.996 against 3.203; outside rear 4.094 against 4.397; tailpipe 4.564 against 5.395 (lower is closer). Most cells improve; cabin 5–7.3k full is slightly behind (2.75 against 2.55). Under the standing rule the best fair fit becomes the default. The GT350 and Cadillac defaults are unchanged; the BRM stays on v8. No reference audio is published.

## v1.11.10 (tag `v1.11.10`), 2026-11-13 (911 steering wheel on the column)

* The 911 Carrera 4S steering wheel now sits on the end of the painted steering column, as on a real 997.1 cockpit. In v1.11.7–v1.11.9 the crest was lined up with the column in X but sat much lower, down by the pedals, so you could see the empty column opening in the dash through the wheel; the wheel was not on the column. The live hub (the crest, which is the rotation axis in the perspective sprite) is now at the centre of the column housing under the gauges (hub [450, 650] → [452, 485]), matching driver’s-eye photographs of a 997. Wheel size is unchanged (520 logical px, from the existing camera). Ink and Flat, straight and turned. Outside / engine-bay views do not show the wheel. No sound or physics changes.

## v1.11.9 (tag `v1.11.9`), 2026-11-13 (911 official specs and 288 km/h top speed)

* **911 Carrera 4S (p997) vehicle data now follows the official MY07 press kit and workshop figures.** Cd 0.29 and frontal area 2.04 m² (CdA 0.59; was 0.30 / 2.0). Gearbox label Aisin G97/31 (C4/C4S); G97/01 is the RWD box. Rear final drive stays 3.44; AWD front final drive 3.33 is now used for the front shaft (`awd.finalDriveFront`, `frontShaftRpm()`). Engine-to-road speed stays on the rear 3.44. Intake valves in the bay are 40.2 mm (LN Engineering / Hamheads 3.8 heads); the cam note cites the 11.0 mm high-lift workshop figure. The throttle body stays 76 mm per Porsche TI 7/05 ENU 1001. Default 911 sound is unchanged (still fixed-cam v9p).
* **Official manual top speed is 288 km/h in 6th**, not 280 (that is Tiptronic S / C4 manual). The v1.11.2 tune (`crr2` 4.56e-6 and `spinDrag` 1.1) targeted 280 and could not reach 288 even with official CdA. `drivelineEff` 0.85 already includes transaxle + AWD average loss, so `spinDrag` is 0 to avoid double-counting. `crr` 0.012 is kept; `crr2` is 3.52e-6 so sixth balances at 288 km/h (~7116 rpm), below the limiter.
* Tests (`test/physics_test.js`): 0–60 4.63 s, 0–100 4.85 s (press 4.8), 1/4 mile 13.12 s @ 179 km/h (no official quarter figure), standing-start vmax 287.8 km/h in 6th. The p997 accel loop was extended to 180 s so vmax is the sixth-gear balance, not a 90 s cut-off. GT350 (6.51 / 6.77 / 202.8), Cadillac (9.93 / 10.50 / 198.2) and BRM (6.30 / 6.51 / 307.8) are unchanged.

## v1.11.8 (tag `v1.11.8`), 2026-11-13 (steering columns redrawn)

* The painted steering column of each car is now drawn in the true direction, tilt and length of the real cockpit, and the live wheel sits at the end of that column, with its plane square to the column. On the Cadillac, the painted shaft had pointed at the floor while the wheel faced you; the long downward tube is gone (Ink and Flat). The short column now comes out of the dash toward the driver, as on a real 1959 Series 62 / De Ville / Eldorado, and the wheel sits on that end with a slight look-down ellipse. On the BRM P30, the painting had no column; a short metal tube now runs from the (already slightly right-of-centre) wheel into the scuttle under the instruments, matching Type 15 / P30 photographs from Beaulieu. The GT350 and 911 columns were already short units aimed at the driver (the chrome dash boss, and the 997 column housing); they are unchanged, and the GT350 wheel gets the same slight look-down so its plane stays square to the column (the 911 wheel sprite is already drawn in perspective). Wheel sizes are unchanged. The Cadillac column shifter and the BRM's front-wheel steering still work. No sound changes.

## v1.11.7 (tag `v1.11.7`), 2026-11-13 (steering wheel alignment)

* In the interior view, each car's live steering wheel is now centred on the steering column in the cockpit picture, and turns about that point. The GT350 and Cadillac wheels sat a little to the left of the painted boss (Cadillac by about 35 px); they now sit on the chrome cup / horn in the artwork. The 911 lines up with the column opening in the dashboard (the crest, which sits below the sprite centre in the perspective drawing, is the rotation axis). On the BRM P30, reference photographs (a Type 15 cockpit at the National Motor Museum, Beaulieu, and the car's layout) confirm the wheel sits slightly to the right of the dashboard centre, as Wim thought; the live wheel is now there (hub x 640 → 696). The BRM's front wheels still turn with the steering wheel (same 11:1 ratio). Ink and Flat. No sound changes.

## v1.11.6 (tag `v1.11.6`), 2026-11-13 (History panel)
* The on-page About / History list is grouped by area: sound models, cars, driving and controls, visuals and engine bay, and site and fixes. Each release has a one-line headline; the full note is in a native `<details>` disclosure (works on the Tesla browser and older iPads, no hover, large tap target). Reverse chronological order inside each area. Every previous version, date and full text is kept.

## v1.11.5 (tag `v1.11.5`), 2026-11-13 (911 997.1 bonnet shape)
* Redrew the Porsche 911 Carrera 4S (997.1) bonnet in the Ink and Flat cockpit art against real 997.1 reference photos (profile, front edge, headlight cut-outs, how the lid meets the fenders and the bumper, and the slope). The previous generated lid was a high centre bulge / scoop, with the centre higher than the fenders and no lamp cut-outs. The new shape is a shallow W (fender shoulders a little above the lid), foreshortened oval lamps in the front-corner cut-outs, hood-to-fender shut lines, a sharp leading-edge shut line, a shallow centre crease and the crest. Dash, wipers, gauges and the road camera are unchanged. Rebuild: `tools/paint_p997_hood.py`.

## v1.11.4 (tag `v1.11.4`), 2026-11-13 (Cadillac muffler fix; Cadillac default is now v9p)
* **Cadillac default sound is now v9p** (v9.11 physics), the best fit on the scorecard after a muffler fix (pooled all paths, OK+UNSURE: 3.60 vs v2 4.07, was 4.76 vs 4.07; OK only 3.37 vs 3.71). The reverse-flow muffler had been ported as nearly lossless expansion chambers (a midrange hole and a strong firing-order boom). It now has packing, a resistive packing loss (`pack_r`, GT350 and 911 stay at 0), two m=6 chambers, and a ~55 Hz Helmholtz resonator for the order-4 drone at ~825 rpm. Cabin transfer was refit (J 3.69 → 2.83). Tailpipe 2.36 vs v2 2.22 (idle slightly ahead, part throttle still behind); outside far 4.01 vs 4.46 (was 6.81); engine bay got worse (4.20 vs 2.59) and was not refit. A `?sound=` link or a saved `streetrod.sound` still overrides the default. GT350 stays on v9p, BRM on v8.
* Porsche 911 outside refit was started (prep + 5 Nelder-Mead evals on the round-2 outside windows): 4.412 → 4.401, not a meaningful move, so the v1.11.2 outside settings stay. Cabin already shipped in v1.11.2.
* `js/engine-v8p.js` DATA re-exported from engine-sound-v8src (`fitted_params_v8phys.json`). Kernel already had typ 3 / Helmholtz / `pack_r` from the prior local commit.

## v1.11.3 (tag `v1.11.3`), 2026-11-13 (GT350 default sound)
* **GT350 default sound is now v9p** (v9.11 physics with `cab_model 1`), the best fit on the scorecard (pooled all paths, OK+UNSURE: 1.93 vs v2 2.81; better in all 3 covered cells). Cadillac stays on v2; BRM stays on v8. Other defaults unchanged (lift-shift, ink, real bay speed).
* A saved `streetrod.sound` choice or a `?sound=` URL still applies to both V8 cars, as before. With no saved choice, switching from the GT350 to the Cadillac still starts the Cadillac on v2.
* Tests: `test/v8p_game_test.js`; Chromium `test/v1113_default_sound_test.mjs`.

## v1.11.2 (tag `v1.11.2`), 2026-11-13 (cabin sound + 911 top speed)
* **v9p in-cabin path refitted** (GT350 / Cadillac `v9p` option and the 911). The v1.11.1 cabin ran each route through two 2nd-order low-passes (24 dB/oct above 180 / 320 Hz) and was far darker than every real cabin recording (cabin distance 12-15). New engine-sound `v8phys` `cab_model 1`, ported to `js/engine-v8p.js` (parity >= 215 dB SNR on all 18 clips): per route a mass-law panel path (transmitted pressure ~ 1/f above `f_floor` / `f_fw`, i.e. TL rising 6 dB/oct) in parallel with a flat seal / grommet / boot leak (`leak_floor`, `leak_fw`), floor level `k_floor`, the intake through the firewall plus the cowl / fresh-air vent aperture (`k_ind`), trim absorption one pole `f_abs`, the cabin's longitudinal / vertical modes (`cab_q`); an open-window listener (`in_cabin_open`, `k_win`) exists but is not used (no recording shows the window state reliably). No wind, tyre or vibration noise. `cab_model 0` reproduces v1.11.1 to 1e-13.
* Fitted per car (physically bounded Nelder-Mead) on the round-2 engine-only cabin windows (OK 1, UNSURE 0.5): GT350 f_floor 15 Hz, leak_floor 0.3, k_floor 1.0, f_fw 72 Hz, leak_fw 0.003, k_ind 0.030, f_abs 8 kHz, Q 4.3; Cadillac 15 / 0.3 / 1.0 / 400 / 0.005 / 0.033 / 8 kHz / 1.5; 911 15 / 0.3 / 1.0 / 400 / 0.003 / 0.50 / 8 kHz / 2.7. Several parameters sit on their physical bounds (floor leak, absorption corner): the recordings want a leakier, brighter cabin than a sealed body.
* Round-2 scorecard (engine-only filter on both sides), cabin v9p before -> after (v2 beside): GT350 12.96 -> 2.25 (v2 6.27), Cadillac 12.12 -> 3.69 (v2 5.58), 911 13.63 -> 4.65. Pooled all paths, OK+UNSURE: GT350 v9p 1.93 vs v2 2.81 (v9p better in all 3 covered cells); Cadillac 4.76 vs 4.07 (OK only 3.41 vs 3.71). **Defaults unchanged** (GT350 / Cadillac v2, BRM v8): Wim decides.
* 911: the round-2 source set (989 videos screened; 24 OK, 33 UNSURE, 8 PSE scored) and refit: outside parameters kept from v1.11.1 (no outside refit shipped), cabin fit shipped because it is better on the same filtered metric (paths mean OK+UNSURE 7.58 -> 4.59; outside rear 4.05 and tailpipe 5.06 unchanged).
* v9p level match redone (`test/level_match_v8p.js`, `test/level_match_p997.js`): in_cabin gainDb GT350 -2.9, Cadillac -0.6, 911 -7.7; rear unchanged.
* **911 top speed**: 295 km/h on the limiter -> 280 km/h in 6th (factory 280). `js/physics.js`: optional `crr2` (tyre rolling resistance growing with v^2, SAE J2452 / Ehlers form at 2.7 bar = 4.56e-6 per (m/s)^2) and `spinDrag` (AWD driveline spin losses, 1.1 N per m/s, ~9 kW at 280 km/h); Cd 0.30, A 2.0 m^2, crr 0.012, G97/01 ratios and 3.44 final drive unchanged. 0-100 km/h 4.86 s (factory 4.8), 1/4 mile 13.15 s. Other cars bit-identical (they have no crr2 / spinDrag).
* Tests: `node_compare_v8p.js` PASS, `old_models_identity.js` (24 outputs bit-identical), `gearbox_test.js`, `physics_test.js`, `v8p_game_test.js`, `wheel_angle_test.js` pass.

## v1.11.1 (tag `v1.11.1`), 2026-11-13 (queue job 9)
* **BRM onboard view: the front wheels steer with the steering wheel** (Wim). Same input as the steering wheel (`rot = steer x 2.1 rad`); road-wheel angle = wheel angle / `STEER_RATIO` 11 (overall ratio 10-12:1 for a 1950s F1 car), so full input = 10.9 deg. Each tyre yaws about its kingpin through the hub: the tread top above the axle stays put, a point `a` round the tread behind the axle moves sideways by `R sin(a) sin(delta)` (turning right swings the rear of the tyres left), drawn as a 16-band piecewise shear of the painted tyre + rolling tread (`tyreSteer` in `js/visuals-painted.js`). The painted tyre and its outline are cut from the cockpit art while steering (the road shows where it was, the inner side shows the tyre's smooth inner sidewall); clipped above the cowl line so it never covers the body.
* Tread spin unchanged (still `angF` / `wWF`); straight ahead (|steer| < 0.002) the old drawing path runs unchanged. Ink and Flat. Chase view unchanged.
* Tests (`test/brm_onboard_test.mjs` 2c): ratio within 10-12:1 and delta = steer x 2.1 / 11; both tyres' lower outer edges move about 35 px the right way at full lock; tread still rolls while steered; repeat renders identical; cowl untouched; tyre regions at steer 0 bit-identical with steering off; in the game, steering right turns the tyres and they come back to straight when let go.

## v1.10.10 (tag `v1.10.10`), 2026-11-13 (queue job 7)
* **Engine bay laid out like each real car** (Wim: look up each car's engine bay and make the layout more similar; "Dont change the dimensions, they need to stick to the model"). New `layout()` in `js/engine-bay.js`, two new Parts toggles (`bay`: body, radiator, battery, bracing; `ancillaries`: distributor / magnetos, pumps, valve covers, intake), sources and assumptions as notes in `js/engine-params.js` (shown in the Parts panel).
  * 1967 Shelby GT350: Cobra finned valve covers (black crinkle), the round chromed 289 Hi-Po open-element air cleaner lid on the Holley 715 with its front / rear float bowls (the oval finned Cobra cleaner was the 1967 GT500 / 1968 part, so it is not used), distributor at the front of the intake, alternator moved to the right-hand head (was on the left), power-steering pump low on the left, shock towers with the Monte Carlo bar across just ahead of the air cleaner and the export brace back to the cowl, battery right front beside the radiator, power-brake booster and dual master cylinder on the left of the firewall, radiator with upper / lower hoses, inner fenders and firewall.
  * 1959 Cadillac 390: stamped valve covers in Cadillac blue, distributor at the rear, the 1959-only canister oil filter top left at the front, power-steering pump with its reservoir at the front right above the water pump, generator high on the right (unchanged), Carter AFB choke housing, air cleaner reshaped (oil-bath can with a wide domed lid when the sound model says oil bath, as on v2 / v4; otherwise the standard 1959 dry-pack cleaner with intake silencer), battery left front, large power-brake booster on the left of the firewall, wide radiator, inner fenders and firewall, no shock towers.
  * BRM P30 Mk II V16: twin-choke SU carburettor (one body, two opposed pistons, two dashpots) feeding the first blower stage with a forward air intake, sixteen short inlet runners from the manifold in the vee to the inlet ports, four Lucas magnetos (each firing four cylinders, driven from the camshafts; drawn at the outer ends of the inlet camshafts), radiator behind the nose grille, scuttle bulkhead.

* **Dimensions unchanged.** Every EngineParams field (bore, stroke, rods, CR, cylinder count, bank angle, firing order, valve / cam / stub / header / blower data, blower ratio 4.15, crank height) and every mesh of the v1.10.9 bay (block, heads, liners, crank, pistons, rods, valves, cams, stubs, headers, collectors, blower, gears, fan, starter: vertex counts, bounding boxes, world and instance matrices at the same crank angle) are identical to v1.10.9. The only intended differences: the GT350 alternator and its fan (same geometry, moved from the left to the right-hand side) and the Cadillac air-cleaner shape.
* Clashes with the real cars, left as the model has them: GT350 tubular (Tri-Y) headers are a sound-model setting, a stock 1967 GT350 had cast-iron manifolds with headers optional; Cadillac oil-bath cleaner (sound model v2 / v4) was a heavy-duty option in 1959, the standard cleaner was dry-pack; BRM impellers are drawn 200 / 170 mm (model assumption) where Rolls-Royce quotes 6 in. / 5.5 in. (152 / 140 mm); the BRM engine is drawn level and centred, the real one is angled so the propshaft passes left of the driver; the parent brief's "oval air cleaner" for the GT350 / Cadillac does not match the 1967 GT350 (round Hi-Po) or 1959 Cadillac (round) records.

## v1.10.6 (released together as `v1.10.9`), 2026-11-13
* **BRM onboard view: the road ahead shows over the nose** (Wim: "the hood is blocking the road"). Cause: not the camera height or a mesh but a 2D misregistration - the BRM cockpit painting sat 130 painting px too high against its own road camera (eye 0.9 m, horizon row 75, f 1100 px): the nose tip was drawn at row 62, above the horizon, which no nose lower than the eye can be, so nose and scuttle covered every road row ahead (< 5 % of the window over the nose was road). Fix (`js/visuals-painted.js`, `BRM_DY = 130`): the whole cockpit layer (painting, gauges, stalk mirrors, lever, steering wheel, tyres) moves down 130 px; a 0.65 m nose tip 2.3 m ahead of a 0.9 m eye belongs at row ~195, it is now at 192. Same camera, same scale, nothing shrunk; road visible from ~8.5 m ahead. Trade-off: the seat back the painting showed (a camera behind the seat) and most of the gear lever drop out of the bottom of the frame; the steering wheel still covers the lower dash as before. Applies to every style the BRM uses (v1, v2, v3 and v4 draw the flat painting; v5 the ink one).
* **BRM tyres turn** (Wim: "the wheels aren't turning"). The tyres were part of the paintings. Each visible tread face (inside: both fronts; chase view: both rears) is re-drawn per frame from the painting's own blurred tyre with tread grooves at the wheel's angle. `js/physics.js`: new output-only `angF` (integral of v / r_front, 0 while the fronts are locked) and `angR` (integral of the rear wheel speed `ww`, so wheelspin and locked rears show), wrapped to 2 pi; `js/vehicles.js` BRM `tyreRadiusFront: 0.335` (5.25-18, visual only); `js/main.js` passes the angles / speeds to the renderer. Motion blur over one frame (360-degree shutter), so the tread crawls at walking pace and smears at speed instead of strobing. Standing still = not turning; the physics has no reverse. GT350 / Cadillac chase tyres not animated (not in this job).
* Physics bit-identical (angles are never read back; lift-shift, rev-match, Hydra-Matic unchanged); gearbox code / UI untouched; sound untouched.
* Tests: new `test/wheel_angle_test.js` (node: angles = integrals of the wheel speeds, standstill, wheelspin, locked fronts, physics bit-identical with the angles frozen) and `test/brm_onboard_test.mjs` (Chromium: road window over the nose >= 85 % bare road in every style, the old placement flagged; drawn tyre angle = car angF / angR, standstill frames identical, angle changes the tread pixels).

## v1.10.5 (released together as `v1.10.9`), 2026-11-13
* **Manual gearbox option: rev-match** (Wim t127u-t129u: blip on downshifts, rev-match on upshifts; the engine's inertia against the car's mass should be heard; "modelling the drive train. Transmission, clutch and perhaps even the differential"). GT350 and BRM; the Cadillac's Hydra-Matic ignores it. Default stays the v1.10.4 lift-shift (bit-identical physics); "Rev-match" button in the top bar (in the menu on narrow / touch layouts), key G, `?gearbox=revmatch|lift`, stored in localStorage. The shift buttons read "down - blip" / "up - match" when it is on. No sound kernel, fitted parameter, NON_ENGINE_NOISE or MIXER_WIND change; v8 stays the BRM default.
* Drivetrain (js/physics.js, unchanged model): engine inertia Je (BRM 0.12, GT350 0.20 kg m^2), dry clutch with capacity (BRM 400, GT350 560 Nm) that locks / slips, gearbox ratios and final drive per car (BRM 2.85/1.95/1.45/1.15/1.00 x 4.10, GT350 Toploader 2.32/1.69/1.29/1.00 x 3.89), rear axle as one rigid final drive (straight line: an open diff splits torque equally, so this is the same as a spool here), tyre slip, 1 kHz fixed step. Locked: (m + Jw/r^2 + Je G^2/r^2) a = Te G eta / r - resistances, w_e = w_wheel G. Slipping: Je dw_e/dt = Te - Tc, wheel side gets Tc G eta.
* Rev-match shift sequence: clutch out (~70 ms, the plates stick until the clamp capacity falls below the torque they carry), gear out -> in (selector time 0.10 s BRM / 0.12 s GT350), throttle drives the free engine to the new gear's speed w* = w_wheel G_new (Te(rpm, thr) = Je (w* - w)/0.09 s: a blip on a downshift, closed throttle and wait on an upshift), clutch back in (~125 ms) when within max(120 rpm, 1.5%) or after 0.6 s; any residual difference is taken up by the slipping clutch (engine inertia against the car). The driver's own blip (fresh gas press during the shift) overrides the automatic one. Pulling away from N / shifting into N keeps the plain shift. The clutch torque during a rev-match shift is solved implicitly (stiff stick-slip, stable at 1 kHz).
* Tests: `test/gearbox_test.js` (blip, upshift match, continuity, locked coupling w_e = w_wheel G, lift-shift + Hydra-Matic bit-identical to b5b2ae9), `test/gearbox_browser_test.mjs` (Chromium, Tesla-size desktop + iPad touch).

* Released 2026-11-12 (Europe/Brussels) on https://wimbosmans1.github.io/streetrod/ after Wim heard hiss on v9.11 (Wim t132u: "If it doesnt come from the engine, can we remove it? Also from the other engine models?", t133u: "Then upload to website"). A subtraction only: no refit, no new picker entry (ids v9_1 ... v9_11 unchanged), gainDb not raised to compensate, v8 stays the default. J is not re-claimed and nothing here is scored as beating v8.

Valve-train (mech), blower whine / rush, exhaust / reversion, afterburn / mouth flame, tyre / road noise, starter and the cabin body filter are untouched.

PH.wind * 300 : 0` (Wind9; on only where `persp.onboard.wind > 0`, i.e. inside), `KVIB = NON_ENGINE_NOISE && PH.k_vib > 0 ? PH.k_vib : 0` (v9.11 only had k_vib = 0.3; k_comb stays 0). Done in the kernel, not in SETS, so a re-export of the fitted sets cannot bring them back; the fitted values (wind 0.10-0.26 in v9.1-v9.11, k_vib 0.3 in v9.11) stay in SETS as the fit record, `NON_ENGINE_NOISE = 1` restores v1.10.3 exactly. The wind noise draws still run (rng2 is shared with the valve-train noise), so the mech noise realisation is unchanged.
* `js/engine-dsp.js` (EngineVoice, every car: v1-v4 Cadillac / GT350, v8 and v9.x BRM): the game speed wind (band-pass 700 Hz + low-pass 3 kHz noise, `0.02 x (v/40)^1.5`, x1.5 inside) is off: `MIXER_WIND = 0` (was the literal 0.02); code kept. Its own LCG is used by nothing else. Tyre / road / skid noise unchanged.

In the full game chain v8 only loses the game speed wind: within 0.1 dB in 0.7-3 / 3-6 / 6-10 kHz at 55 m/s. v9.1-v9.11 outside: bit-identical (wind was onboard only, k_vib never outside). v9.1-v9.10 inside at standstill: bit-identical (Wind9 ~U^3 is 0 at rest).

Outside unchanged.
