## v1.10.3 (tag `v1.10.3`), 2026-11-12
* Released 2026-11-12 (Europe/Brussels) on https://wimbosmans1.github.io/streetrod/ so Wim can hear v9.11 online (Wim t130u: "Great. Lets give it a try online. Update website pls"). dist/ and deploy/ rebuilt for release. v8 stays the BRM sound default; no 2D art, controls or engine-bay model changed; v8 and v9.1-v9.10 audio is bit-identical.
* **BRM sound v9.11 in the picker**. Port of engine-sound `v9.11-eval` (7729146, `brm9.py` + `fitted_params_v9_11.json`, candidate B = the file's main params; candidate A is not shipped). Physical reversion: on a shut throttle the stub gas that flows back into a cylinder brings its enthalpy, so the cylinder pressure rises (`dp = gamma R Ts dm_in / V`, `rev_mix = 1`, a fixed physical law, not a fit parameter); before, the cylinder was a bottomless sink and the stub flow flipped in a step that rang the stub modes at ~1.0 / 1.4 kHz. Those overrun pipe tones are mostly gone and the overrun is ~3 dB closer to Mason (still ~5 dB too loud on average, 11 dB on the lift from 9.4k, O6). New onboard-only source `k_vib` (0.30): load-following combustion excitation (charge pressure x rpm, 8-per-rev modulation, 650 Hz-6 kHz band) picked up by the onboard camera mic through the chassis; its level is fitted, the structure-borne path is a modelling assumption (the airborne form `k_comb` wrecked the fly-by and stays off). Joint refit (B): cockpit wind 0.189 -> 0.10, wind_fc 514 -> 439 Hz; mech 0.0103 -> 0.0503; whine 0.638 -> 0.538, whine_res 0.5 -> 0.35, whine_ov 4.0 -> 2.5; onb_lp 3500 -> 4400 Hz; comb_lo 650 Hz; ov_map 0.051. Mason-only harness N J 0.585 (v9.10 0.631), onboard 0.748 5/5, fly 0.947 3/9, seg 14 1.055 (v9.10 0.807): best v9.x but does NOT beat v8 (onboard 0.649): not the default, engine-sound FIT_FILE stays v9.6. Misfire off, fuel 80:10:10 as before.
* `js/engine-brm9.js`: kernel back-flow `p2 = p + REVM * g * R * Ts * (-dm) / V` before the isentrope reset (`REVM` from q, default 0 = v9.10 exactly); post chain: `chs = load * BOOST / PRMAX` per sample, 1st-order 200 Hz low-pass, `vv = k_vib * 300 * max(lc, 0)^comb_x * rpm/redline * (1 + mech_mod cos(8 x crank phase)) * bandpass[comb_lo, 6000](noise)`, added to both onboard channels after the mic chain and the wind (as brm9._render), never outside; own noise generator (rng3), so the older sets' mech / wind noise draws are untouched. New set `v9_11`. Sets without `REVM` / `k_vib` use the kernel defaults (0 / off), so v8 and v9.1-v9.10 node renders are bit-identical to v1.10.2 (raw EngineVoice output, both views). `engine-worklet.js` regenerated.
* `tools/export_brm9_params.py`: fit id 9_11; exports `REVM` only for sets from v9.11 on; the new phys keys (k_vib, comb_lo, comb_x, k_comb, rev_mix) come with the merged PHYS. Re-exporting v9.10 on the v9.11 kernel gives the shipped v9.10 set exactly (plus the new keys at their off defaults).
* JS vs Python (`test/ref_render_v9.py ... 9_11` + `test/node_compare_v9.js`; det now also turns k_vib off, its numpy RandomState noise is not reproduced): deterministic kernel outside 184-250 dB SNR (idle, WOT, sweep, part load, overrun), onboard det 42-50 dB; full model spectral distance 0.28-1.76 dB, long-term spectrum within 0.03-0.2 dB on average (max 1.6 dB, part load onboard). v9.10 re-checked on the new kernel: unchanged (238-250 dB det).
* Level: `test/level_match_v9.js v9_11` mean v9.6 - model rear +1.08 dB / inside +1.22 dB vs v9.10's +0.23 / +0.57 dB (0.85 / 0.65 dB apart, inside the 1 dB tolerance). The difference is the near-idle shut-throttle point (3800 rpm / 0.08: -4.2 dB, the reversion change itself); on throttle within 0.5 dB, inside part load +1.5 dB (k_vib). gainDb = v9.10's (rear -24.0, inside -36.3), so v9.10 <-> v9.11 is level-matched and the A/B is the physics.
* Picker: v8 (default) -> v9.1 ... v9.10 -> v9.11 -> v8 (select, key B), `?sound=v9.11` / `v9_11` (a v9.x value alone opens the BRM); `?sound=v9` still means v9.6. Flash text "BRM sound: v9.11 physics model (reversion: overrun pipe tones gone)". Engine bay with the v8 sound shows the latest fit, now v9.11 (identical bay parameters to v9.9 / v9.10).
* Tests: `test/brm_v911_identity.mjs` (every older model bit-identical to a reference git rev or js dir; v9.11 differs from v9.10: test-ramp overrun 0.9-1.6 kHz band -8.3 dB outside / -6.7 dB inside; k_vib inside +2.1 dB at 2-6 kHz on throttle, +0.1 dB on the lift, no effect outside), `test/brm_sound_v1103_test.mjs` (the v1.10.2 picker test with the 10-model list and v9.11 URL / key B / offline worklet checks).

## v1.10.2 (tag `v1.10.2`), 2026-11-12
* Released 2026-11-12 (Europe/Brussels) on https://wimbosmans1.github.io/streetrod/ so Wim can hear v9.10 on his Tesla / phone (Wim t113u: "Yes"). dist/ and deploy/ rebuilt for release; the v1.10.1 desktop pedals stay. v8 stays the BRM sound default; no 2D art, controls or engine-bay model changed; v8 and v9.1-v9.9 audio is bit-identical.
* **BRM sound v9.10 in the picker** (Wim t113u: "Yes" to hearing the v9.10 overrun compressor whine, v8 still the default). Port of engine-sound `v9.10-eval` (6d8f78a, `brm9.py` + `fitted_params_v9_10.json`): the blower sound is split into shaft-speed tones and flow noise. The impellers are geared 4.15:1 to the crank and the SU throttles sit ahead of the blower, so on a shut throttle the 8.30x-crank siren keeps its tip-speed level (`whine_res`) and rises below the surge flow (`whine_ov`, `surge_q`), while the air rush still follows the through-flow (`rush_res`). Best v9.x (onboard 0.795 5/5, overrun siren error 1.49 dB vs 4.30 for v9.9) but does NOT beat v8 (0.649 2/5): not the default, engine-sound FIT_FILE stays v9.6.
* `js/engine-brm9.js`: blower block `wsh = WRES + (1-WRES)*flow`, `bsh = BRES + (1-BRES)*flow`, `rfl = RRES + (1-RRES)*load`, `srg = 1 + WOV*max(0, 1-load/SURGQ)`, tone x `wsh*srg` / `bsh*srg`, rush x `rfl`; new set `v9_10` (q.WRES / BRES / RRES / WOV / SURGQ = 0.5 / 0 / 0.25 / 4 / 0.2; whine 0.638, whine_w 0.18). Sets without the new q entries use the kernel defaults 0 / 0 / 0.25 / 0 / 0.25 = v9.9 exactly, so v8 and v9.1-v9.9 node renders are bit-identical to v1.10.1 (raw EngineVoice output, both views). `engine-worklet.js` regenerated.
* `tools/export_brm9_params.py`: fit id 9_10; exports the five new Q constants only for sets from v9.10 on.
* JS vs Python (`test/ref_render_v9.py ... 9_10` + `test/node_compare_v9.js`): deterministic kernel outside 238-250 dB SNR (idle, WOT, sweep, part load, overrun), onboard det 41-50 dB and full-model spectral distance 0.04-1.27 dB, the same as the v9.9 port on the same clips.
* Level: `test/level_match_v9.js v9_10` gives the same difference to v9.6 as v9.9 at every condition (within 0.1 dB), so gainDb = v9.9's (rear -24.0, inside -36.3): switching v9.9 <-> v9.10 is level-neutral. On the test ramp's overrun at 9800 rpm the 8.30x line is +10.7 dB (outside) / +8.5 dB (inside) over v9.9, +0.4 to +0.7 dB on throttle, total level +0.05 / +0.2 dB.
* Picker: v8 (default) -> v9.1 ... v9.9 -> v9.10 -> v8 (select, key B), `?sound=v9.10` / `v9_10`; `?sound=v9` still means v9.6. Flash text "BRM sound: v9.10 physics model (overrun pops + blower whine)". The picker select is 7-9 px wider (longer label), top bar height unchanged. Engine bay with the v8 sound shows the latest fit, now v9.10 (identical bay parameters to v9.9).
* Tests: `test/brm_sound_v1102_test.mjs` (the v1.9.2 picker test with the 9-model list, v9.10 overrun / outside / URL / key B cycle checks), `test/brm_v910_identity.mjs` (every older model bit-identical to a reference git rev or js dir; v9.10 differs from v9.9 with a stronger overrun siren and an unchanged on-throttle line).

## v1.10.1 (tag `v1.10.1`), 2026-11-12
* Released 2026-11-12 (Europe/Brussels) on https://wimbosmans1.github.io/streetrod/ so Wim can test it in his Tesla's in-car browser (localhost is not reachable from the car). dist/ and deploy/ rebuilt for release; no v9.10 in this release. v8 stays the BRM sound default; no sound code, 2D art or engine-bay model changed.
* Wim (priority): "add the control buttons on the desktop web browser version, so I can test it in my Tesla browser as well". The Tesla browser is a large landscape Chromium on a touchscreen that presents itself as a desktop (Linux / X11 UA, fine or no coarse pointer), so `isTouch` is false, `#touch` stayed `display: none`, and there is no keyboard to hold throttle / brake.
* `js/main.js`: new `deskPedals = !isTouch && ?pedals != 0` adds the class `pedals` on `<html>` (not `touch`). Detection of phones / iPads (v1.8.1 / v1.8.2, `maxTouchPoints > 1`, iPadOS as Mac), `?touch` and `?touch=0` unchanged. Not based on the user agent: every desktop gets the buttons. `__game.deskPedals` exposed for tests. The engine bay gets `pedals: true` so its bar sits between the pedals (as on touch landscape).
* `css/style.css`: `.pedals #touch { display: block }`, the same absolute overlay as `.touch` landscape before the phone rewrites (BRAKE bottom left, GAS bottom right, shift up / down stacked next to the gas); desktop sizes: pedals at least 92 px wide (11 % / 13 % of the stage, 50 % of its height), shift buttons 84 x 72 px. The `.touch` full-screen layout, hamburger top bar and aside hiding are not applied; the aside stays, the top bar is the v1.10 desktop top bar (same height measured with and without the pedals, and as v1.10).
* Input: pedals and shifts already used the v1.8.2 pointer handler with a touch / mouse fallback, attached on every layout, so mouse, pen and fingers work with `isTouch` false; drag-to-steer on the road is not gated on `isTouch` either (verified). Multi-touch (gas + brake, gas + shift) works. On the desktop overlay the pedal fills (green / red) with how far it is pressed, so a finger or a click shows it registered (phones / iPads unchanged). Audio unlock on the first tap / Start engine unchanged.
* The painted / drawn scene frames itself in the space between the pedals (the existing v1.5 safe-area logic), so the instruments in the inside view stay clear; the art itself is unchanged.
* `index.html`: aside "Touch" note now "Touch / mouse" (mentions mouse and drag-to-steer).
* Tests: `test/desktop_controls_test.mjs` (new; Chromium + WebKit, desktop 1280 x 800 and 1920 x 1080, plus a Tesla-like Linux Chromium UA at 1500 x 880): desktop detection, pedals / shifts visible on the stage and touch-sized, aside kept, Start engine / gas / brake / shift up / shift down / drag-steer by mouse, CDP touch gas, gas + brake, gas + shift and drag-steer with `isTouch` false, `?ptr=0` fallback (mouse and touch), engine-bay bar clear of the pedals, `?pedals=0`, top bar height = `?pedals=0` = v1.10, `?touch` layout. Screenshots in /workspace/shots/desktop-controls (not committed).

## v1.10 (tag `v1.10`), 2026-11-12
* Released 2026-11-12 (Europe/Brussels) on https://wimbosmans1.github.io/streetrod/ after Wim's go-ahead ("Can you put it life on the website? Before doing that, check the exhaust steps of the BRM phase 16"). v8 stays the BRM sound default; no sound model, default or audio output changed. dist/ and deploy/ rebuilt for release.
* Wim: a true 3D engine bay, parameter-driven, an exact reflection of the sound-model numbers; one unified parametric model for all three cars with the parts a car does not use switched off. New view **Engine bay** next to Inside / Outside for every car (key E, `?view=bay`). The 2D art is unchanged; the sound keeps its listening position (inside / outside) while the bay is open.
* `js/engine-params.js` (new): `EngineParams.forCar(engineKey, soundId)` builds one parameter object per car.
  * BRM: straight from the v9 kernel fit (`engineModuleBrm9`, PHYS / GEOM / MD): bore, stroke, rod, 16 cylinders at 135 deg banks, firing order and angles, cylinder x from the stub geometry (pitch, centre gap, bank offset), exhaust valve lift law `lmax*sin(pi*x)^camx`, stub length (kernel formula, no end correction), stub mouth points, centre gear drive, two-stage blower at ratio 4.15. The v8 sound has no geometry, so the bay shows the v9.9 fit (noted on screen).
  * GT350 / Cadillac: `EngineCarsV4` overlaid with the current sound model's car spec: stroke from displacement, cam from duration@0.050 / LSA (installed straight up, sin^2 law), headers (tubular with collector, or log manifold; `header_len_m`, `pipe_diam_in`, H crossover), 4-barrel carb, fan blades, alternator / generator ratio, starter (ring / pinion teeth, ratio) from the start-up specs. Bore, rod, bore spacing and valve sizes from the shop manuals (sources in the file).
  * Every part carries notes and an `assumed` flag. Assumptions shown on screen: BRM stub bore (d 0.034 m), exhaust cam / intake valve timing (mirrored about overlap TDC), blower vane counts (12 / 12) and impeller diameters, centre gear teeth (36); V8 bore spacing (Cadillac), cam installed straight up. Stubs: see the parallel-stub entry below (two parallel rows, mean fitted length).
* `js/engine-bay.js` (new): three.js r187 (vendored, ES2017), WebGL 2 only. Crank, pins, webs, pistons, rods (pin distance = the kernel's V(theta) law), valves, cams at theta/2 (DOHC or pushrod), heads and liners (translucent), stubs or headers, blower with readable blades and 83:20 gears, carb with throttle plates, fan, alternator / generator, starter with sliding pinion, flywheel ring gear. Repeated parts are instanced; one hemisphere + one directional light. Exhaust glow, overrun pops (afterburn energy) and the v9.9 mouth flame are additive sprites. Parts panel: the parameters, a checkbox per part (per car), the notes.
* Audio sync, no second physics engine: the voice publishes a small state (crank angle, rpm, throttle, boost, blower phase, per-cylinder exhaust energy / afterburn / flame) every 8th block from the AudioWorklet (`{type:'bay'}`, transferred) or directly in the ScriptProcessor path, only while the bay is open. The v9 kernel gives its own crank angle (`BrmSynthV9.prototype.bay`, read-only); the other models integrate the rpm they render at. The page extrapolates to hearing time (`getOutputTimestamp`, else `currentTime - outputLatency - baseLatency`). Sound off: the game rpm drives it. Rendered audio is unchanged (md5 of v9.9 / v8 / v4 / v2 node renders identical to v1.9.2).
* Slow motion: auto (about 1 rev/s), 1/10, 1/50, 1/200, real; scrub slider 0-719 deg; the sound keeps playing at real speed.
* Lite mode (performance only: pixel ratio 1, 30 fps cap, fewer segments, plain gears; valves, cams, vanes and the blower casing stay, see below): Detail auto / full / lite; auto goes lite on weak mobile GPUs, on the ScriptProcessor audio path, when the median frame time is over 28 ms, and with v9 at full rate. `?bq=lite|hq` honoured. No WebGL 2 (old Safari): the button says so and the 2D view stays.
* Layout: the bay bar sits between the pedals on touch; iPhone / iPad top bar no taller than v1.9.2 (view labels shorten to Inside / Outside / Engine bay on touch screens under 1000 px, "Engine" on small landscape phones).
* Measured (headless, SwiftShader / WebKit): BRM full about 30k triangles, lite about 17k, about 60 draw calls, 25-65 fps desktop depending on detail. Not yet measured on a real iPhone.
* Tests: `test/bay_test.mjs` (render, audio crank source, parts per car, crank turns, scrub, lite, back to 2D, layout, no errors; Chromium + WebKit, desktop / iPhone 15 landscape and portrait), `test/bay_fallback_test.mjs` (no WebGL 2, `?audio=sp`, `?bq=lite`, `?bq=hq`), `test/bay_shots.mjs`; `test/brm_sound_v192_test.mjs` and `test/oldsafari_test.mjs` re-run. Screenshots outside the repo.
* **Visible valves and a clearer blower cutaway** (fix on the preview before release). Wim on the preview: "Missing the valves on the brm" and "Centrifugal compressors vanes not visible? Housing not clear".
  * Cause: Detail auto went lite whenever the v9 sound ran at full rate, and lite dropped the valves, cams and impeller blades, so the default BRM + v9.x view had none (status bar "lite 30 fps").
  * Lite is now a performance mode only: pixel ratio 1, 30 fps cap, fewer segments, plain gear discs, coarser header tubes. Valves, cams, impeller vanes and the blower casing are drawn in full and lite; only the Parts panel hides them. Auto still goes lite on weak mobile GPUs, ScriptProcessor audio, median frame > 28 ms and full-rate v9. `?bay=lite` / `?bay=full` as before, `?bay=hq` added as an alias of full; `?bq=lite|hq` (audio rate) unchanged.
  * Valves: head diameters unchanged (the parameters); stems drawn thicker (Ø 0.22 d, the stem is not in the model) with a spring retainer near the top that stands above the translucent head; brighter bronze exhaust / steel intake. Lift still follows the kernel's law, cams turn at half crank speed.
  * Impellers: 12 + 12 backswept centrifugal vanes (counts still the noted assumption; impeller Ø 200 / 170 mm as before), 4.5-5 mm thick, 26 mm tall at the eye tapering to 12 mm at the tip, gold against a dark backplate and hub so they can be counted.
  * Casing: each stage is a volute scroll whose section grows from the tongue to a tangential outlet, cut away in front of the vane-tip mid-plane (front-half cutaway): back half of the scroll in clearer glass (opacity 0.5), back wall (0.32), red cut faces along the spiral and at the tongue, an opaque rim on the scroll outline and outlet flanges. The two stages sit in series on the shared shaft: stage 1 outlet, a glass interstage duct round the far side into the stage 2 eye, and the stage 2 delivery to the plenum in the vee. The casing is static; only the impellers turn.
  * Tests: `test/bay_test.mjs` and `test/bay_fallback_test.mjs` now check that valves (32 BRM / 16 V8), cams, 24 vanes and 2 housings are drawn in auto, lite and full (`?audio=sp`, `?bq=lite`, `?bq=hq`, `?bay=lite`, `?bay=hq`). Sound code untouched.
* **Parallel BRM exhaust stubs** (fix on the preview before release). Wim: "check the exhaust steps of the BRM phase 16, they don't look parallel." Before, each stub was aimed at the kernel's mouth point (y +-0.45 m, z 0.25 m) and swept back so its own fitted length fitted; the per-stub length spread (stub_sig) changed the sweep, so a bank fanned out by several degrees.
  * Now one shared unit direction per bank, the other bank its mirror image: straight out sideways (outboard), swept back 10 deg (the P30 2/02 photos in engine-sound/research/brm_exhaust: straight, evenly spaced, parallel stubs with the open ends facing out and slightly back) and tilted down 6.8 deg, the angle at which the mean mouth height equals the kernel's stub_z = 0.25 m. Direction (three.js x fwd, y up, z side): (-0.172, -0.119, +-0.978). Mouth = port + D x length.
  * Every stub is drawn at the mean fitted length (542 mm for v9.8 / v9.9), so the mouths line up in a clean row; the sound keeps its per-stub lengths (shown in the Parts panel). The kernel's mouth side offset is no longer used for the picture. Bore 34 mm, the sweep and the tilt stay marked as assumptions (on-screen note rewritten).
  * Sound code untouched (no audio file changed). `test/bay_test.mjs`: new check that the 16 stub directions are parallel per bank (max angle < 0.5 deg), mirrored between banks, outboard and equal in length.

## v1.9.2 (tag `v1.9.2`), 2026-11-12
* Wim: "Update the live website ... so it includes all engine models, not just the current v8 versus v9.6 switch ... including v9.9 and any others." The BRM picker now offers every engine-sound model that ports faithfully: **v8** (still the default, never stored; every visit starts on v8), **v9.1, v9.2, v9.3, v9.4, v9.6, v9.8, v9.9** (physics models). The v1.9.1 "v9 physics" was v9.6 and is now labelled v9.6; `?sound=v9` still means v9.6. The GT350 / Cadillac sound (v1–v4), the radio, the visuals and the v1–v8 code are unchanged.
* Picker: `#segBrmSound` is now a compact drop-down (options "Sound v8" ... "Sound v9.9", own caret, the same height as the car buttons) instead of the two-button switch, so it fits iPhone SE / 15 and iPad in both orientations with no overflow and a top bar no taller than v1.9.1 (desktop wraps differently by 4–12 px). Shown only for the BRM. Key **B** steps v8 → v9.1 → ... → v9.9 → v8. `?car=brm&sound=v8|v9.1|v9.2|v9.3|v9.4|v9.6|v9.8|v9.9` (also `v9` = v9.6, and the internal ids `v9_8` etc.); `?sound=` alone selects the BRM for any v9.x. Switching crossfades live (no reload, no restart).
* One kernel, several fitted parameter sets (`js/engine-brm9.js`): the engine-sound `brm9.py` kernel at tag `v9.9-eval` (fc63153), with each set's merged PHYS / GEOM and derived constants exported by `tools/export_brm9_params.py` (numbers only, no audio). The kernel classes are shared by all models (per-model data object) so JavaScriptCore keeps one monomorphic fast path when you switch.
  * **v9.1–v9.4**: the tag snapshots' fits rendered on the current kernel are bit-identical in Python to their own tags (checked Python vs Python), so they are exact replays of those versions.
  * **v9.8**: fuel thermochemistry (80:10:10 methanol / petrol / acetone by volume, lambda 0.8: gas constant, gamma, fired EVO temperature and pressure ratio, stub temperature law) and the VP88 onboard mic (pressure + gradient pattern, aim, tunnel wall images). Loads `fitted_params_v9_8.json`.
  * **v9.9**: v9.8 plus the physical overrun **afterburn** (unburnt charge stored in the stub, lit by the next hot pulse; residual-gas dilution, survival, raised-cosine heat release, part of it at the mouth) and the rich-exhaust **mouth flame** (mass-flow-driven monopole with turbulence), both on, with the RNG draws in the Python order. Loads `fitted_params_v9_9.json`.
  * The Python default fit stays `fitted_params_v9_6.json`.
* Fidelity vs the Python renderer (`test/node_compare_v9.js` + `test/ref_render_v9.py <dir> <secs> <fit>`, 44.1 kHz, idle / WOT 8000 / sweep / overrun, numpy noise off = "det"):
  * v9.6: unchanged, det SNR 240–283 dB (bit-exact as in v1.9.1).
  * v9.1–v9.4: det SNR 224–287 dB, outside and inside.
  * v9.8 / v9.9 outside: det and full (afterburn + flame on) SNR 238–250 dB; full 1/3-octave distance 0.03–0.15 dB.
  * v9.8 / v9.9 inside: the Python VP88 gradient term is a zero-phase 1024-tap FIR, which cannot run causally; the pressure term is exact, the gradient term is a causal biquad fit (f0 15 Hz, Q 0.6, zero 120 rad/s). Det SNR 38–49.5 dB, 1/3-octave distance 0.03–0.07 dB per frame, long-term mean ≤ 0.05 dB, worst band ≤ 0.85 dB. With the numpy noise sources on: ≤ 1.3–1.5 dB per frame, ≤ 0.2 dB long-term (v9.6: 0.7–1.1).
  * Offline AudioWorklet render = node render for every model and view in Chromium and WebKit (SNR 200–383 dB).
* Not offered, and why: v9.0 (an older kernel before the real firing order; its geometry was replaced in v9.1), the v9.1 4-2-1 collector variant (not ported and not the reference car's exhaust), v9.5 (no refit, the same numbers as v9.4), v9.7 (an evaluation harness, not a fit), `far_sigma` (only used by the Python "distant" perspective, not one of the game views), and the GT350 / Cadillac v1–v7 table models (other cars).
* Level: each model is loudness-matched to v9.6 after the limiter (`test/level_match_v9.js <id>`), within 1 dB (gain_db outside -24.0 to -25.4, inside -35.9 to -39.0).
* Speed (`test/perf_v9.mjs`, 48 kHz, one desktop core): Chromium v9.6 12–13 %, v9.8 12–13 %, v9.9 13–15 %, v9.9 lite about 7 % of real time; WebKit is noisy in this headless test, the same 14–31 % for v9.6 on v1.9.1 and v1.9.2. The ScriptProcessor fallback (half rate), the automatic lite step and `?bq=hq|lite` work for every v9.x.
* Tests: `test/brm_sound_v192_test.mjs` (worklet = node for every model, picker, select, key B cycle, no persistence, URL names, bq=lite, AudioWorklet + ScriptProcessor, desktop / iPad / iPhone, layout vs v1.9.1; Chromium + WebKit; `ONLY=layout` for the layout part), `test/node_compare_v9.js` and `test/level_match_v9.js` take the model from the reference set.

## v1.9.1 (tag `v1.9.1`), 2026-11-12
* Wim: "Can add both of them so i can compare." Then, on the preview: "Yes, put it live." A comparison switch only: v8 stays the shipped default sound of the BRM. The BRM gets its own engine-sound switch, **Sound v8 | v9 physics**, next to the GT350 / Cadillac / BRM buttons (shown only when the BRM is selected; key B toggles it). Switching crossfades live (0.25 s), no reload, no engine restart.
* **v9 physics** = a real-time browser port of engine-sound v9.6 (`brm9.py` + `fitted_params_v9_6.json`), new `js/engine-brm9.js`: the time-domain kernel line by line (crank with torque-impulse speed ripple, firing order 1-10-6-13-2-16-5-11-8-15-3-12-7-9-4-14 at even 45°, weak cylinder / COV / misfire, 16 isentropic blow-down cylinders with choked / subsonic valve flow, 16 stub waveguides with hot-gas speed of sound, open-end radiation and finite-amplitude steepening, two-stage blower at 4.15× crank with its 2-per-rev tone at 8.30×, blade pass and rush), then Lighthill jet noise, valve-train noise, the Spatializer (per-stub delays / gains, 1/r, ground image, air absorption, far-bank diffraction) and the fitted recording chain. Same xorshift64 random generator and seed as the Python kernel. Listening positions: Outside (rear) = the Python `rear` perspective, Inside = the fitted onboard mic (with the v9 cockpit wind).
* Fidelity vs the Python renderer (`test/node_compare_v9.js`, 44.1 kHz, same rpm / throttle inputs): the kernel and chain match sample for sample (SNR 240–283 dB, max error ~1e-11 of peak) with the two noise sources that use numpy's generator (valve-train noise, onboard wind) switched off; with them on, the 1/3-octave spectra differ by 0.02–1.1 dB per 50 ms frame and ≤ 0.4 dB long-term. Against the shipped Python clips `out/v9_6/brm_idle_rear.mp3` / `brm_idle_onboard.mp3` the waveform matches at 26 / 23 dB SNR (the residual is the 192 kb/s mp3 coding and the valve-train noise). The offline AudioWorklet render equals the node render in Chromium (SNR 231 dB) and WebKit (200 dB).
* Resolution: full rate in the AudioWorklet (about 12 % of real time on one desktop core in Chromium, 22–25 % in WebKit; v8 is 3–4 %, `test/perf_v9.mjs`); half rate with ×2 interpolation in the ScriptProcessor fallback (old iPads, Safari before 14.5) and automatically in the worklet if the model needs more than ~45 % of real time (within ~2.5 dB of full rate up to 5 kHz, softer above 6 kHz). `?bq=hq|lite` forces it.
* Default unchanged: the BRM starts on v8 on every visit (the choice is not stored); `?car=brm&sound=v9` (or `?sound=v9`) opens straight into v9. GT350 and Cadillac sounds, visuals and the start-up behaviour are unchanged. Still ES2017, no new CSS features.
* Level: v9 is loudness-matched to v8 after the limiter (`test/level_match_v9.js`: gain_db rear -25, inside -39; on throttle v9 sits about 1.5 dB (outside) to 3 dB (inside) under v8, because its sharper pulses reach the limiter first).
* Tests: `test/node_compare_v9.js` (+ `test/ref_render_v9.py`, `test/compare_v9_6_clips.js`), `test/brm_sound_test.mjs` (switch, live change, URL, no persistence, both audio paths, other cars; Chromium + WebKit, desktop + iPad), `test/perf_v9.mjs`; `test/ipad_test.mjs`, `test/oldsafari_test.mjs` and `test/browser_test.mjs` extended to play v8 and v9 on the BRM (Chromium + WebKit, AudioWorklet and ScriptProcessor). `tools/build-single.mjs` gains a preview mode (`STREETROD_PREVIEW=<dir>`) that leaves dist/ and deploy/ alone.

## v1.9 (tag `v1.9`), 2026-11-11
* Wim: "Ok to launch with v8." The 1953 BRM P30 Mk II V16 is in the live game, next to the GT350 and the Cadillac.
* Visuals: polished photo-derived British Racing Green cockpit and rear in v4 (flat) and v5 (ink) only. v1 (drawn), v2 (painted) and v3 (3D preview) fall back to the flat cockpit — there is no drawn or 3D BRM. Period 5-speed H-gate lever, stalk rear-view mirrors (a mirrored desert crop, not a live tail), and Smiths water / oil plus ribbon-tach sweeps matched to the real faces. Chase view: two compact 2×2 banks of short straight-out exhaust stubs per side, just behind the front axle.
* Sound: real-time browser port of engine-sound tag v8 (`brm8.py`, `cal_v8.json`) as sound model v8, used only for the BRM (the v1–v4 picker is unchanged for the other cars). Additive half-orders 0.5–48, supercharger whine at 8.30× crank, rpm-tracking bandpass body in place of the Python STFT so it stays real-time on older iPads. Runs in the AudioWorklet and in the ScriptProcessor fallback. Numeric tables only; no reference audio is shipped.
* Compatibility: still ES2017, no `inset` or other pre-iOS-14.5 CSS. The GT350 and Cadillac are unchanged.
* History: dated v1.9 entry in the on-page History and here.

## v1.8.2 (tag `v1.8.2`), 2026-11-11
* Wim's real iPad (older model with a home button, portrait Safari; status "scriptProcessor ... AudioWorklet not available", so iOS before 14.5) showed the toolbar and a black picture area with no control deck on live v1.8.1. Root causes:
  * **CSS `inset`** (Safari 14.1 / iOS 14.5+): every full-screen box of the touch layout (`body`, `#app`, `#stage`, `#dash`, `#touch`, `#about`) was positioned with `inset: 0`. Older Safari drops the declaration, so the stage shrank to the toolbar and the canvas and the deck got zero height. Reproduced in WebKit by removing the `inset` declarations from the built page (same picture as Wim's photo). Fix: `top/right/bottom/left` longhands everywhere (CSS and the inline style of the WebGL canvas).
  * **three.js bundle syntax**: the vendored three.js r187 uses class static blocks and other ES2022 syntax, a SyntaxError before Safari 16.4 (it only disabled v3, but it is an error on every older device). Fix: `js/vendor/three.min.js` lowered with esbuild `--target=es2017,safari11`; v3 is only offered with WebGL 2 (three r163+ needs it; Safari 15+), otherwise it is left out of the Visuals list.
  * Other gaps found and covered: `aspect-ratio` (Safari 15) for the desktop stage -> height set from the width in JS; flex `gap` (Safari 14.1) -> margins via a feature test (`html.nogap`); `clamp()` (Safari 13.1) -> `max(a, min(b, c))`; Pointer Events (iOS 13) -> touch / mouse fallback for the pedals (several fingers, by touch identifier), the buttons (shift on press) and steering; ResizeObserver (Safari 13.1) was already optional (resize / orientationchange listeners).
  * Sound: without AudioWorklet (Safari < 14.1) the ScriptProcessor fallback with `webkitAudioContext` runs all sound models; v3 / v4 start-up and hand-over verified on that path.
* **On-screen error report**: an inline script in `<head>` shows any uncaught error, failed script / asset load or unhandled promise rejection (and the first error thrown by the frame loop) in a red box at the bottom with the browser's user agent, so a real-device failure reports itself (tap x to close).
* Checks: `test/syntax_check.mjs` (acorn, every inline script and the worklet parse as ES2017); `test/oldsafari_test.mjs` (the built page with inset / aspect-ratio / gap / clamp / dvh stripped and AudioWorklet, unprefixed AudioContext, ResizeObserver, PointerEvent, WebGL 2, OffscreenCanvas, createImageBitmap, structuredClone, Array#at and canvas roundRect / getTransform / filter deleted; iPad 9th gen portrait + landscape and iPhone 8, Chromium + WebKit).
* Minimum now: iOS / iPadOS 12 (Safari 12). v3 3D needs iOS 15; AudioWorklet is used from iOS 14.5, ScriptProcessor before.

## v1.8.1 (tag `v1.8.1`), 2026-11-11
* Wim: "Can you also make it ipad friendly?"
* Detection (`js/main.js`): iPadOS Safari reports itself as a Mac in desktop mode (its default); a touch device is now `maxTouchPoints > 1` on a Mac/iPad platform, or an iPad UA, or (as before) touch with a coarse pointer (also `any-pointer: coarse` without a fine pointer). An iPad with a trackpad / keyboard stays a touch device. Phones and desktops unchanged (same layout geometry as v1.8 on iPhone 15 / SE portrait + landscape, Pixel 7 and desktop).
* Layout by aspect ratio and size (`css/style.css`), not phone breakpoints:
  * touch landscape narrower than 16:10 (iPad 4:3 / 10:7): the scene is shown full width at its own 16:9 ratio (full-width interior, no mirrored bands above / below) and the pedals and shift buttons move to a control deck below it (at least 150 px): brake bottom-left, gas bottom-right, shift column inboard of the gas, i.e. both thumbs at the bottom corners. Phones (about 19.5:9) keep the overlay layout;
  * touch portrait at least 600 px wide: dashboard crop as before, control deck capped at clamp(220 px, 24 %, 300 px), pedal / shift widths capped;
  * both sides at least 600 px: 40 px+ touch targets, larger labels;
  * `-webkit-text-size-adjust`, `touch-action: manipulation` on the page, no text selection / callout outside the About text, fixed body (no rubber band), safe-area insets as before.
* Orientation change, Split View and Slide Over: resizing repaints at once (no empty frame) and an unchanged size no longer clears the canvas (`UI.Dash.resize` returns whether it changed); a quality step also repaints immediately.
* v3 3D: the WebGL canvas now covers exactly the picture area (it covered the whole stage behind the control deck in portrait, so the 3D camera was framed for the wrong area; also on phones in portrait).
* Adaptive resolution: on big screens it may go below 1 device px per CSS px (0.85 / 0.7 / 0.6) while the canvas keeps at least 350k pixels (about an iPhone's), and steps two levels at once when frames are over 42 ms. Phones stop at 1 as before.
* Tests: `test/ipad_test.mjs` (WebKit + Chromium; iPad 10th gen 820x1180 and iPad Pro 13 1024x1366, portrait + landscape, plus the desktop-mode Mac UA with maxTouchPoints 5), `test/ipad_shots.mjs` (screenshots `screenshots/ipad-*.png`), iPad configs in `test/perf_test.mjs` (`ONLY=ipad`).

## v1.8 (tag `v1.8`), 2026-11-11
* Sound models **v3** (engine-sound v3: start-up / shutdown) and **v4** (engine-sound v4: gated afterfire, on/off-load timbre with an 80 ms blend, off-throttle sound, `fitted_params_v4`, plus the v3 start-up) in the Sound picker as `v3` / `v4`. v1 and v2 unchanged; the default model is unchanged.
  * Ported to the AudioWorklet: `js/engine-v34.js` (CarSound v3/v4 incl. the start-up state machine: off, ignition, cold / warm cranking, catch, idle settle, shutdown run-on), `js/cars-v4.js` (from `tools/export_cars_v4.py`, fitted_params_v4); registered in `js/models.js` (start-up models, listening positions rear / in_cabin). Level matched to v2 (`test/level_match_v34.js`): gainDb v3 GT350 4.9 / 5.8, Cadillac 18.5 / 18.2; v4 GT350 10.1 / 3.9, Cadillac 3.0 / 1.2 (rear / inside).
  * Game link: with v3 / v4 the audio drives the start-up: the physics waits for the catch reported by the worklet (6 s timeout, 2.5 s fallback when no report arrives), the tach follows the audio rpm until hand-over, the idle follows the audio's idle; a warm start after the engine has run 10 s.
* Validation against the Python engine-sound reference (idle, rev blips, 1st-3rd pull, lift-off / overrun, cold start, warm start; both cars, rear and inside; `test/node_compare_v34.js`, `test/v18_web_test.mjs`):
  * JS engine (Node, 64-bit) vs Python: SNR 243-252 dB in all 48 cases (bit-identical apart from rounding), RMS envelope difference 0.000 dB, spectral feature distance 0.000 dB; catch time identical (GT350 cold 2.7225 s, warm 2.2814 s).
  * Web build (Chromium AudioWorklet, 32-bit output) vs Python: v3 all scenarios and v4 idle / start-up 152 dB SNR (float32 limit), envelope 0.000 dB; v4 rev blips / pull / overrun 25-59 dB SNR with RMS envelope difference at most 0.037 dB (mean 0.004 dB) and spectral feature distance at most 0.16 dB. The residual comes from the 128-sample worklet block vs 256 in the Python control loop for the v4 extras (a JS render at 128 matches the worklet at 152 dB); not audible.
* CPU on iPhone (Playwright WebKit, iPhone 15): v2 3.9 % of the audio budget, v3 4.8 %, v4 9-18 % (worst: GT350 inside, cabin IR); 5.6-25x real time.
* Demo: `out/v18_web_v4_demo.mp3` (v4 rendered by the web worklet: GT350 cold start, blips, pull, overrun, inside pull; Cadillac warm start, blips, pull, inside overrun).

## v1.7.1 (tag `v1.7.1`), 2026-11-11
* Wim: "V2/4/5 are all very slow." Performance fix for the painted renderer (`js/visuals-painted.js`) and an adaptive resolution (`js/main.js`). Profiled with Chrome CPU profiles and frame-interval runs (Playwright, desktop DPR 2, iPhone 15 landscape in Chromium with CDP CPU throttle 4x / 6x, and WebKit iPhone emulation); per-feature ablation via the `window.__pvOff` debug flags (`test/perf_ablate.mjs`).
* Hot spots and fixes:
  * the ground was about 160 pattern-filled perspective bands per frame -> simplified roadside (approved by Wim: "Can make that part simpler so it can be digested?"): flat sand from the style's palette with darker cross stripes every 8 m (alpha fading with distance, out to 220 m) streaming past at true speed; the side window shows vertical stripes streaming backwards; the v1.7 textured ground is kept behind the debug flag `texGround`;
  * roadside props: one bush per 10 m segment on alternating sides (5.8-20 m out), a post every 50 m, no fence; culled below the dash;
  * sky + painted far terrain + 8 fade bands (3 large drawImages each) -> one cached far layer per horizon height, shifted with the heading;
  * inside views drew the whole scene under the opaque dashboard -> the scenery is clipped at the lowest see-through cockpit row;
  * cockpit (2560x1440) and wheel (~1200 px) bitmaps were minified every frame -> resampled once to canvas scale and blitted 1:1; wheel + wheel shadow kept as one composite while the steering is still;
  * mirror band copy refreshed every 4th frame; v5 ink outline (16 offset blits) pre-composited; v2 dust gradients -> one puff sprite;
  * `shadowBlur` on the v2 needles and the Caddy lever -> pre-blurred shadow sprites;
  * adaptive quality: canvas device-pixel ratio steps 2 -> 1.5 -> 1.25 -> 1 when the median frame time over ~1.5 s stays above 21 ms (ignores a 30 Hz display cap), tries one step up after 15 s below 17.8 ms, and does not retry a step that failed in the last 6 s; `?q=<dpr>` pins it.
* Frame rate (mean of the frame interval, 90 km/h, v2/v4/v5 x GT350/Cadillac x inside/outside; `test/perf_test.mjs`):

  | device | v1.7 | v1.7.1 |
  |---|---|---|
  | desktop 1440x900 DPR 2 | v2 8-28, v4 20-31, v5 15-18 fps | 58-60 fps (p50 16.7 ms) |
  | iPhone 15 Chromium, CPU 4x | 4-7.6 fps | 43-60 fps (p50 16.7 ms in all 12 cases) |
  | iPhone 15 Chromium, CPU 6x | 2.7-4.7 fps | 24-54 fps |
  | iPhone 15 WebKit | 31-53 fps | 54-62 fps |

  (The 4x / 6x after-numbers were measured while the shared test machine was fully loaded by other jobs; worst cases are v2 GT350 outside and v5 Cadillac outside.)
* Look: v2 / v4 / v5 cockpits, chase car, sky and mesas unchanged; only the ground beside the road and the props are simpler. Screenshots `screenshots/v171-*.png`.

## v1.7 (tag `v1.7`), 2026-11-11
* Wim: "V2 feels right on the interior, v4 and v5 look off." v4 flat and v5 ink interiors now use v2's composition: the style-A / style-D restylings of the v2 cockpit (pixel-aligned with v2, from proposals/visuals-v3) with v2's interior meta (hub, wheel size, gauges, levers, mirror, side window) and v2's portrait / landscape framing. The v1.6 93-degree camera layout is no longer used by any style (its code path stays for reference). v2 painted interior unchanged. Built by `proposals/visuals-v5/build-v17.py`; backdrop, props and chase sprites stay the v1.6 sets.
* Wim: "the picture next to the road is not moving for v2/v4/v5". Cause: the land beside the road was the static painted backdrop (only the dashes and a few sprites moved). Now (`groundBands` / `sideGround` in `js/visuals-painted.js`):
  * the ground is a top-down 16 m tile (sand patches, scrub tufts, rocks; colour sampled from the style's backdrop) mapped onto the ground plane in thin horizontal bands and scrolled by the distance driven, so it streams past at true speed with true perspective (slow near the horizon, fast near the edges); a pre-filtered copy is used beyond 30 m;
  * the painted backdrop terrain is only kept for the far ground (beyond about 90 m, fading out), distant mesas stay (nearly) still;
  * roadside sprites denser: a bush or two every 5 m on alternating sides 5.5-30 m out, a fence 8.5 m right of the centre line, delineators every 45 m;
  * side window: distant mesas drift slowly, the ground below the horizon streams backwards at (y - horizon) / eye height px per metre;
  * the chase view uses the same moving ground.
* Wim: "In exterior view the car is not blending so well with the environment ... It doesn't look part of the picture." Diagnosis: (1) the car sprites are evenly front-lit studio renders while every backdrop has a low sun ahead-right (backlit scene); (2) the only shadow was a thin 9 %-high ellipse, so the tyres looked pasted on; (3) on desktop the speed panel covered the tyres and the ground under the car; (4) the sprite was 3-5 % too wide (scaled at the bumper distance without the camera pitch); (5) keyed edges left a light fringe; (6) in v5 the car's ink lines were scaled down to under 1 px, much thinner than the scenery's. Fixes (`gradedRear`, `carShadows` and the dust in `drawOutside`):
  * lighting graded once per car and style: rear face in shade (multiply with a cool-neutral shade), warm rim light on the upper-right (sun-side) edges, warm bounce from the road along the bottom (v2 gradient; one hard band in v4/v5 to keep their flat levels), a touch of the horizon haze;
  * shadows on the road plane of the chase camera: dark contact strip under each rear tyre (real track 1.47 m / 1.55 m), ambient occlusion over the footprint (soft in v2, hard two-level in v4/v5), a shadow falling 1.8 m towards the camera and to the left (sun ahead-right), fading out;
  * width from the pitched camera (rear face at mid height), 1.73 m GT350 / 2.03 m Cadillac in the 3.6 m lane;
  * alpha eroded by 1 px (no fringe); v5 gets a 1.6 px ink outline from the silhouette, the scenery's line weight;
  * pale sand dust thrown up behind the tyres above 25 km/h (soft in v2, flat / inked puffs in v4 / v5);
  * landscape chase framing moved so the car sits above the speed panel (focus box [360,150,920,790]; the road continues below the painting).
  * Before / after: `screenshots/v17-blend-<v2|v4|v5>.png`.
* Engine bay view removed (Wim): the 'Engine bay' button, the B key and its help line, the engine-bay listening position of sound model v2 (the worklet now only gets rear / in_cabin), the 'Mic: engine bay' tag and the view-2 code paths in main.js, ui.js, visuals-painted.js and radio.js. The painted engine bay is no longer planned.
* Motion proof: `test/v17_motion.mjs` (two frames ~120 ms apart at speed vs standing still, mean pixel change in the terrain beside the road, the side window, and beside the chase car).

## v1.6 (tag `v1.6`), 2026-11-11
* New Visuals options (Wim picked styles A and D from proposals/visuals-v3): **v4 – flat** (refined flat/vector) and **v5 – ink** (ligne claire, black ink outlines, poster palette). Ids v4/v5 because v3 is the 3D preview. v1 drawn stays the default; v2 painted and v3 3D unchanged. One renderer (`js/visuals-painted.js`, now a factory `PaintedVisuals.make`) drives v2, v4 and v5 with per-style assets (`assets/flat`, `assets/ink`, `js/flat-assets.js`, `js/ink-assets.js`) and themed live parts (needles, lamps, HUD, road). The page controls follow the style (`html.vis-flat` / `html.vis-ink`: slate + amber sans-serif, or cream panels with 3 px ink outlines and hard shadows).
* Proportions: new driver camera from real dimensions (`proposals/visuals-v4/camera/camera6.py`), eye point 37 cm (GT350) / 42 cm (Cadillac) left of the car centre line, eye 107 / 112 cm above the road, 93 degree horizontal view (f 600 on the 1280 painting) so the cabin fits pillar to pillar. The cockpit art was redrawn from layout guides projected with this camera; proofs in `proposals/visuals-v4/camera/proof-*.png` (magenta = parts with published sizes, cyan = cabin and hood lines, yellow = driver axis and horizon).
  * Off in v1.5: the 65 degree camera squeezed the passenger side (right A-pillar at about 34 degrees instead of about 50, radio and shifter too close to the driver) -> wide camera, right side at true angles; the GT350 right A-pillar is drawn from the camera.
  * Off: wheel 8-10 % too big against the gauges, gauges too small (3.2 in instead of the real 5 in Mustang bezels) -> 1967 GT350 15.75 in (40 cm) wood wheel, gauges at true size and spacing (live, in a housing over the painted pods, which were 13 % too big and too far apart); Cadillac 17.5 in wheel.
  * Off: the Cadillac ribbon window is wider than the real 14 in speedometer -> the live scale uses the true 14 in span.
  * Portrait shows the driver side (wheel, gauges, radio, a strip of road); the shifter does not fit at true geometry there, the HUD shows the gear.
* Road inside the car, all visuals (shared model): the painted visuals drew the road for an eye height of about 2.3 m (a chase-camera value), ignored the camera pitch, had no driver offset and no heading -> exact pitched ground projection at the real eye height, car centred in the right 3.6 m lane with the driver left of its centre, vanishing point straight ahead of the driver, 3.6 m asphalt with 12 cm edge lines and shoulders, centre dashes 3 m long with 9 m gaps at true speed, delineator posts 1 m tall every 45 m, the road heading swings with steering (and straightens at the lane limits). The painted backdrop ground is sheared with the lane offset so it stays under the live road.
* v1 drawn: the road was centred on the camera (car straddling the centre line) with its vanishing point 60-70 px right of the wheel and a very wide lens (the road began 1.6 m ahead) -> vanishing point straight ahead of the wheel, right lane, two 3.6 m lanes with edge lines, road starting 8-10 m ahead past the hood, posts 1 m and telegraph poles 8 m tall; the chase view now shows the car in the right lane.
* Tests: `test/v16_test.mjs` (desktop, iPhone 15 landscape and portrait; both cars; inside idle / driving with steering / braking, outside driving / braking; any list of visuals).

## v1.5 (tag `v1.5`), 2026-11-11
* Visuals v2 (painted), Wim: "wheel and dashboard are not in proportion". Driver camera computed from real dimensions (proposals/visuals-v2/r5/camera.py): 65 degree horizontal view (f = 1005 px on the 1280 painting), eye 74 cm behind the rim and 30 cm above the hub, wheel tilted 25 degrees. GT350: 15 in wheel -> rim radius 259 px (was 300), gauge pods 3.75 in at 87 cm -> r 55 px (matches the painted bezels). Cadillac: 17.5 in wheel -> 302 px (was 330), 14 in ribbon speedometer at 81 cm -> 441 px (matches). The horizon follows from the same camera (GT350 y 97, Cadillac y 148; was 196 / 186).
* Bug (Wim): the road had disappeared from the windshield. Cause: the horizon (vanishing point) sat too low, at the hood line, so the hood hid the whole painted road; the framing then cropped the rest. The camera-derived horizon puts the road back above the hood, with live centre dashes and roadside posts and bushes streaming past.
* Separate portrait / landscape / desktop layouts. Phones in portrait: top bar, then the scene, then pedals and shift buttons below it (no more "rotate your phone" prompt). Phones in landscape: compact top bar (the extra controls are behind a menu button), shift buttons above the gas pedal. The painted views fit a focus box per car, view and orientation into the part of the canvas that no control covers (measured live from the page, so orientation changes reframe immediately).
* New painted rear chase view for both cars (GT350 Wimbledon White with Guardsman Blue stripes, Cadillac pink with fins): cut-out painted car over the painted desert road, live tail and brake lights, exhaust smoke, painted roadside props streaming past, squat under power, dive under braking, body roll and sway when steering, drag-strip lines, compact HUD.
* Gear levers move: GT350 Hurst T-handle animates through the 4-speed H gate (out to neutral, across, into gear, eased throws) about its boot pivot; Cadillac column lever (cut out of the painting, hole repainted) swings about the column through P N D S L R together with the quadrant indicator.

## v1.1 (tag `v1.1`), 2026-11-11
* iPhone: synchronous audio unlock, silent-switch handling, resume after backgrounding, ScriptProcessor fallback, audio status line.
* Touch layout: pedals, shift buttons, tap buttons; Inside car / Outside (rear) toggle; period interiors for both cars; cabin sound model.

## v1 (tag `v1`), 2026-11-11
* JS/AudioWorklet port of the v1 engine synth (bit-exact to Python), vehicle physics (manual with auto-clutch, Hydra-Matic, traction, brakes with lock-up and fade), gauges, odometer/trip, single-file build.
